# Changelog — Sirsi Pantheon
All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.
Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).

**Building in public** — see [docs/BUILD_LOG.md](docs/BUILD_LOG.md) for the full narrative.

---

## [Unreleased]
- **Thread discovery scoped by machine identity, not hostname — the actual clock behind the 1-2 minute thread churn** (claude-home, 2026-07-26, run 20260726-2013). Fixing the two handshake defects above collapsed the fabric to exactly one active record per agent — and **five re-minted 100ms later, one per live `claude` pid**. `ReconcileDiscovery` indexes already-registered sessions with `t.Host == host`, and **a Mac's hostname is not stable**: it follows DHCP/mDNS, so this one host had written records under `Mac.fios-router.home` (40), `MacBook-Pro-2.local` (20, current) and `Mac` (1) — all the same machine. Every record written under a previous hostname was therefore invisible to the index, so a live session read as unregistered and discovery minted it a fresh thread on each pass, forever. The registry already had the right primitive and the right reasoning written down next door: `ReapDeadThreads` scopes by `MachineID`/`SameMachine` explicitly "not hostname", because "a record with a different machine id is provably foreign". **The check is conditional rather than a bare `SameMachine`, deliberately:** machine id wins when the record carries one (27 of this host's 40 stale-hostname records did), but `SameMachine` reads a *missing* id as "mine", and an id-less foreign record must still not shadow a local PID of the same number — using it unconditionally broke `TestReconcileDiscovery_RemoteHostThreadDoesNotShadow`, which is pinning a real invariant. Id-less records keep the hostname comparison, which is the only signal they carry. Both directions are now pinned (`StaleHostnameDoesNotRemint`, `IDLessRemoteRecordStillDoesNotShadow`). Also reordered `defaultPIDState` to ask signal 0 **before** forking `ps`: reconcile now probes every stale record, and the gone case — the common answer, since records go quiet because sessions exited — no longer costs a fork. Semantics unchanged and now pinned across alive/gone/defunct. **Honest test accounting:** `TestAnubisWeigh` fails on this branch AND passes on untouched `origin/main` only by a margin that is closing — it budgets a fixed 60s for a repo scan that took 29s early in this session and 54.6s later under load, and `anubis weigh` reaches none of the changed functions (they are reachable only from `sirsi thread reconcile`/`discover`). That is a fragile test meeting a loaded host, not a regression, but it is red and is being called red rather than papered over. Refs: ADR-024 §2; #322; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Idle is not exit: a live session keeps its thread record — ends the mint churn and the watcher leak it spawned** (claude-home, 2026-07-26, finalwishes-thread report + run 20260726-2013). An agent reported its thread "cycling every 1-2 minutes rather than persisting," re-arming its `/loop` watcher almost every turn. It was not cycling, it was accreting: **6 concurrent `claude-home` records**, two leaked watchers still heartbeating threads **absent from the store** after 2d16h and 1d14h, and `thread prune` climbing 99 → 118 records. **`ReconcileExits` suspended on the clock alone.** `t.IsStale(now, staleAfter)` never consulted the process table, so a desktop session sitting quietly between prompts had its ACTIVE record suspended out from under it; the session's next hook fire found no active record for its anchor pid and minted a fresh one — the same live pid cycling through thread ids indefinitely (observed live: pid 84991 held `thr-3fa31b42…`, then `thr-166081be…`). The reaper next door already had this right: only OS truth (gone/defunct) proves a session ended. Stale-suspension is now gated on `getPIDStateFn()(t.PID) == PIDAlive` for same-machine records, with `PIDUnknown` falling through to the old idle behavior so an unreadable process table cannot pin a record alive forever. `TestReconcileExits_LivePIDNotSuspendedOnIdleAlone` pins both directions — live anchor stays active, dead anchor still suspends. **Scope narrowed after review (codex-pantheon, item 20260726-205304).** This branch also carried a hook change making concurrent sessions *share* one record (`adopt_or_register` adopting any ACTIVE same-agent record, live foreign anchor included). That is reverted, and the review is why: a shared record is still consumed as one session by `CloseThread`/`SuspendThread`/`ReconcileExits`/`ReapDeadThreads`, so the last writer's exit could close or suspend the record out from under live siblings — lifecycle truth racing on hook order; the adoption filter checked only `agent_id`/`status`/pid, not surface or machine, so it could re-register over a same-agent record on another host; and `watcher_armed` matching any argv containing the shared id proves nothing about a live owner, so one reparented orphan would suppress re-arming for every sibling. Machine-level record sharing needs a real aggregate with contributor membership, not a last-writer-wins alias. The discovery fix below is what actually stops the churn, and it stands on its own. **Consequence for the red-main item:** the 2 `TestAdoptOrRegister` failures logged as knowingly-red in the 2026-07-24 entry remain red — router item 20260724-234927 ("main is RED") stays open. The two leaked watchers were reaped by hand (argv read first per A32; both were `bash -c while true` loops reparented to init, not load-bearing). **Second review correction (codex-pantheon, item 20260726-213250):** the `defaultPIDState` reorder — signal 0 before forking `ps`, to stop a fork-per-record now that reconcile probes every stale record — read *any* `syscall.Kill(pid, 0)` error as `PIDGone`, so the "semantics unchanged" claim was wrong. POSIX returns **EPERM when the process exists but belongs to another user**, routine on a host where root-owned launchd services sit beside user sessions, and a false `PIDGone` feeds `ReapDeadThreads` and stale reconciliation directly — the one direction this probe must never fail in. Now branched on the errno: `nil` refines alive-vs-defunct via `ps`, **only `ESRCH` answers gone**, and EPERM or any other indeterminate errno answers `PIDUnknown`, which callers already treat conservatively. Pinned by `TestDefaultPIDState_PermissionDeniedIsNotGone` against pid 1 — always running, never ours — skipping under root or where pid 1 does not answer EPERM. Refs: ADR-022 §4 / ADR-024 §2 / ADR-043; #322; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Menubar panel behaves like a Mac window: no dead strip above every screen, resize reflows instead of zooming, and it stops floating over everything** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, owner report + screenshot). Three defects in one surface, all from the layout being written against beliefs about the window rather than the window. **(1) The gap was a double-counted titlebar.** `RootView` opened every screen with `Color.clear.frame(height: 50 / scale)` "titlebar clearance", justified by a comment describing the host as a `fullSizeContentView` NSPanel — which it is not: the style mask is `[.titled, .closable, .resizable]`, so AppKit *already* insets `contentView` below the titlebar. `NSWindow.contentRect(forFrameRect:)` measures that inset at exactly **32pt**; the spacer stacked a second clearance under the real one, and with Home's 14pt header padding put **~96pt of dead space above every screen, Home and drill-in alike**. Deleted; the real titlebar was always doing the job, and the BackBar it was supposed to protect still clears the traffic lights (verified on a drilled-in screen). **(2) Resizing zoomed instead of reflowing.** The view was designed at a fixed `baseWidth: 360` and then `.scaleEffect(geo.size.width / 360)` — so widening the window magnified every glyph, divider and padding, resampled all of it off the pixel grid, and, because the design height was `geo.size.height / scale`, *shrank* the vertical content space as you widened. No Mac app does this: Finder, Mail and System Settings hold their point sizes and give you more room. Now the content simply fills the real frame (the 55 `maxWidth: .infinity` sites were already fluid); at 820pt the type is unchanged and two more tools fit on screen. This intentionally reverses the 2026-07-10 "resizing resizes the elements" directive — per-window type scaling is not a macOS behaviour, and it is what forced the `/scale` fudge in (1). If deliberate text scaling is still wanted, the Apple-shaped version is a Cmd-+/− view zoom, not a resize side-effect. **(3) It could never be put behind anything.** The panel was a `.nonactivatingPanel` at `.floating` level with `.canJoinAllSpaces` — permanently above every window of every app, on every Space. #224 addressed the owner's earlier "never loses focus and blocks other windows" report by ordering the panel out whenever it resigned key, which traded *always in front* for *vanishes the moment you look away* — neither is window behaviour. Now `.normal` level, ordinary style mask, `.moveToActiveSpace`, and no resign-key handler: click another app and it goes behind (verified — a Finder window draws over it and the panel stays open), click the Eye and it comes forward. The status-item toggle now keys on `isVisible && isKeyWindow`, because a `.normal`-level panel can be open-but-behind and hiding an already-invisible window is not a toggle. Verified live on the installed `~/Applications` build, not the snapshot harness: header flush under the traffic lights, reflow at 361pt and 820pt, Finder over the panel, both toggle directions, and BackBar clearance. Refs: #184/#191/#224; owner report 2026-07-24; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Memory-death check reads AVAILABLE memory, not free — stops calling a healthy 48 GB machine a death spiral** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, self-routed alert 20260724-232056). The launchd liveness watch paged every 15 minutes with `swap 92% / free 0.09 GB / load 8.6 — SPIRAL` on a host macOS itself reported as **85% memory free**. Root cause is the same class as the four probe bugs fixed earlier today — the metric does not mean what the code assumed. On macOS `Pages free` is near-zero *by design* on any long-running machine: the OS parks idle RAM in `Pages inactive` as cache and reclaims it on demand. The live host had free 0.5 GB but **inactive 20.2 GB**, so the `freeGB < 0.5` arm of the spiral ladder fired on a machine with 20.7 GB immediately available. `readVMStatGB` now returns free + inactive (what a process can actually get), and the ladder's second arm reads that. Swap occupancy alone was never the trigger and still is not: swap fills during a transient spike and STAYS full, which is why the ladder requires exhausted swap AND either a paging-driven load storm or genuinely no reclaimable memory. Also collapsed the two hand-duplicated copies of the ladder (`checkMemoryDeathSpiral` for `sirsi diagnose`, `SampleMemoryDeath` for the launchd probe) into one `isDeathSpiral` — the comment already claimed they "mirror" each other, which is a drift invitation (Rule 0). `MemoryDeath.FreeGB` → `AvailableGB`, and every surfaced string says "available" so the number is not misread as free. Verified live: the deployed binary reported `WEDGED … free 0.09 GB … SPIRAL` and routed an item; the fixed binary reports `ok — swap 86% / available 9.22 GB / load 9.4/18` and routes nothing. `TestIsDeathSpiral_InactiveIsAvailable` pins the exact live host state plus the three conditions that MUST still trip (no reclaimable memory, load storm, and swap-with-room staying quiet). Refs: A32/ADR-040; reference_pantheon_health_blind_to_memory_death; #304/#310/#311 (same measure-the-wrong-thing class); Changelog: Unreleased
- **`SIRSI_ROUTER_AGENT` may now only NARROW, never contradict the cwd — closes the cross-agent liveness lie** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, claude-home item 20260724-205100). The override was honored whenever the cwd resolved to `None` *or* `claude-home` — but `claude-home` IS a confident resolution (a session at `$HOME` genuinely is claude-home), so the guard treated a correct answer as overridable. And `SIRSI_ROUTER_AGENT` is an ordinary env var: Claude Code's user-level `settings.json` `env` block exports it into **every** session on the machine, and the hook cannot distinguish "this session declared its identity" from "the machine declared every session's identity". One global `SIRSI_ROUTER_AGENT=claude-deck` therefore relabeled every home-rooted session — including the 15-minute router-conduit scheduled task, verified live registering and heartbeating `thr-f624e7b9248155ab [claude-deck]` — so a session actively asserted **liveness for another agent's thread**, which the doctor, the work board, and the ADR-022 reaper all consume as truth. It also drove mint churn: each mislabeled session has its own LIVE anchor pid, so the machine-level reuse path (which adopts only DEAD-anchor records) correctly declined and minted a fresh record, ~11 heals per conduit run indefinitely. New `apply_identity_override` honors the override only when the named agent's own registered cwd **contains** this session's cwd — a session may disambiguate among agents it could actually be, never claim one its cwd rules out. **Deliberate trade, stated not hidden:** this removes the documented "a home-rooted session can declare it is really claude-pantheon" rescue. That rescue is unrecoverable by design — it is byte-for-byte indistinguishable from the machine-global trap — and it was never needed: a session at `$HOME` *is* claude-home, and one that means to be claude-pantheon belongs in the pantheon repo's cwd, where `resolve_agent` gets it right with no override at all. Five tests pin the invariant, including the exact live hijack. Test accounting is honest: 2 `TestAdoptOrRegister` failures remain, verified red on untouched `origin/main` (25 tests/2 failures) and unchanged here (30 tests/2 failures) — pre-existing, not owned by this change. Refs: ADR-024 refinement, item 210348; claude-home item 20260724-205100; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Cutover readers now fail CLOSED, and the wake pass stops annotating months-closed items** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24; codex-pantheon FAIL review of #315, item 20260724-232529, and claude-home item 20260724-232706). Follow-up to #315, both findings correct and both on merged code. **(1) Fail-open fallback.** `OpenItems`/`AllItems` (and the `inboxUnion` they wrap) kept the pre-cutover `if err != nil { return work.ListInbox(...) }` degrade-to-files path on a store-open failure. Post-cutover that resurrects the frozen legacy rows through pull/wake/plan/board — reproducing the exact P0 #315 fixed, precisely when the store is unavailable — and it contradicted the facade's own read methods, which already fail closed. They now return a wrapped authority error and never touch `internal/work`; the pre-cutover degrade path is untouched, and a test pins that it still works (Rule 14). **(2) The wake pass was a phantom AMPLIFIER, not just a stale reader.** `Facade.SetWake` preferred the file whenever one existed, so `router doctor --fix` recorded wake-unavailable state onto items closed in the store since June — every conduit run re-annotating dead rows. Post-cutover the annotation goes to the store row. (claude-home's stranded-inbox evidence for claude-deck/ask-eliot/codex-nexus was measured at 23:28Z, two minutes before #315 was deployed at 23:30Z; the deployed binary now reports those inboxes clean, so the READ half was already covered — the WRITE half was not, and is fixed here.) **(3) `internal/liveness/main_test.go` used a fixed `/tmp` database** that survives process exit and is shared across repeated and concurrent runs, so an earlier run's synthetic alert silently changed a later run's dedupe outcome — a stateful suite masquerading as a hermetic one. Now `os.MkdirTemp` per run, removed after `m.Run()`. Also added the three-way divergence fixture codex asked for (store-closed/file-open, store-open/file-closed, file-only-open), proving post-cutover reads return exactly the store's ids and statuses, that pre-cutover union semantics are unchanged, and that no read reconciles a file-only row into the store. `TestCutoverReadersFailClosed` was verified to fail on the merged code with the exact symptom ("returned 1 item(s) and no error — it fell back to the frozen legacy files"). Refs: ADR-036/ADR-037; #315; codex item 20260724-232529, claude-home item 20260724-232706; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Store-cutover observers stop resurrecting closed work, and the last file-only writer is retired** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24; owner item 20260724-210246, claude-home item 20260724-230545, codex-pantheon item 20260724-225830). Two defects, one root cause — a dual-source router where only ONE side ever gets updated. **(1) The reads unioned the frozen files with the store, and the file leg outranked the store on `status`.** Post-cutover `close` writes the store alone, so any `items/<id>.md` written before the cutover — or closed from a different git worktree, where `CloseItem` finds no file at its own root — stays frozen at `status: open` forever. `Facade.Inbox`/`ListAll` had a guard for the *other* direction only (skip a store row the file says is closed), so the frozen copy resurrected its own closed item on every read. Measured on this host: **34 items file-open whose store row is `closed`**, `router status` reporting **47 open against the store's 9**, and `router pull claude-home` handing back 2 items closed since 2026-06-18 — `pull` is the wake path, so 44 phantoms across 11 recipients were actively waking agents onto work finished five weeks earlier. Fixed by making the store TOTALLY authoritative once `routercfg.StoreWake()` is live: `Inbox`, `ListAll`, `Show`, and `Get` read the store alone; the dual-read union stays exactly as-is pre-cutover, so a binary shipped with the flag off is unchanged (Rule 14). Safe because it is lossless — measured directly against the live store, **zero** file-open items lack a store row, so the file leg contributes nothing but stale status. The four surfaces still reading `internal/work` directly (`ctr`, `conduit-tick`, `router plan`, the A33 work board) now go through one cutover-aware entry point (`router.OpenItems`/`AllItems`), so no observer can drift back onto the files. **(2) `internal/liveness` was the only remaining direct file writer** (`work.Send` at livenesswatch.go:191) — proven by exhaustive grep, and exactly the "surviving writer keeps refilling the phantom set" claude-home warned about. Because it bypassed the store entirely, its alerts never reached the wake path (consumers block on `router wait`, which reads the store) while still landing in the legacy dir the observers unioned back in; and `hasOpen` read files while the send wrote nothing to the store, so **the dedupe could not see its own previous send** — the mechanism behind the owner's recurring leaked-session alert. Both the send and the dedupe now go through the dispatch facade. New `dispatch.OpenRoot` lets router-root callers skip the `filepath.Dir(filepath.Dir(…))` reconstruction, which is wrong for any root not literally two levels below a repo. Corrected the `router send --help` text, which still advertised the retired `items/` dir. Regression-locked both ways: `TestStoreWakeIgnoresFrozenOpenFile` models the real worktree-split close and reproduces all three symptoms on the old code (phantom in `Inbox`, wrong status in `ListAll` and `Get`); `TestRun_DedupsUnderStoreCutover` fails on the old code with the telling signature — "route sent → claude-pantheon" alongside **0 open store items**. Refs: ADR-036/ADR-037 (store cutover), A31, A33; items 20260724-210246, 20260724-230545, 20260724-225830; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Router-supervisor nudge stops minting a new thread id every emission — the anchor now walks the ancestry to the real `claude` process** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, claude-home item 20260724-202920). `adopt_or_register` keys on the ADR-022/024 durable identity `(agent_id, anchor pid)`, but the ANCHOR was wrong: `claude_session_pid()` hard-coded a 2-hop walk (`claude → shell → python3`) and took the grandparent. Where the chain is deeper — the desktop surface — that grandparent is a TRANSIENT per-prompt process, so the anchor changed on every hook fire, the `(agent_id, pid)` match never hit, and a NEW thread was minted per emission: claude-deck accreted **7 active records** while every other agent held exactly 1. The freshest-active fallback could not save it — it is gated on `anchor is None`, and a wrong-but-PRESENT anchor bypasses that gate entirely (the worst case for that guard). Fixed by walking UP the ancestry (bounded 12 hops, cycle-safe) until the durable `claude` process is found, returning None when there is none so callers reach the fallback rather than adopt a wrong pid. Subtlety caught by test: `"Claude Helper (Renderer)"` CONTAINS "claude" but is the transient child — matching it would have reproduced the bug, so helpers are excluded explicitly. This also satisfies the ancestry-walk spec (`TestClaudeSessionPidWalk`) that already existed on main with no implementation behind it: **5 erroring tests now pass**. Refs: item 20260724-202920; ADR-022 §4 / ADR-024 §2; phantom-accretion finding 20260602-032542; Changelog: Unreleased
- **`TestKaHunt` and `TestCheckDiskAccess` skip in short mode — pre-push hook no longer blocks on live-system integration tests** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24). Both tests probe the live machine (Ka ghost scanner does a full filesystem walk; `CheckDiskAccess` queries the TCC permission database) and take 60+ seconds — exceeding the hook's 60s timeout and blocking all new-branch pushes with `❌ Tests failed in changed packages`. Added `t.Skip` under `testing.Short()` so `go test -short` (what Ma'at uses) skips them while `go test` (no flag) still exercises the live probe for explicit integration runs. No logic changed. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A6/A17; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Release pipeline now auto-bumps `Casks/sirsi-pantheon.rb` on every tagged release — eliminates silent cask drift** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, homebrew-tools item 172647). The goreleaser `brews:` block auto-commits `Formula/sirsi-pantheon.rb` on every release, but the Homebrew Cask (`Casks/sirsi-pantheon.rb`) was never touched — it drifted 5 versions behind (stuck at v0.18.0 while formula was at v0.23.8-beta) until claude-homebrew-tools caught it manually (homebrew-tools PR #2). A new `bump-cask` job in `release.yml` runs after the `menubar` job (which uploads the DMG asset), downloads the DMG, computes its SHA256, and pushes an updated Cask directly to the `homebrew-tools` repo via the GitHub Contents API using the existing `HOMEBREW_TAP_TOKEN`. The DMG SHA256 must be computed at release time — it cannot be known earlier — so a separate post-`menubar` job is the correct seam. Cask version and sha256 are set to the exact release version; the `#{version}` Ruby interpolation in `url` and other fields is left untouched (the heredoc emits them as literal Ruby). Refs: homebrew-tools item 172647; Changelog: Unreleased
- **gemma-triage calibration probe no longer false-DEGRADEs by measuring its own cold load** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, claude-home item 20260724-193113). `sirsi-gemma-triage.sh` aborted a conduit run with `warm server DEGRADED — 1 tok/s < 5 tok/s floor` while the broker was healthy (25 tok/s measured immediately after, no restart). Root cause (same class as the #304/#310 liveness probe): the calibration probe timed its FIRST inference, which pays the full model weight-load from disk (~45s) when the server default isn't already resident — dividing 48 tokens by the load time yields a false ~1 tok/s. The prior 20s post-fail retry (#295) was SHORTER than the load it triggered, so the retry landed mid-load and confirmed the false verdict. Fix: an explicit **warmup-discard** — one throwaway generation with generous headroom (180s) so the model is definitively resident, THEN measure warm decode; weight-load time is never in the rate. The probe body is factored into a `gemma_probe <timeout>` shell function (removes the duplicated heredoc), and the post-warmup transient retry drops 20s→5s (no cold load left to wait out). Verified live: warm probe measures 27 tok/s (48 tok in 1.8s). NOT fixing the separate resolver model-swap churn here (conf drift `qat-mxfp8` vs resident) — the warmup makes the probe robust to it regardless. **Also fixed the SAME bug in `sirsi-gemma-model-resolver.sh`**, where it is more consequential: its empirical-fit probe names the model explicitly, so a non-resident model's ~45s load is timed as decode — and below-floor there does not just log, it STEPS DOWN to a smaller fallback and bounces the server, silently downgrading the fabric's model on one cold reading. Live evidence: the same model measured 8 tok/s cold and 25 tok/s warm 15 minutes apart (`gemma-model-resolver.log`); at 8 it cleared the floor, but the 1 tok/s cold-loads measured elsewhere would have forced a downgrade. Same warmup-discard applied. Refs: #295, #304, #310; item 20260724-193113; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Liveness probe retries once on timeout — a BUSY broker no longer reads as WEDGED** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, recurring self-routed alerts). The gemma broker serializes requests (`mlx_lm.server`), and the triage loop + gemma-worker + conduit keep it generating, so a liveness probe firing mid-request queues behind an in-flight generation and exceeds the 30s timeout on a perfectly HEALTHY broker — firing `gemma broker wedged` every ~15-min tick (proven live: 5 back-to-back probes measured 21.58s, 0.39s, 0.39s, 0.39s, 0.39s — the first queued, the rest instant). `ProbeGemmaState` (shared by the launchd liveness watch AND the router's self-healing duty) now retries ONCE after a short drain on a timed-out attempt: a busy broker answers on retry (healthy), a truly wedged one fails both (wedged). Only the net-timeout case retries — a decisive non-200 or zero-token result is still wedged immediately. Regression-locked in `TestProbeGemmaState_RetryOnTimeout` (busy-then-answers → healthy; always-hangs → wedged; `probeTimeout`/`probeRetryPause` made overridable vars so the test runs in ~1s). Refs: A32/ADR-040; #304 (transport-truth probe); Changelog: Unreleased
- **ADR-048 work-path amended (owner decision): llama.cpp re-homed to Win/Linux, mlx-c cgo spike is the Apple-Silicon play — plus a `.gitattributes` union driver that kills the CHANGELOG merge treadmill** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, owner ruling + claude-home concurrence, item 151054). Owner ruled llama.cpp is not necessary on Apple Silicon (our workload is prefill-dominated — MLX's home turf — so llama.cpp is least likely to clear the ≥90%-mlx-lm adopt bar, and the Python-serving fragility that motivated "delete Python" is already closed on the Mac: bounded cap wrapper, KeepAlive-durable broker #286, self-healing #295). ADR-048 Step 2 is re-homed from an Apple benchmark to the **cross-platform GGUF backend for the first non-Apple Sirsi build**, benchmarked there against that platform's baseline, behind the `sirsi gemma serve` seam; Step 3 (mlx-c cgo embeddings spike) is elevated to the **primary Apple-Silicon de-Python/sovereignty play** (accepted as scoped; implementation scheduled for a dedicated session per owner — the amendment records the decision, not the spike); added a platform backend matrix (Apple→MLX today / mlx-c cgo future, Win-Linux→llama.cpp). Separately, added `.gitattributes` `CHANGELOG.md merge=union` (claude-home's tip, item 152531): every PR prepends to `[Unreleased]`, so each merge re-conflicts its siblings on the same lines — a rebase-per-merge tax the whole stack pays; git's built-in union driver keeps BOTH sides' added lines on conflict (correct for an append-only newest-first changelog), so the treadmill stops without any `git config` setup. Refs: ADR-048; ADR-031/045; #286/#295; items 151054, 152531; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Release pipeline now bumps the Homebrew Cask too — no more silent drift behind the Formula** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, claude-homebrew-tools item 172647, re-raised after auto-triage). goreleaser (the `release` job) bumps `Formula/sirsi-pantheon.rb` on every tagged release but has no view of the DMG (built later, in the `menubar` job), so `Casks/sirsi-pantheon.rb` was never bumped — the cask silently drifted to **v0.18.0 while the Formula reached v0.23.8-beta** (5 versions), caught only by a manual homebrew-tools audit (their PR #2) and guaranteed to re-drift on the next release. Fixed by mirroring the Formula bump for the Cask in the `menubar` job, right after the DMG upload (the DMG is in hand there): compute its sha256, update the tap cask's `version` + `sha256` (the `url` is `#{version}`-templated, so nothing else changes), and push to `SirsiMaster/homebrew-tools`. Idempotent (no-op when already current), fails loud if the cask is missing. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES A13 (release versioning), A28; homebrew-tools item 172647, PR #2; Changelog: Unreleased
- **node-status `stranded_inbox` stops false-stranding wake-loop agents + the owner queue, and the Fabric banner no longer contradicts the list below it** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, owner item 172430 context + claude-home item 160154). Owner surfaced the menubar Router — Fabric view: a green "No blockers — **nothing is stranding work**" banner sitting directly above a "STRANDED INBOXES" list naming claude-pantheon, claude-deck, and user. Two defects, both source-side. **(1) Go (`computeStranded`)**: it credited a watcher only from the thread REGISTRY, so it flagged any agent whose consumer is the launchd `ai.sirsi.router.wake.<agent>` pull-loop — the durable post-cutover consumer that lives in launchd, NOT the registry (the #298 false-positive class; proven live — claude-pantheon was flagged stranded while `router.wake.claude-pantheon` ran as PID 80806). It also stranded `user`, but the owner queue has no session watcher to "arm" — a category error. Fixed: `computeStranded` now unions live `ai.sirsi.router.wake.<agent>` labels (new `liveWakeAgents`, the same domain-correct `launchctl list <label>` probe node-status already uses) into the "watched" set and never strands `user`. Verified LIVE: node-status `stranded_inbox` went `[{user,1}]` → `null` on the fixed binary (real strands still surface — an agent with neither a thread nor a wake job is flagged; unit tests pin both). **(2) macapp (Fabric banner)**: reworded "No blockers — nothing is stranding work" → "No blockers — fabric is healthy", so the Blockers headline (auth/daemons) never collides with the separate stranded-inbox section below it. claude-home shipped an interim board-producer filter (schema 1.2.1) so the owner's menubar was correct this cycle; this makes the raw CLI JSON honest at the source, so that filter becomes harmless belt-and-suspenders. Refs: #298 class; owner item 172430, claude-home item 160154; Changelog: Unreleased
- **CI: each m5 runner gets its own GOMODCACHE + a zero-byte sweep — kills the unlink-race flake AND the fake-SECURITY-ERROR truncation, one preflight step** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, claude-home items 150517 + 172218). Two symptoms, one root cause: the two self-hosted runners on the box (`m5-sirsi`, `m5-sirsi-2`) shared a single machine-global `GOMODCACHE`. Concurrent `go test`/`go build` then raced on read-only module files (`Can't unlink already-existing object: Permission denied` under `modernc.org/{sqlite,libc,cc,ccgo}` — #288/#292 ate a red + a manual re-run each), and a cache truncated mid-write during a memory/swap storm reddened Go CI on EVERY self-hosted repo at once with `checksum mismatch / SECURITY ERROR / an attacker may have intercepted the download` (PR #303, a CHANGELOG-only docs PR — 52 zero-byte artifacts across 49 modules, all stamped 13:14–13:15 during that window's storm; the wording sends every agent down a supply-chain rabbit hole for a corrupt cache). Fixed with ONE preflight step in the `test` and `build` jobs (before `setup-go`, so `go env GOMODCACHE` reports the override): point `GOMODCACHE` at a persistent per-runner path (`$HOME/.cache/sirsi-ci/$RUNNER_NAME/gomodcache`) so the runners never share a module tree — no unlink race, and a truncated cache is isolated to one runner instead of fleet-global — then `find … -size 0 -delete` the download artifacts (`.mod/.zip/.info/.ziphash`), which are always corrupt and always safely re-fetchable (content-addressed + checksum-verified on re-download), turning the scary security error into a self-healed no-op. Chose per-runner decoupling over serialize-the-Test-job (option 3 halves throughput on the 2-runner pool) and over a warm read-only shared cache (still one blast radius). No runner surgery — the step self-provisions each runner's cache on first run. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES A28 (CI gate), ADR-042; reference_gomodcache_truncation_fake_security_error; items 150517, 172218; Changelog: Unreleased
- **liveness-watch routes machine-health blockers to the agent that remediates them, not the owner queue** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, owner standing correction items 144451). The watch hardcoded `user` as the recipient of every routed blocker, so a wedged broker / memory spiral / leaked-session pileup / dead menubar re-alerted the OWNER every 15 min — even though Pantheon now REMEDIATES all four under A32/ADR-040 (broker right-size/restart, caller-protected safe reap, launchd kickstart). New `recipientFor(check)` maps each machine-health Check to `claude-pantheon`; only a genuinely owner-only condition (none today) falls through to `user`, so a novel finding still reaches the owner rather than being silently misrouted. Dedup and the routed Send both use the mapped recipient. This is the root-cause fix the owner asked for ("fix the routing source so recurrence no longer targets user"): the recurring liveness alerts now land in Pantheon's lane, where they get fixed, instead of the owner's. Test updated (worst finding routes to claude-pantheon + dedups there) plus a `recipientFor` unit test pinning the ownership map and the fail-safe-to-owner default. Refs: A32/ADR-040; owner standing correction 2026-07-24; Changelog: Unreleased
- **`sirsi runner install --instance N` — parallel runners to widen a serialized CI queue** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, owner directive "add the second runner"). The M5 ran ONE self-hosted runner per repo, so all of sirsi-pantheon's CI serialized behind it — a deep queue stalled a six-PR stack for over an hour. Adding a second runner needed a manual rsync/config.sh/svc.sh dance because `Install` hard-coded the name `m5-sirsi` and dir `<repo>`, which a second instance on the same repo would collide on. Productized: `InstallInstance(owner, repo, instance, …)` suffixes both the GitHub runner name (`m5-sirsi-N`) and the donor-clone dir (`<repo>-N`) for N>=2, while **instance 1 stays byte-identical** to the prior single-runner path (`Install` now just calls `InstallInstance(…, 1, …)`). New `--instance` flag on `sirsi runner install`. Verified LIVE: the hand-built second pantheon runner (which unblocked the stack — CI throughput doubled, the queue drained, #293 landed) is now reproducible as `sirsi runner install sirsi-pantheon --instance 2`, and `sirsi runner status` reports both (`m5-sirsi: online, working` / `m5-sirsi-2: online, working`). Panic-safety note: the second runner runs Go builds (CPU only) — it does NOT add to the GPU/MLX churn that caused the 2026-07-23 kernel panic, so it is safe on a post-panic host. Tests pin instance naming incl. the instance-1-equals-RunnerName invariant. Refs: ADR-042/044; owner directive 2026-07-24; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Canon P0: the duplicate A32 resolved (census → A33) + wake-substrate text trued to the store cutover** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-23, claude-nexus canon-alignment audit item 160838). Two rules both claimed **A32** — Do No Harm To The Running Host (§2.29, ADR-040) and Universal Thread Census & Work Board Overseer (§2.29, the July 17 census) — so every downstream citation was ambiguous about which law it invoked. Census renumbered to **A33** (§2.30) across all three synced canon copies (PANTHEON_RULES/CLAUDE/GEMINI) and every code citation that means the census rule (`cmd/sirsi/censuscmd.go`, `internal/router/census.go`, `internal/router/supervisorduties.go`); Do-No-Harm keeps A32 (it is the more widely cited of the two, and the newer census rule is the one that collided into an occupied number). Also trued A27's wake mechanism: the loop mandate still told Claude threads to watch `items/` via a file Monitor, a substrate the store cutover retired — now `sirsi router wait <agent>`, which blocks on the durable store until work lands, in all three copies. AUDIT FINDINGS VERIFIED STALE, no action: the audit's P0 claim that A29-A32 and ADR-030-045 live only in uncommitted worktrees (main carries A29-A33 and 49 indexed ADRs, 19 ADR files) and the `enforce_admins` P1 (all three copies already say `true`, matching live branch protection). The §0/§5 identity rewrite stays HELD pending owner ratification, as the audit specifies. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES A26/A27/A32/A33; ADR-040; canon-alignment audit (SirsiNexusApp docs/CANON_ALIGNMENT_AUDIT.md Part II); Changelog: Unreleased
- **Broker respawn bind-race + swap-drain + probe-warmup — the death-#3/#4 defect pair closed** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-23, claude-home forensics items 133747/141424). Two of the day's four broker deaths shared one signature: a respawn bound port 8765 while the dying predecessor still held it → Errno 48 inside mlx_lm.server → a WEDGED ZOMBIE (model loaded 12.9 GB, main thread dead, port unheld) that non-daemon threads kept resident. Fixes at the source: (1) the capped-server wrapper now WAITS FOR THE PORT TO FREE before starting (probe-bind loop, 30s cap, distinct exit 48 for launchd's ThrottleInterval retry — benign TOCTOU: the only competitor is our own dying predecessor, which never re-binds), and every exit path ends in os._exit with a printed traceback — no zombie class survives, and the next silent death is ATTRIBUTABLE in the launchd log (4 today had no trace). Proven in isolation: held-port → exit 48 at deadline; port freed mid-wait → binds and proceeds. (2) the model resolver's swap bounce gained a DRAIN GUARD: established client connections on the broker port (lsof, the in-flight truth — no protocol change) defer the swap to the next idle run, so a batch never again dies at item 15/24 mid-swap. (3) the triage calibration probe retries once after a 20s pause before declaring DEGRADED — the first inference after a model load measures ~1 tok/s (warmup/compile) and was false-failing the 5 tok/s floor right after every heal. Refs: ADR-031/040/045; items 133747, 141424; Changelog: Unreleased
- **CI: `concurrency: cancel-in-progress` on ci.yml — a rebase-heavy PR stack no longer starves the self-hosted runners** (claude-home, 2026-07-24, claude-pantheon item 154839). `.github/workflows/ci.yml` had no concurrency group, so every force-push (rebase/amend) left its OLD run QUEUED; during today's PR-stack drain ~12 superseded runs (dead SHAs) piled ahead of current commits on the 2 m5 runners and nothing merged until they were manually cancelled. Added `concurrency: {group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}, cancel-in-progress: true}` so a branch's prior in-flight run auto-cancels on every new push. Scoped to ci.yml only — android.yml/ios.yml are dormant (`workflow_dispatch`-only, ADR-032 mobile-off) and never auto-run from PRs, so they need no group. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES A28 (CI gate), ADR-042; router item 154839; Changelog: Unreleased
- **`sirsi router dump` — store-aware JSONL export for the hypergraph fabric feeder** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, claude-home fabric-gap item 145240). The ADR-036/037 store cutover stopped writing `items/*.md`, so the hypergraph's file-based router feeder went blind to every store-only item (proven: a G3 test event absent from both `items/` and the graph). New read-only verb streams every router item to stdout as JSONL — one object per line, exactly nine fields (`id, from, to, type, title, status, opened, closed, body`). It reads through the **dispatch facade** (the file+store union), NOT a raw `items/` scan, so it sees store-only items across the cutover boundary — the whole point. H4 boundary held: the field set is a fixed contract via an extracted, unit-tested `dumpRecord` projection that provably drops internal state (wake frontmatter, Result) so the fabric feeder gets a stable dedup surface and no schema leak. Verified LIVE: dumped 1837 items as valid JSONL with the exact 9-field contract, and item 145240 itself appears in the dump while having ZERO `items/*.md` files — the exact store-only blindness, now visible. claude-home's PR7 ingester emits matching payload shapes so content-derived IDs dedup automatically. Refs: ADR-036/037; fabric-gap item 145240; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Registry police credits the REAL watchers — 10 false A27 violations → 2 real, and the script is finally version-controlled** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, police item 004530 root-caused instead of acked). The police alarmed hourly with "N registered-but-not-looping agents" while the fleet was fully armed: it credited an agent as looping ONLY if that agent's own registered thread listed itself in `watches`, so it saw neither of the two mechanisms that actually do the waking today — the `ai.sirsi.router.wake.<agent>` **launchd jobs** (the post-cutover durable loop, which live in launchd and not in the thread registry) nor the **fleet supervisor's** watch list (horus-supervisor watches all 19 agents on their behalf). Proven before fixing: 9 of the 11 flagged agents had their launchd wake job loaded and armed at the moment of the alarm. Detection now unions three sources — any live thread's `watches`, self-watch, and `launchctl list` wake labels — so liveness is keyed on the real mechanism (the same lesson as sweep.sh #284, which was re-keyed off the retired dispatch.sh). Live re-run: **stale-loop 10 → 2, verdict PASS**. Also fixed the reason it could rot unseen: `.agents/idea-router/.gitignore` ignored `police/` wholesale, leaving `registry-police.sh` untracked on ONE machine — the ADR-031-C class (local automation outside the repo, invisible to canon and review) — now narrowed to the log files, with the script version-controlled. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES A27; ADR-031-C; #230, #284; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Local sovereignty P1 — deterministic supervision + the owner-facing run report (`sirsi report` + menubar "Last check")** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-23, owner directive via claude-home items 130309). The fabric now reports what it DID to the OWNER, and keeps itself alive without any LLM. New `internal/report`: the run-report contract (`~/.sirsi/conduit-report.json`, schema 1.0.0, newest-first, 50-run cap, atomic writes, one shared `Sentence()` renderer so surfaces never drift) — written by the resident supervisor each pass and by the cloud conduit (same schema; Fabric-Board-Producer pattern). New supervisor duty `launchd-kickstart` (5-min cadence): every managed `ai.sirsi.*`/`actions.runner.*` plist on disk absent from launchd gets re-bootstrapped — the reboot proved a label that dies at boot stays dead until a CLOUD agent notices, inverting the sovereignty order; deterministic shell-out, no LLM, skips `.bak`/`.quarantined`, never touches loaded labels (A32 do-no-harm), error-isolated per label. Heal collector: duties `RecordHeal()` plain-English lines as they fix things (gemma-liveness restores now recorded); the pass grades green/healed/degraded and stamps an Anthropic-API reachability probe (any HTTP response = reachable; false renders "cloud unreachable, local AI holding the fort" — the sovereignty signal). All wiring is inert-by-default seams (A16, auto-heal pattern): tests never shell launchctl, probe the network, or write the user's home — the un-seamed first cut leaked 3 test runs into the real report file, caught and locked. `sirsi report` prints the last N runs plain-English (`--json` for machines); menubar Home gains the "Last check:" line (quiet secondary when green, amber only on a current degraded condition). Verified: build+vet green, new tests green (kickstart selection/isolation/missing-dir, collector drain, report round-trip/trim/sentence), duty-table pins updated 7→8, live pass wrote a real run ("11:30 — all green", api_reachable true) rendered identically by CLI and file. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A16/A32; ADR-040/042; Fabric Board Producer #204; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Reboot-durable local model broker — launchd owns the bounded server (ADR-045; owner directive "local-llm-sovereignty" 2026-07-23)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-23). The warm Gemma broker was launched via nohup and died on reboot AND once more mid-day — each time a CLOUD agent had to revive it, inverting sovereignty (owner: "the local LLM should survive and reboot everyone else"). The legacy `ai.sirsi.gemma` LaunchAgent couldn't help: a one-shot `KeepAlive=false` launcher whose forked, detached child was invisible to launchd (ADR-031-C provenance). Fix, canonized from the shape already deployed + kill-tested live on the owner's machine (broker pid 36715 SIGKILLed → launchd revived it as 36848 within seconds): **(1)** new `sirsi gemma serve --foreground` runs the full ADR-031 bounded path (RAM refuse-gate, node-derived concurrency, memory-cap wrapper, `--prompt-cache-size/-bytes`), writes the pid/port files + Hapi registration, then **execs the capped server in place** — the pid launchd supervises IS the serving process, so the pid file stays truthful for `--stop`, ADR-040 load-bearing recognition, and Hapi governance; **(2)** new `setup.InstallGemmaBroker()` (run by `sirsi setup`, macOS + MLX-present only, idempotent) writes the `ai.sirsi.gemma-broker` LaunchAgent (`RunAtLoad + KeepAlive`, `ThrottleInterval 30`, `ProcessType Interactive`, `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`, output → `~/.sirsi/gemma-server.log`) and **retires the mis-wired `ai.sirsi.gemma` plist**; **(3)** with the agent installed, plain `sirsi gemma serve` starts the broker THROUGH launchd (`launchctl kickstart`) so there is exactly one supervisor — the gemma-liveness duty's restore path now lands on the durable spine automatically — and `--stop` says plainly that launchd will bring the broker back (with the bootout command to keep it off). P1 watchdog hardening + P2 offline takeover are follow-ons under router items 20260723-130309. Verified: build + vet + `internal/setup` and `cmd/sirsi` tests green; new table tests pin plist shape, MLX-absent skip, legacy retirement, and idempotence. Refs: ADR-045, ADR-031/-A/-B/-C, ADR-040, PANTHEON_RULES.md A32; Changelog: Unreleased
- **`sirsi runner` — self-hosted CI as a product verb (ADR-044, extends ADR-042)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-22, claude-home item 191710; gemma drafting bounced 3× on broker capacity so claude-pantheon built directly). The incident-night installer seed is now a Go verb: `sirsi runner install <owner>/<repo>` (bare repo defaults to SirsiMaster) clones the donor runner software stripping ALL instance state (preserving both proven gotchas: `.runner_migrated` makes config.sh silently skip setup; svc.sh requires a staged `./runsvc.sh`), fetches a registration token via gh (gh owns auth), configures runner `m5-sirsi` with labels `self-hosted,macOS,ARM64,m5`, installs the reboot-durable launchd service, and succeeds only when the runners API reports **online** (60s poll) — proof, not "started". `sirsi runner status` grades every locally-installed runner live against GitHub (online/offline/unregistered/unreachable + busy); `--json` emits the published `[{"repo","name","status","busy"}]` contract for the board producer. `sirsi setup` now offers the install default-yes when run inside a git repo with a GitHub origin (local-frames default: builds on owned silicon, cloud as the exception). Found live: the actions-runner writes `.runner` with a UTF-8 BOM that Go's JSON decoder rejects — every real fleet file silently failed to parse until the status pass surfaced zero runners; BOM now stripped, unparseable dirs surface as `unreadable` instead of vanishing, regression test pins the BOM case. Verified: build+vet+tests green; live `runner status` graded all 7 fleet runners (sirsi-pantheon correctly `working` — it was running its own CI at the time), JSON byte-checked against the contract. Refs: ADR-042, ADR-044, PANTHEON_RULES.md A16/A32 spirit; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Menubar: Ask Sirsi — Local AI panel (on-device state + query, board 1.2.0)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-22, owner directive via claude-home item 013859). The on-device model's state is now visible inside Pantheon and queryable from there. New `AskSirsiView` + Home TOOLS row with a live online/offline chip: a state card renders the board's `local_llm` block in plain English (Online/Offline light, memory in use, uptime, answer-cache cap — NO model identifier: brand-over-model-name is owner canon, so the GUI says "Local AI" and the id stays in the CLI/board), and a query box POSTs OpenAI-style chat JSON straight to the board's live `endpoint`+`query_api` (port-move-proof — never a hardcoded port). Response quirks handled per the conduit's live verification: render `choices[0].message.content` with fallback to the model's `reasoning` field, 90s timeout that reads as "Local AI is busy loading a model" (not an error), offline = "Sirsi restores it automatically each cycle" (never a greyed stale panel). Engine side: `RouterBoard.local_llm` decode + `askLocalAI()`; `loadRouterBoard` now GRAFTS the file board's `local_llm` onto the live node-status read when the file is stale (node-status doesn't carry the block — without the graft, the #279 freshness gate silently blanked the panel; caught by the harness's new live probe). Snapshot harness renders the panel AND performs a real round-trip through `askLocalAI` — this build's run answered `READY` on-device. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10/A23; brand-over-model-name, live-data-not-greyed, plain-english-gui; #279; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Thread registry self-reaps stray/ghost records — ADR-043 supersession reap (one live watcher per surface)** (claude-home, 2026-07-22, owner directive). The registry had accreted **520 records for ~11 live processes** (259 suspended, 215 reaped, 27 closed) — 145 `claude-home` records for ONE live watcher. Root cause: the `SessionEnd` hook `suspend`s every ephemeral session (incl. every 15-min conduit run), suspends are reaper-immune by design (ADR-025/ADR-022), and `ReapDeadThreads` enforced OS-truth on actives but never enforced ADR-024 "one watcher per surface" against duplicates — so ghosts accreted unbounded and `sirsi thread prune` (manual, age-based, 7-day suspended retention) never caught the churn. Fix: new `router.ReapStrayThreads`, run in the read-time integrity pass (`sirsi thread list` + node-status — no daemon). Keyed on `(agent, surface, machine)`: once a **live watcher** holds a surface, its **non-live siblings** (superseded suspends + dead-PID ghosts) retire to `closed`. **ADR-025 preserved** — a suspend is swept ONLY when a live sibling supersedes it; an un-superseded suspend (no live watcher) survives untouched. **OS-truth preserved** — a live PID is never retired. **Nothing lost (owner directive)** — every reap is checked against durable memory: `straySalvage` captures real continuation state (non-boilerplate resume prompt, owned open items, thoth ref, in-flight current item) and inscribes it to the Stele ledger (`stele.TypeThreadReap`) before the sweep; empty `"session ended"` tombstones inscribe nothing (checked, nothing to save). Salvage decision split from the Stele side effect (Rule A16) → unit-testable. Effect: a churny surface can no longer accrete tombstones — the pile is bounded to ~(live surfaces), self-cleaning the moment a successor registers. Verified: full build + vet + `-race` router/stele suites green; 3 new table tests (supersession sweep, lonely-suspend survival + foreign-machine skip, salvage-only-saves-real-work). New `docs/ADR-043-STRAY-THREAD-SUPERSESSION-REAP.md`. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A22/A24/A25/A32; ADR-043; owner directive 2026-07-22; Changelog: v0.23.9-beta
- **Cross-agent QoS + Ra node capacity (PRD P3/P7)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-21). `sirsi relieve --agents` deprioritizes every background AI-agent process (claude-code runners, codex, local mlx/gemma) to background QoS in one pass — renice +10 + `taskpolicy -b`, QoS only (the ANE is CoreML-only; no core-pin claims). New `ReniceTargetAgents` matcher excludes GUI install locations (`/Applications`, `/Frameworks`) so the owner's foreground desktop app is never touched, while claude-code's headless runners under Application Support still match; the A1 protected list is now enforced at the `reniceWith` layer for EVERY target (a pattern drifting to overlap a protected name gets skipped structurally). Preview-then-confirm (A1); live preview found 15 real background runners. `sirsi ra status` now surfaces per-node capacity evidence (free RAM, DynamicReserve, pressure + source) via `guard.SampleNodeCapacity()` — documented in ADR-031-B "Ra exposure". PRD ledger `.agents/prd/claude-pantheon.json` reconciled with evidence: 15 done, 2 owner-gated (P1 pressure-runner placement, P8 roadmap tranche prioritization). Refs: ADR-031-B, PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A16.
- **Menubar: owner-gated items → toast → action screen** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-21, owner directive via claude-home P-owner-gated-toast). Open `to: user` router items (board `owner_gated[]`, schema 1.1.0) now reach the owner: the app re-reads `~/.sirsi/router-board.json` on launch + each 90s tick (file-only on the timer — no CLI spawn when the conduit is down) and posts a `UNUserNotification` for each genuinely-new id (persisted dedupe set `ownerGatedToasted` in UserDefaults — no restart re-spam, no reopened-item re-toast). Clicking the toast deep-links into a new "Owner action" screen (AppDelegate `UNUserNotificationCenterDelegate` → `engine.pendingOwnerItemID` → RootView push); the same screens are reachable from a "🔑 Needs you" home row with count. The action screen renders title/type/age, full body via `sirsi router show <id>`, `refs[]` as reveal-in-Finder buttons, **Mark handled** (`sirsi router close`, confirm-first, provenance-logged) and for decisions a reply field that routes back via `~/.local/bin/sirsi-respond.sh` (sender actually notified, not just an audit close; falls back to close-with-result if the script is absent). Snapshot QA covers both new screens; `CommandResult`-style 120s bound via new `runProgram`. Verified live: relaunch toasted the two real waiting items and the dedupe set persisted. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10/A23; router item 20260721-154426.
- **Menubar: project-aware Ma'at & Net (repo-scoped verbs get a project root)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-07). After #170 the SwiftUI menubar honestly showed "unmeasured — not inside a code repository" for Ma'at — Quality and Net — Plan, because `SirsiEngine.run/runJSON` pin cwd to `$HOME` (correctly — the launchd-cwd=/ disk-walk fix). New optional project root: `SirsiEngine` reads UserDefaults key `projectRoot` (scriptable via `defaults write ai.sirsi.pantheon projectRoot -string ~/Development/<repo>`), validates it (existing dir + `.git`), and runs ONLY the repo-scoped verbs (`maat`, `net`) with cwd=projectRoot; every other verb keeps the `$HOME` pin. The Ma'at/Net drill-ins gain a ProjectBar naming the repo being weighed with an in-popover Menu picker (git repos one level under `~/Development`; a modal NSOpenPanel would dismiss the transient popover) plus "None" to return to the honest default; home rows show the weighed repo name. No project configured (or an invalid/deleted path) stays honestly unmeasured. Also new: `SirsiMenubar --snapshot <dir>` renders the popover's key screens offscreen to PNGs (settles when consecutive frames are byte-identical) — headless display-gated QA without Screen Recording TCC. Verified: rebuilt via `macapp/build-app.sh` (stable "Sirsi Local Code Signing" cert), snapshots show Ma'at weighing `sirsi-pantheon` with a real score and Net reading the repo's build log. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10/A23, #170.
- **ADR-031-C — Broker Enforcement Must Be Universal (no bypass paths)** (claude-home, 2026-07-03). Owner reported the machine repeatedly "churning and effectively shutting down" running codex; diagnosed independently (96% swap, ~1GB free of 51.5GB). Codex itself was not the cause — two pieces of LOCAL automation (outside this repo, not tracked in git) bypassed the already-correct ADR-031-A/B broker entirely: `~/.local/bin/sirsi-gemma-worker.sh` (the router-triage daemon) called `mlx_lm.generate` directly with no RAM gate on its default path, and the `ai.sirsi.gemma` LaunchAgent that starts the warm server invoked raw `mlx_lm.server` directly (zero references anywhere in this codebase) — so the broker's own warm-server detection could never see it. Neither ADR-031-A nor ADR-031-B failed; neither was in the call path. Both fixed and verified live: the worker now calls `sirsi gemma` (confirmed it correctly REFUSED a cold load at ~1GB free rather than blindly loading); the LaunchAgent now runs `sirsi gemma serve --port 11434` with `KeepAlive` corrected `true`→`false` (this command is a one-shot ensure-warm launcher, not a persistent process — it forks a detached Hapi-governed child and exits) and correctly refused to start at the 2×model+DynamicReserve boundary rather than being forced. New `docs/ADR-031-C-BROKER-ENFORCEMENT-UNIVERSAL.md`; `docs/case-studies/2026-06-18-pantheon-did-not-prevent-oom.md` §6 addendum recording the recurrence and the "coverage gap, not design gap" lesson; ADR-INDEX updated (34 → +C sub-decision). Regression guard (CI/lint audit for direct `mlx_lm.*` invocations outside `cmd/sirsi/gemma*.go`) recommended, tracked as a follow-up, not yet built. Refs: ADR-031-A, ADR-031-B, PANTHEON_RULES.md A1, A5; the owner's 2026-07-03 verdict *"this situation is exactly what the pantheon and router are supposed to prevent and then remedy."*
- **router_wait MCP tool** — blocking long-poll counterpart to router_poll; a session stays active waiting for inbox work instead of going idle (anti-idle leg 2, capped 50s). Thin wrapper over PollInbox; files stay source of truth. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27.

### Changed
- **Gemma broker retains MULTIPLE prompt prefixes (`--prompt-cache-size 8`) — oMLX-inspired multi-prefix KV caching for the router's shifting-prefix workload** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-21). Lesson harvested from oMLX (an MLX inference server built for coding agents on Apple Silicon): "every existing MLX server invalidates the KV cache when the prompt prefix shifts," forcing a full context recompute — the exact pattern of Pantheon's router, which alternates between reconcile / build / worker-triage / NL-query prompts that each carry a large STABLE prefix (canon, source) with a small variable tail. Our broker set `--prompt-cache-bytes` (a byte cap) but never `--prompt-cache-size` (the count of DISTINCT KV caches retained), so `mlx_lm.server` held ~one prefix and evicted it on every shift → recompute → the slow TTFT visible on the liveness probe (16–20s). Fix: pass `--prompt-cache-size 8` in the Go serve argv so up to 8 recurring prefixes stay warm simultaneously; total memory is unchanged — the 8 slots SHARE the existing `--prompt-cache-bytes` budget (LRU-evicted by bytes), so this is a pure latency win with **no added RAM risk** (safe under A32/ADR-040). We already hold oMLX's other headline idea — a whole-process memory guard — on the serve path (the wrapper's `capBytes` → `mx.set_cache_limit`), which is why the serve broker survived the 2026-07-21 jetsam while the unbounded cold path did not. Verified: build+vet clean, flag in compiled argv; the flag is a byte-bounded enablement of an existing `mlx_lm.server` capability (magnitude of the TTFT win to be quantified by a before/after benchmark on the router prefix set). Follow-on oMLX ideas noted for evaluation: paged SSD KV tier (large cache without RAM cost), continuous batching (BatchGenerator, ~4.14× at 8× concurrency — gated on the memory guard), and a whole-process memory-guard on the cold path too. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A30, A32; ADR-031, ADR-040; oMLX (ml-explore/mlx discussion #3203); Changelog: Unreleased
### Fixed
- **Liveness probe stops false-flagging a healthy reasoning model as "wedged" — assert transport truth, not content shape** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-24, owner item 172430 + claude-home false-positive item 155040). The launchd liveness watch (and the router's self-healing broker duty — both share `ProbeGemmaState`) called the broker "wedged" whenever `choices[0].message.content` was empty. But the live model is `gemma-4-12B-it-8bit`, a REASONING model that fills `message.reasoning` FIRST; at a small `max_tokens` the whole budget is spent there and `content` is legitimately empty on a perfectly healthy broker (claude-home's evidence: `max_tokens=8` → HTTP 200, `finish_reason="length"`, `content=""` — the probe called this wedged; only `max_tokens≥160` reached content). So the probe fired a P0 to the owner every cycle AND triggered needless Tier-0 broker bounces. Fixed to assert on transport truth: HTTP 200 + `finish_reason ∈ {stop,length}` (or unset) + tokens produced (`usage.completion_tokens>0`, or non-empty content/reasoning — robust to servers that omit `usage`) ⇒ healthy; anything else ⇒ wedged. Content shape is never read as a liveness signal. Probe `max_tokens` dropped 128→32 (we no longer need a full answer to form). Regression-locked in `TestProbeGemmaState_Classification` (reasoning-shaped empty-content response ⇒ healthy; zero-token response ⇒ wedged; via httptest). This binary ALSO carries #300's `recipientFor` routing (machine-health→claude-pantheon) which the installed 11:16 binary predated — so the deploy retires the stale `to:user` producer the owner flagged (15:36Z `liveness-watch → user`). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A32; ADR-040; #300; owner item 172430, claude-home item 155040; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Menubar Home is two honest groups — dead space, fake-state chips, and a lying router count all fixed** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-22, owner verdict on screenshot: "this is a mess"). Four defects, one screen: (1) the 50pt titlebar clearance lived INSIDE the scaled design space, so `.scaleEffect` ballooned it with the window width — a third of a wide panel was dead air; now `50 / scale`, a constant 50pt at every size. (2) Eight rows wore static category labels ("orchestration", "ingestion", "noise sieve") styled exactly like live state — `DeityRow.detail` is now optional and LIVE STATE ONLY; a row with nothing current to say shows just its name. (3) Sixteen flat rows had no hierarchy — Home now renders a NEEDS ATTENTION section (rows appear only while a non-green condition holds: owner-gated items, Horus non-green, router blockers/pending, reclaimable storage) above a quiet TOOLS section; the permanent "Full Disk Access granted" confirmation row is gone (granted = silent). (4) the router row showed "48 pending" with a GREEN dot while the live fabric held 17 — the count came from a stale `router-board.json` (the producer still counts phantom-open store rows the #276 facade hides) and the dot ignored pending entirely; `loadRouterBoard` now trusts the board file only within 5 minutes of freshness (else reads the live CLI), and `routerStatus` grades pending>0 as amber. Verified: clean `swift build`, snapshot harness renders the new Home with the live count (19) under a yellow dot and honest sections. Producer-side count bug FYI'd to claude-home (their lane). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10/A23; feedback canon surfaces-current-actionable-only, live-data-not-greyed; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Dispatch facade no longer resurfaces phantom-open items — and re-closing heals the store** (claude-home, 2026-07-22). Found live on the conduit run: item `20260717-183742…a28` was closed 2026-07-17 in its canonical file (by a pre-facade file-only close), but its store row stayed open — `router pull claude-finalwishes` re-listed it as open for 5 days while `router close` refused with "already closed", a self-sustaining phantom no operator command could clear. Two root causes in `internal/dispatch/facade.go`: (1) `Inbox`'s dual-read union deduped store rows only against file-OPEN items, so an open store row whose file is closed slipped through — now a row missing from `seen` is checked against the canonical file and skipped when the file says closed; (2) `CloseItem` returned on the file's already-closed error BEFORE the store mirror ran, so the heal path was unreachable — `work.Close` now returns a typed `work.ErrAlreadyClosed` and `CloseItem` falls through it to mirror the close into the store. Regression-locked in `TestPhantomOpenStoreRowHiddenAndHealable` (file-only close → Inbox empty → facade re-close heals the row). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A26; ADR-035/036 (Router v2 dual-write); #198 class; Changelog: Unreleased
- **jackal/rules git-fixture tests are now hermetic — the #99 GIT_* env-leak class can no longer commit fixtures onto the host repo** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-22). Under the Ma'at pre-push gate, git exports GIT_DIR/GIT_WORK_TREE into `go test`; git honors those OVER `-C dir`, so initGitRepo's fixture commit ("init", one README) landed on a live feature branch twice on 2026-07-22 (claude-nexus, recovered via reflog) — the same class that hit internal/osiris on 2026-07-09. Fix: package TestMain scrubs every GIT_* var before any test runs (parallel-safe; t.Setenv is illegal under t.Parallel), both fixture helpers use a filtered gitTestEnv(), and a HEAD guard records the enclosing repo's HEAD before the suite and fails loudly if any test mutated it — an escape can never again pass silently. Verified: full suite green under a deliberately exported GIT_DIR with host HEAD byte-identical. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A16/A21 spirit, repo issue #99 class; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Cold-path `sirsi gemma` now bounds its KV cache — closes the last unbounded-Python jetsam vector (A32; claude-home P0 2026-07-21)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-21). A fresh JetsamEvent (2026-07-21 10:02) showed a separate, unbounded `Python` (pid 36339) balloon to **28.88 GB** and force an OS jetsam — the same unbounded-instance pattern as 2026-07-14, a week later. Investigation (evidence, not assumption): the warm broker path IS bounded and deployed — `cmd/sirsi/gemma_serve.go:191` passes `--prompt-cache-bytes` (#215), the installed binary carries the literal, and the `ai.sirsi.gemma` LaunchAgent auto-starts via `sirsi gemma serve` → capped (forensics confirm the live broker pid holds `--prompt-cache-bytes`). So the 28.88 GB balloon was NOT `serve`; it was the **cold `mlx_lm.generate` path** (`cmd/sirsi/gemma.go`), the only non-serve mlx spawn a bare `sirsi gemma` call makes, which passed **no** KV bound — a pathologically long prompt could grow its KV cache without limit. Fix: pass `--max-kv-size 32768` (the generate-path analog of `--prompt-cache-bytes`; `mlx_lm.generate` supports it, verified via `--help`) so the cold path can never balloon unbounded — 32K tokens is generous for Tier-0 work while hard-capping the balloon (mlx rotates the cache past the cap). The cold path was already RAM-gated at START (ADR-031-A/B: refuses if 2×model+reserve won't fit) and serialized (flock); this adds the missing RUNTIME KV bound. Verified: build+vet clean, `--max-kv-size` present in the compiled argv, warm path still answers (no `runGemma` regression). Follow-on noted: the MCP-runner generate paths (`cmd/sirsi-gemma/runner.go`, `internal/gemma`) are a separate binary and still unbounded — tracked separately. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A32; ADR-040; #215; claude-home P0 jetsam 2026-07-21; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Gemma worker (`cmd/sirsi-gemma`) now bounds its `mlx_lm.generate` KV cache — closes the last unbounded mlx spawn on the machine (A32)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-21). The #263 cold-path fix noted a remaining follow-on: the `sirsi-gemma` worker binary (`ai.sirsi.gemma-worker` LaunchAgent) shells `mlx_lm.generate` directly and passed no KV bound — `runner.go` built `--model --prompt --max-tokens --temp` only, so a pathologically long prompt could balloon its Python process unbounded, the same 28.88 GB jetsam vector. Fix: add `--max-kv-size 32768` to the worker's generate argv (const `gemmaWorkerMaxKVSize`, mirroring `gemmaColdMaxKVSize` in `cmd/sirsi/gemma.go`). Every mlx spawn on the box is now bounded: serve (`--prompt-cache-bytes`, #215), cold `sirsi gemma` (`--max-kv-size`, #263), and now the worker. Accepted by claude-home as the P0 follow-on. Verified: build+vet clean, flag present in the compiled argv. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A32; ADR-040; #263; Changelog: Unreleased
### Added
- **liveness-watch proactively alerts on a leaked-session pileup (A32; claude-home-endorsed)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-17). The `sirsi reap-sessions` doctor lever (#259) surfaces the leaked-session pileup only when an operator runs `sirsi diagnose`. This adds a **proactive, alert-only** leg: the launchd liveness-watch now includes a `session-leak` probe that counts leaked claude-desktop task-runner sessions via `reaper.Plan` (read-only; the caller's own ancestry is never counted) and, at/above the same 8-session threshold the doctor uses, routes ONE non-duplicate alert to `user` through the watch's existing dedup/route path — so the pileup that swap-kills the broker is surfaced before it does, even with no operator present. **Alert-only** (claude-home overseer verdict): it never reaps — the hard reap stays owner/doctor-invoked via `sirsi reap-sessions --apply`, and the completion-gated hard reap stays in `ccd-reap-completed.py` (no duplicated kill). The count is behind an injectable seam (A16/A21). Ordered in the watch by severity (broker > memory-death > session-leak > menubar — the leak is the precursor that causes the spiral). Verified live: `liveness-watch run` shows `session-leak ok — 3 leaked (below 8 threshold)` (no false alert); unit test pins the threshold branch. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A32; router item 20260717-220723 (claude-home endorsement); Changelog: Unreleased
### Fixed
- **gemma-worker is now a true singleton (ownership-token; A32 resource hygiene)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-17). Observed 2026-07-17: two `sirsi-gemma-worker.sh` processes running at once — a `make install-gemma-worker` kickstart spawned a replacement while the previous worker was mid-generation (blocked on the broker, slow to honor SIGTERM). Two workers polling the same gemma inbox waste GPU and can double-grab an item. Fix in `scripts/gemma/sirsi-gemma-worker.sh`: the pidfile (`~/.sirsi/gemma-worker.pid`) is now an **ownership token** — the newest worker claims it on start, and each older worker checks ownership at its loop boundary and **steps down** when superseded (a mid-generation worker gets there only after its broker call returns, so it never grabs another item once it has lost ownership — no gap, no double-grab). The EXIT trap only reclaims the token if we still own it, so an exiting old worker never clobbers a newer worker's token. Also folds the previously-clobbered `GEN_STDERR` cleanup into the single EXIT trap (bash keeps only the last trap per signal — the pidfile trap had been silently overwriting it). Verified: `bash -n` clean + an isolated simulation proving old→steps-down, token intact, new→keeps-polling. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A32, A12; the gemma-worker-not-a-singleton router item; Changelog: Unreleased
### Added
- **Gemma liveness wired into the router — self-healing broker survival on Pantheon's supervisor, not the IDE (A32; owner directive 2026-07-17)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-17). Owner: "Gemma's liveness is absolutely critical — even more than [the IDE's]. The local LLM has to survive based on PANTHEON's survival, not the IDE." The gap: the `ai.sirsi.gemma` LaunchAgent is `RunAtLoad + KeepAlive=false` — a one-shot launcher that starts `sirsi gemma serve` at boot and exits (the broker forks a detached child), so if the broker later dies (crash, OOM, wedge) NOTHING restores it until the next reboot; its survival was tied to boot events, not to Pantheon's always-on infrastructure. Fix: a new **`gemma-liveness` supervisor duty** (`internal/router/gemmaliveness.go`) on the resident Horus supervisor — a `KeepAlive` LaunchAgent that is always-on and IDE-independent — that each tick (2-min cadence, faster than auto-heal's 5-min because gemma is the Tier-0 substrate everything depends on) probes the broker via the shared `liveness.ProbeGemmaState` (a REAL `/v1/chat/completions` generation, because health lies — `/v1/models` 200 while content is empty) and RESTORES it: **down** (no port / connection refused) → `sirsi gemma serve` (start); **confirmed-wedged** (up but non-200/empty/timeout, across ≥2 consecutive ticks so a transient never bounces a working broker) → graceful `serve --stop && serve`. Load-bearing-safe (A32/ADR-040): restore is the idempotent, RAM-gated `serve` path (no-ops when warm, refuses rather than OOM), and a wedge is restored with a graceful SIGTERM-to-process-group, **never a SIGKILL**. Mirrors the auto-heal injection pattern — the duty is a no-op by default (`gemma-liveness` runs inert in library/test context) and cmd/sirsi wires the real pass at init, but UNLIKE auto-heal it is **not gated on autonomous mode** (gemma must always live). The `ProbeGemmaState` refactor also splits the read-only liveness-watch probe into down-vs-wedged classification (one "wedged" definition shared by the watch and the duty). Verified live: `sirsi horus supervise --once` shows the `gemma-liveness` duty registered and RAN (healthy broker → no-op, no false restart); unit tests pin the branches (healthy→untouched, down→start, wedged→restart only after 2-strike confirmation, transient-wedge-that-recovers→no restart). Closes the `gemma-broker-no-auto-restart-when-it-dies` item. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27, A29, A32; ADR-040; owner directive 2026-07-17; Changelog: Unreleased
- **`sirsi reap-sessions` — safe reclaim of leaked claude-desktop task-runner sessions (A32 leak-source stopgap; routed by claude-home)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-17). The companion to the launchd liveness watch: the watch alerts on the swap-death, this reclaims its cause. Every Claude scheduled-task/wakeup run leaves a resident `claude-desktop` session that never self-exits (~8/hr → 40 accumulated → 6.8 GB → swap 82% → gemma wedged, 2026-07-17); the runners are process-IDENTICAL to the owner's live interactive session (same argv/env/tty), so there is NO safe blind reaper. The safety contract — ported EXACTLY from the proven reference shell reaper into `internal/reaper` — is **caller-ancestry self-protection**: the verb walks its own PID up the ppid chain to 1 and protects every PID in it, so running it from your live Claude conversation can never close that conversation (the conversation's PID is an ancestor). Only `claude-code/<ver>/claude.app/Contents/MacOS/claude` procs past the age (≥600s) and RSS (≥120 MB) floors are ever candidates — never `/Applications/*.app` (the main app), never the gemma server, never a non-claude process. Default is **dry-run** (Rule A1: selection mutates nothing); `--apply` SIGTERMs, `--force` SIGKILLs survivors; `--json`, `--min-age-sec`, `--min-rss-mb`, `--self-test`. The safety-critical selection (`selectCandidates`) is a PURE function of the process table + protected set, and the OS is behind injectable seams (Rule A16 — `reaper.Deps`: process enumerator, kill, liveness). DOCTOR lever (ADR-037): `sirsi diagnose` counts the pileup read-only (its own ancestry never counted) and surfaces `sirsi reap-sessions --apply` as the fix when the pileup is large (≥8) OR memory is already dying — owner/doctor-invoked ONLY, never auto-run from a headless task (no safe signature to distinguish the owner's live session — alert-only there, which the liveness watch already does). Regression test pins the contract: a synthetic table where the caller's live session (400) is old + large is NEVER selected (nor SIGTERM'd), dry-run calls Kill zero times, and `--apply --force` SIGKILLs only the TERM-resistant leaked survivor. Verified live: `--self-test` PASS (5 ancestry PIDs protected), dry-run found 4 leaked sessions (831 MB reclaimable) while protecting the caller; `sirsi diagnose` shows the honest OK/Warn leaked-sessions line. The launchd liveness watch eliminates the leak SOURCE; this is the manual/doctor reclaim lever + the stopgap. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1, A16, A32; ADR-037; `reference_scheduled_task_session_leak`; router item 20260717-220723 (claude-home); Changelog: Unreleased
- **`sirsi liveness-watch` — OS-level (launchd) reboot-proof liveness safety net (A32 durability; routed by claude-home)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-17). The router conduit, gemma broker, and menubar were watched only by Claude-app scheduled tasks: they survive a session close but NOT a full OS reboot until a human relaunches the app — and each scheduled run leaks a resident claude-desktop session (the swap-death source of 2026-07-17). New `ai.sirsi.liveness-watch` LaunchAgent (RunAtLoad + StartInterval 900, no KeepAlive — the watch runs and exits) invokes `sirsi liveness-watch run` each tick: (a) a REAL `/v1/chat/completions` generation probe against the warm gemma broker (health lies — `/v1/models` returns 200 while content is empty; wedged = no port / connect error / non-200 / empty content / >30s), (b) a SwiftUI-menubar alive check by exact executable path, (c) a swap/free-RAM memory-death read via the shared `guard.SampleMemoryDeath()` (one definition of "dying" — the same spiral ladder the doctor uses), then routes exactly ONE non-duplicate item to `user` for the worst current owner-fixable blocker (deduped against the open inbox so a persistent wedge never floods the router). It needs no Claude app and spawns no claude session — zero-leak by construction — and is load-bearing-safe (A32/ADR-040): read-and-route ONLY, it never signals, kills, resizes, or restarts any process, never touches `/Applications`, never acts on an unverified PID. `sirsi liveness-watch {run,install,uninstall,status}` + a `sirsi diagnose` lever line (not-installed → Warn with instant fix `sirsi liveness-watch install`). Verified live: `run` answered gemma in 16s, read swap 85%/free 20 GB as NOT a spiral (below the 90%+starvation threshold → no false alarm, A32), menubar running, routed nothing, exit 0. Tests: plist lever-shape (invokes run, RunAtLoad+StartInterval, no KeepAlive), route-once-and-dedup (a down broker routes exactly one item on the first pass, zero on the second), and severity ordering (broker > memory > menubar; OK/non-fixable never routed). Once proven, this retires the leak-prone Claude scheduled tasks (the launchd job is the correct durable watcher precisely because it is a plain binary invocation, not a resident session). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27, A29, A32; ADR-040; the launchd-durability router item (claude-home 2026-07-17); Changelog: Unreleased
- **`sirsi router prune` — retention sweep for the router fabric (90-day default; owner directive 2026-07-10)** (claude-home, 2026-07-10).
