The router core is daemonless by design, so nothing ever owned the lifecycle of the byproduct artifacts it leaves behind — closed items, dated incident dumps, and above all append-only logs accumulated without bound (a 54 MB `~/.sirsi/triage.err.log`, a 17 MB `wake.log`, a 45 MB one-time quarantine dump, a 34 MB uncapped `processes.json` snapshot). New `router prune [--days N] [--dry-run] [--json] [--items-only|--logs-only] [--no-home]` applies two orthogonal caps: an **age cap** (default 90 days — anything older is removed) and a **size cap** (active append-only logs tail-capped to their most recent 4 MiB; the regenerated process snapshot removed above 8 MiB). `internal/work.PruneItems` deletes only `status: closed` items past the window and NEVER touches an open item or one whose `closed:` timestamp is missing/unparseable (fail-safe: keep). `internal/router.PruneArtifacts` extends that to dated `quarantine-YYYYMMDD-*` dumps, the `logs/` dir, terminal (completed/failed/blocked) `work-queue.json` records, and the snapshot; `PruneHomeLogs` sweeps `~/.sirsi`. Live logs are shrunk with **copy-truncate** (logrotate's strategy — the inode is preserved so a launchd daemon holding an open append fd keeps writing to the same file instead of an unlinked one) rather than rename, which would silently lose the writer's in-flight lines. Every destructive path is gated behind `--dry-run`, which reports exact reclaim bytes and mutates nothing (Rule A1); the snapshot removal is non-destructive because `LoadProcessRegistry` returns an empty registry for a missing file and the writer regenerates it. First live run reclaimed **121.2 MiB** (`.agents/idea-router` 131M→77M, `~/.sirsi` 86M→18M) with the router fully functional afterward. Table-driven tests cover item age/open/no-date fail-safes, quarantine age boundary, log delete-vs-cap, work-queue terminal-record drop, snapshot cap, tail-cap line alignment, and dry-run no-mutation; `go build`/`vet`/`gofmt`/`golangci-lint`/`go test` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1, A12, A16; owner directive 2026-07-10 ("at most a 90-day log period; logging beyond that is wasteful"); Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Router board — menubar fabric-liveness surface + wake-install + `sirsi router install-daemons` + honest auth probe** (claude-pantheon P-ROUTER-BOARD lane, 2026-07-02). Makes the agent-router's liveness owner-actionable from the native menubar and closes the false-logout auth bug behind it. **(1) Menubar "Router — Fabric" view** (SwiftUI `macapp/`, ADR-030): reads `~/.sirsi/router-board.json` (the lean board the conduit regenerates each cycle), falling back to shelling `sirsi router node-status --json` when absent — one set of Decodables covers both since they share the NodeStatus contract (schema 1.0.0). It leads with **BLOCKERS — current + fixable ONLY** ([[feedback_surfaces_current_actionable_only]]): a REAL logout (`needs_login==true`) and a broken/missing router daemon (`launch_agents[].installed==false` or `program_found==false`), never `auth_ok==false` alone. Then **stranded inboxes** — per-agent open-item counts, each drillable to a one-click action; a healthy fabric reads calm green. Every pushed view carries the mandatory `BackBar`; copy is plain English ([[feedback_plain_english_gui]]). **(2) Wake-enablement**: each stranded agent's drill-in has a one-click "Arm wake channel" = `sirsi router wake-install <agent>`; an auth blocker gets a re-auth affordance (Open Terminal / Copy command) that NEVER authenticates programmatically — the /login is the operator's action. **(3) `sirsi router install-daemons` — single backstop** (also wired into `sirsi setup` on macOS — [[feedback_permissions_in_install]]; backlog ruling 20260629-230327): the router's background duties (dispatch pump, hourly queue sweep, registry-police reap) are FOLDED into the ONE resident supervisor loop (`internal/router/supervisorduties.go` — cadence-gated, bounded 2-min, error-isolated passes inside `sirsi horus supervise`, Phase-1 invoking the existing `.agents/idea-router` scripts as subroutines); `install-daemons` now ensures the single supervisor LaunchAgent (reusing `setup.InstallSupervisor`, content-compare idempotent so a healthy loop is never bounced) and MIGRATES AWAY the three legacy per-duty plists when present (unload + remove, reported). `node-status` marks the three legacy labels `legacy:true` and inventories the supervisor (`ai.sirsi.horus.agent-router`, role `router-supervisor`) so no surface alarms on the legacy agents' expected absence. **(4) Honest auth probe** (`internal/router/nodestatus.go`): `DefaultAuthProbe`'s Claude timeout raised 8s→30s (a cold CLI start routinely exceeds 8s), and a THIRD state added — a timeout/transient/env failure is now `Degraded` (probe inconclusive), NOT `needs_login`; only an explicit `isAuthError` signature means "logged out". `blocked_items` and the dashboard's red drift flag now count ONLY a real logout, so an inconclusive probe can no longer strand every pending item or paint Horus red behind a slow CLI. **(5) `sirsi router board`** prints `~/.sirsi/router-board.md`; with the root `--json` flag it emits a real JSON envelope ({path, exists, content, modified_at}) instead of silently printing markdown. **(6) App-context repo root** (`internal/router/reporootmarker.go`): root-ful invocations (`sirsi setup`, `sirsi agent register`, supervisor start/install) persist the clone root to `~/.sirsi/pantheon-repo` (env-overridable via `SIRSI_PANTHEON_REPO_MARKER`), and `FindRepoRoot` reads it as a validated LAST resort (`.agents/idea-router` must exist there) — so every menubar lever now works when launched by launchd (cwd=/) with no dev shell open. **(7) Menubar honesty**: `routerSummary` shows "no data yet" when the board never loaded — never a false "healthy". Tests: `TestDefaultAuthProbe_TimeoutIsDegradedNotLoggedOut` (timeout → degraded, needs_login=false, blocked_items=0) + `TestDefaultAuthProbe_EnvTimeoutOverride` (SIRSI_AUTH_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS=500 + injected slow CLI → fast Degraded return, never NeedsLogin) + `TestClaudeProbeTimeout_EnvParsing` + `TestDefaultAuthProbe_LoginSignatureNeedsLogin` (real /login → needs_login=true, counts the blocker) + supervisor-duty cadence/error-isolation/missing-script tests + legacy-migration install/idempotent/failure tests + repo-root-marker last-resort/precedence/stale-rejection tests; `go build/vet/test`, gofmt, golangci-lint(0), and `swift build` all green. This is the v2 re-land of #147 (merged prematurely, reverted in #154) with every correction from the 2026-07-02 adversarial review folded in (B1 single-backstop, B2 app-context root, B4 probe-override test, minors 5-7). Known contract-drift follow-up: the conduit script that generates `~/.sirsi/router-board.json`/`.md` lives out-of-repo. Refs: adversarial review 2026-07-02 #147; backlog ruling 20260629-230327; PANTHEON_RULES.md A26, A27, A28, A1, A10; ADR-026, ADR-030; [[feedback_surfaces_current_actionable_only]], [[feedback_permissions_in_install]], [[feedback_plain_english_gui]]; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Orchestration Brain P1 — control plane + `sirsi brain` (A29, ADR-034; reuses the EXISTING wake substrate)** (claude-fable, 2026-07-02). The first increment of the tiered, pluggable orchestration brain: `internal/brain/config.go` (the `~/.sirsi/brain.yaml` control-plane config — per-role provider `none`/`local:<id>`/`hosted:<id>`, structured YAML via the repo's yaml.v3 standard, with the LLM **Level 0–3 derived** from config, not stored) + `internal/brain/controlplane.go` (a `Dispatcher` interface over the CURRENT router so Router v2 can swap underneath later — Amendment 1) + `cmd/sirsi/braincmd.go` (`sirsi brain {status,use,levels,doctor,test}`, `--json` on status/doctor for the menubar/Nexus surfaces). The **deterministic Tier-0 floor** is enforced structurally: dispatch is pinned to `none` and both the config layer and `doctor` reject a model plugged into dispatch, so a fresh install runs at Level 0 (zero AI, zero keys, zero cost) and `brain use <role>` swaps a tier with **no restart**. Crucially, the wake/registry substrate is **NOT rebuilt** (Rule 0): `brain status`/`doctor` READ the existing `internal/router` API (`ProbeWakeReadiness`, `CollectNodeStatus`) to **surface + enforce** the Tier-0 Registry/Wake invariant (A29 §Tier-0) — every registered-but-unwakeable agent and every stranded inbox is flagged in plain English with a fix that points at the EXISTING verbs (`sirsi router wake-install`, `sirsi router doctor --fix` → `WakePass`); waking + repair stay the router's job. The RAM gate consults `guard.NodeCapacity.Fits()` and reports "won't fit — N GB short" instead of OOMing (Amendment 3). Verified live against the real router (surfaced 16 registered agents with no live wake-channel + 4 stranded inboxes) and table-tested (provider parse/round-trip, Level derivation, floor-invariant rejection, swap-without-restart, doctor findings via an injected fake Dispatcher + injected NodeCapacity, RAM gate both directions). `go build ./...` + `go vet` + gofmt + golangci-lint clean; `go test ./internal/brain/...` green. Canon: PANTHEON_RULES **A29** (§2.26) + **ADR-034** + ADR-INDEX. Remaining Brain phases (T1 triage, T2 agentic execution, menubar/Nexus swap surfaces, provenance-ledger wiring, E2E level matrix) tracked in ADR-034 + the PRD. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A29, A27, A11, A16; ADR-034, ADR-031-A/B; `docs/prd/ORCHESTRATION_BRAIN.md`; Changelog: Unreleased
- **`sirsi thread watch [--install|--uninstall]` — durable per-thread wake channel (A27), a thin alias over `router wake-install`** (claude-fable, 2026-07-02). A27 asks every registered thread to run a heartbeat loop that survives session restarts. That durable cross-session watcher ALREADY EXISTS for worker/headless surfaces: the per-agent `KeepAlive` LaunchAgent (`ai.sirsi.router.wake.<agent>`) that `router.InstallWakeLaunchAgent` writes and `sirsi router wake-loop` runs. This adds NO new watcher subsystem — `sirsi thread watch --install` is a thread-scoped, self-resolving front door over that same channel: it resolves the CURRENT thread's agent the way `heartbeat`/the Stop hook do (via `--agent`, `$SIRSI_AGENT_ID`, the session→agent marker, or a sole live thread) and delegates to the existing `InstallWakeLaunchAgent` path (Rule 0, reuse-not-reinvent). `sirsi thread watch --install` == `sirsi router wake-install <that agent>`. The one genuinely-missing primitive — a clean-off path — is filled: new `router.UninstallWakeLaunchAgent` (best-effort `launchctl bootout` + plist removal, idempotent) backs `sirsi thread watch --uninstall`; `sirsi thread watch` with no flag reports install status. Interactive `claude` sessions are unaffected (they heartbeat via `/loop`). Install/uninstall are idempotent (safe to re-run) and never duplicate the supervisor or wake-loop. Documented the equivalence in `.agents/idea-router/README.md` §Heartbeat Loop so future agents know the two verbs are one channel. Tested via the injected-writer boundary (Rule A16): router-level idempotent uninstall under `launchAgentsDirOverride`, and a CLI end-to-end install→status→uninstall under a pinned `HOME` (never touches the real `~/Library/LaunchAgents` or launchd domain) + the both-flags-error and unresolved-agent-error guards. `go build`/`vet`/`gofmt`/`golangci-lint`(0)/`go test ./internal/router/... ./cmd/sirsi/` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md Rule 0, A16, A27; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **gitleaks secrets-scanning gate armed on CI + pre-push (A28 backstop)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-02). Mechanical committed-secret scanner mirroring SirsiMaster/SirsiNexusApp PR #111, wired as the automated backstop for PANTHEON_RULES.md A28 (Ma'at Gate & CI Protection Mandate) and SECURITY.md — a behavioral "don't commit secrets" rule is not enough on its own. New `.github/workflows/gitleaks.yml` runs `gitleaks/gitleaks-action@v2.3.9` (pinned, not `@latest`) on every push + PR to main/develop (auto-scoped to new commits), plus a `workflow_dispatch` + weekly `schedule` full-history sweep (`fetch-depth: 0`) to catch anything predating the gate; the job is designed to be a REQUIRED status check ("Secrets Scan (gitleaks)") for branch protection. New `.gitleaks.toml` extends the default ruleset (`useDefault = true`) with a PRECISE, individually-documented allowlist — only the two Seshat filter test suites (`internal/seshat/filter_test.go`, `coverage_boost_test.go`, which carry secret-SHAPED fixtures by design: the AWS-docs EXAMPLE key, runtime-concatenated `ghp_`/`sk_test_` literals, a truncated PEM placeholder) and a doc-example regex for ellipsis-elided Stripe keys — never a blanket suppression. The `.githooks/pre-push` Ma'at gate gained an early gitleaks step that scans only the pushed commit range (fast; warns-not-blocks if gitleaks is absent, since CI is authoritative). Baseline evidence: a full-history scan (1057 commits, 33.72 MB) finds ZERO leaks — this repo has no committed secrets. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A28, SECURITY.md; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Router v2 Phase 1 — `internal/routerstore` durable SQLite index (foundation, wired to nothing)** (claude-fable, 2026-07-02). The durable-dispatch spine from `docs/prd/ROUTER_V2_DURABLE_DISPATCH.md` (also committed this change — the PRD was drafted but never tracked). A new CGO-free SQLite store (`modernc.org/sqlite`, Rule A3 static-binary mandate) that is an **additive, queryable index/mirror** of the file router — the markdown items under `<router>/items/*.md` remain the CANONICAL source of truth (Rule A12, A26); nothing in the live router is wired to this store yet, so it ships at zero risk behind tests (PRD Phase 1 acceptance). The DB lives OUTSIDE any git repo (`~/.sirsi/router.db`) so runtime state never pollutes the tree (PRD /goal #2). Schema mirrors `internal/work.Item` field-for-field — `items(id,from_agent,to_agent,title,type,status,opened,closed,instructions,result,wake_status,wake_attempted_at,wake_adapter,wake_error)` + `(to_agent,status)` index; plus `agents(id,registered_at,last_seen,pid)` and `state(key,value)` — so import/export is lossless including the wake-delivery truth (PRD /goal #4); the parity is ENFORCED by a reflection test (`TestFieldFidelityWithWorkItem`: field-set equality both directions, field↔column bijection against the live schema, full Put→Get round-trip), so a future `work.Item` field addition fails the build until the schema catches up. Schema is versioned via the SQLite `user_version` pragma + numbered append-only migrations, so future column adds work against existing DBs. API: `Send`/`Get`/`Inbox`/`ListAll`/`CloseItem`/`Put` (item lifecycle, ids match the file router's `timestamp-from-to-slug` convention via a byte-for-byte `slugify` port; `Send` enforces the ADR-024 §5 type vocabulary `proposal|review|decision` exactly as `router_submit`→`internal/router.Submit` does, while `Put`/`Backfill` stay deliberately lax so nonstandard legacy types mirror losslessly), `RegisterAgent`/`Heartbeat`/`GetAgent`/`ListAgents` (idempotent on id — a surface restart never duplicates a record, Rule A27 intent), `SetState`/`GetState`, `Backfill([]Item)` — the idempotent, best-effort store-side spine of the Phase 4 one-shot importer (takes a plain `[]Item` to avoid an import cycle, keeping Phase 1 dependency-free) — and `ExportMarkdown(dir)`, the PRD §4 DB-corruption recovery path: it emits each item in the file router's exact frontmatter+body format, round-trip proven byte-identical (file → `Backfill` → `ExportMarkdown` → same bytes; the file router re-parses the dump losslessly). `CloseItem` is an atomic `UPDATE ... WHERE status='open'` (no Get→check→UPDATE TOCTOU): concurrent double-close yields exactly one success + one `ErrAlreadyClosed`, proven 200/200 trials under `-race`. WAL + single-writer connection for concurrent-write safety (Rule A21 intent at the DB layer); injectable clock seam for deterministic timestamps (Rule A16). Table-driven CRUD, inbox filtering, backfill idempotency, agent lifecycle, field fidelity, export round-trip, double-close race, migration versioning, and a 16×20 concurrent Put/Close stress test all green under `-race`; 86.8% coverage; golangci-lint 0 issues. Remaining phases (2 event-driven dispatch, 3 MCP/CLI shared facade, 4 migration+cutover) tracked in the PRD. Refs: docs/prd/ROUTER_V2_DURABLE_DISPATCH.md, ADR-024 §5, PANTHEON_RULES.md A3, A12, A16, A21, A26, A27; adversarial review 2026-07-02; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Five-screen operator console (TUI) — Pulse / Waste / Ghosts / Health / Activity (ADR-020)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-01). The interactive terminal surface, "Horus", built on bubbletea v2 + lipgloss v2 per the merged `docs/TUI_DESIGN_PROOF.md` design gate. `sirsi` with no arguments now opens the console on a genuine terminal; every non-interactive context (piped, redirected, `--json`, `--quiet`, or `SIRSI_NO_TUI=1`) still prints help unchanged. The five screens each read exactly one merged `sirsi <verb> --json` contract through an injectable command-runner seam (Rule A16), so Go stays the brain and the TUI never re-implements probing: **Pulse** (memory-first — RAM gauge + pressure + top hogs from `vitals`, 2s tick, one-key relief with a before/after delta from `relieve`), **Waste** (`scan` → per-item review with drill-in and tier toggles → tier-honest `clean` with a freed-space proof), **Ghosts** (`ghosts` structured per-app residuals → `clean`), **Health** (`diagnose` findings, each with its HONEST one-key fix classified by FixKind — a `guidance` no-op is never offered as a fix, ADR-033), and **Activity** (read-only `activity` provenance ledger). Structural guarantees from the proof: status-bar hints are generated from the command Registry so a shown key is provably wired (a dead hint cannot render); deity/status identity uses BMP-safe sigils, never layout-bearing hieroglyphs, so terminal-font tofu can never break the grid (Rules G1–G3); color is semantic with a truecolor→256→16→attribute ladder and every severity also carries a text token (NO_COLOR/colorblind lose nothing); destructive `clean` never fires from one keystroke — a confirm modal names the targets and requires a deliberate second confirmation (Rule A1). Startup is lazy (only the first screen loads on Init — the first frame never blocks on a scan); every screen has explicit loading/empty/error states; resize-safe (WindowSizeMsg, width-clamped, `< 80×24` takeover). Supersedes the Gate-2 fixture scaffold (Scan/Ra/Inbox proof views removed cleanly). Deterministic golden-frame + keymap-completeness + confirm-gate + fixKind-honesty + non-TTY-help tests (canned JSON through the injected runner; `-race` clean). Refs: ADR-020, ADR-033; PANTHEON_RULES.md §2.7, A16, A21, A25; Changelog: Unreleased

### Changed
- **Ma'at coverage lift — audit 50→85+** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-01). Three audit-honesty fixes plus a portfolio-wide test-coverage lift. (1) The default fast audit no longer trusts an incomplete coverage cache: when the cache lacks entries for any threshold module it falls back to a real scan and repopulates itself — the root cause of ~45 bogus "no coverage data found" warnings pinning the score at 50. (2) The coverage parser now recognizes Go's modern bare `-cover` output for packages with no test files (`\tpkg\t\tcoverage: 0.0%`), which matched neither the `ok|FAIL` nor the `?` pattern — such modules were invisible to Ma'at. (3) FeatherWeight is now threshold-normalized COMPLIANCE (cov/threshold, capped 100) instead of raw coverage, so a Tier C module that meets its declared 30% standard no longer drags the average — the feather IS the standard. Coverage lifted to green across the weak modules: ka 42.7→89.7 (injectable seams for system_profiler/brew/lsregister enumerators), mirror 60→94.6 (Serve loop, scan flow, browse/pick handlers, partial hashing), guard 72→88, ra 33→95, setup 34→77, mcp/cleaner to threshold, and first-ever tests for help (94%) and vitals (94%). All new tests deterministic (t.TempDir only, injected exec seams, no t.Parallel with package-level swaps). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A16, A17, A21

### Added
- **`docs/AGENT_ROLES_CONTRACT.md` + canon A28 true-up (spec-fix Gap 3)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-16). The claude/codex/gemma/bind contract now exists as one mechanical page: what gemma receives (embedded source only — no tools, no paths) and returns (complete files, `MISSING-SOURCE` markers, always a Tier-0 DRAFT), what codex is authoritative on (source-deep review at current head, verdict returned in the requester's Result), what bind mechanically is (ADR-041: APPROVED review, login ≠ author, current head SHA; the `sirsi-bind` key on the conduit host IS the enforcement), A30 tier routing, and A26 relay mechanics. First draft produced by the local gemma worker via the new `TASK: draft` path (135s on-device, zero API tokens) and Tier-2 reviewed/corrected before landing — the contract's own tiering doctrine, exercised to write it. Canon true-up: A28 claimed `enforce_admins=false`; live protection is `true` (verified via `gh api`) — the line now states reality in all three synced canon copies (PANTHEON_RULES.md, CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md), with the founder override documented as a per-decision toggle, never a standing bypass. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A26, A28, A30; ADR-041; spec plan Gap 3; Changelog: Unreleased

### Added
- **Menubar Home leads with MEMORY, not storage — the pre-eminent-view canon** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-17; canon `project_pantheon_memory_first_view`). Home's headline was safe-to-reclaim storage ("2.0 GB"); canon says RAM is the pre-eminent surface. New `MemoryVitals` (`sirsi vitals --json`) drives a memory-first lead card: free RAM as the headline number with a pressure light (green normal / amber warn / red critical), swap + total on the evidence line, the biggest process named ("biggest: Claude Helper 601 MB"), and storage-to-reclaim demoted to a secondary gold line. Falls back to the storage lead only until vitals load, so Home is never blank; `.task`-driven with a snapshot-harness preload so QA renders the real card. Gemma was dispatched this build via `TASK: build` first (it returned empty — logged as a worker defect handed to claude-nexus); the card was then built Tier-2 directly. Verified: installed-app snapshot shows "9.8 GB free · memory healthy · swap 7.1 GB". Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10; `project_pantheon_memory_first_view`; #232; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Work Board is broadcast every supervisor pass — every thread reads one file** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-17; owner directive "the job board should be broadcast through the router to all threads"). The Work Board (#243) was pull-only — an agent had to run `sirsi router workboard` to see it. Now the full board (packages + peers + pace/turnaround, `ComputeWorkBoard`) is folded into the `SuperviseReport` and written to `board.json` on EVERY supervisor pass (best-effort; a compute error leaves it nil and never fails the tick). `board.json` is the one file every thin renderer already reads (menubar, TUI, dashboard, wake-loops, CTR), so the board reaches every thread with no per-thread aggregation and no new channel. Test asserts `report.WorkBoard != nil` and the persisted `board.json` carries the `work_board` block. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A26, A32; #243; owner directive 2026-07-17; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Universal Thread Census + Work Board overseer role — Rule A32** (claude-pantheon ↔ claude-home, 2026-07-17; owner directive). The invariant: every non-system agent-class process on every surface — CPU **and GPU** — exists as a registered thread, no misses, current and future. Driven by two jetsam incidents caused by the model broker: a ~30 GB wired-Metal GPU process registered in NO registry, invisible to every board, reaper, and overseer. **(1) `internal/router/census.go`** — the census reconciles `ps` against the thread registry via the `censusMatchers` table (broker/mlx → `gemma`/`gpu-server`; workers; supervisor) and REGISTERS every unmatched agent-class process (RegisterThread's (agent,pid) idempotency + machine-id scoping; foreign records never mask local processes; registers-never-kills — governance stays with ADR-022/ADR-031). Runs as the supervisor's seventh duty (`thread-census`, 10-min cadence) so a FUTURE process is caught within one cadence of first launch; extensibility contract: a new agent-class service ships its matcher row in the same change or it is a Ma'at governance failure. **(2) `sirsi thread census [--json]`** — on-demand pass with per-process outcomes. **(3) Overseer charter**: claude-home (the router conduit) is the Work Board OVERSEER — scheduled sweeps read `sirsi router workboard --json`, verify the census invariant (zero unregistered agent-class processes), escalate misses as router items, publish board state ambiently; the supervisor duty stays as the machine-local backstop (two independent legs, no single point of blindness). **(4) Canon**: PANTHEON_RULES **A32** (§2.29) in all three synced copies — the sync also trued CLAUDE.md/GEMINI.md up to the canonical body. Tests: registered/known/system-untouched/idempotent second pass, foreign-machine record never masks a local process; full router suite green (duty table now 6). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27, A29, A32; ADR-022, ADR-031; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Autonomous auto-heal: the monitor→identify→FIX loop, gated + GateAction-floored (ADR-039 P3)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-16; router items 20260713-213507 + 20260714-170033). The #203 autonomous switch finally has its real-time consumer: new `internal/autoheal.Run` — one bounded pass that, ONLY when `brain.AutonomousMode()` is ON, runs the health doctor and applies the remediation lever of every Warn+ finding through TWO lines of defense: (1) the whitelist — only the doctor's own remediation catalog executes (every Fix is a safety-gated `sirsi …` verb; anything else is refused), and (2) **`router.GateAction` on the action text — the shipped-but-never-wired second line now has its production call site**: a Gated decision downgrades the fix to a proposal, never applies. OFF = propose (default): the pass is a silent no-op and every existing surface keeps carrying the findings with their Fix buttons. Bounded by design: ≤3 fixes per pass, 1-hour per-lever cooldown (a relief lever that didn't clear a finding needs the owner, not a hot loop), 2-minute exec timeout, per-fix failures isolated. Every outcome is inscribed to the Stele (`auto_heal` events — no black box, A29). Runs as the supervisor's fifth folded duty (`auto-heal`, 5-min cadence) via an injected seam (`router.SetAutoHealFn`, wired in cmd/sirsi — internal/autoheal imports router for GateAction, so a direct import would cycle; the default no-ops for library consumers). With the trigger-mesh-fired supervisor this IS the launchd-managed real-time manager: monitor (doctor) → identify (Warn+ with lever) → FIX (gated apply), continuously, owner-switchable via `sirsi autonomous on|off`. Tests: OFF-is-inert, Warn+-levers-applied, GateAction-floor-holds, non-sirsi-verb-refused, budget+cooldown, fix-failure-isolated; full router suite green (duty table now 5). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1, A16, A21, A29; ADR-033, ADR-039; #203, #209; Changelog: Unreleased

### Fixed
- **Menubar drill-downs show full information; the Eye keeps its menu-bar position; gemma sizing stops the delay spirals** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-17; owner live-QA: "serious delays across the board", "drill-down screens incomplete or dead end or don't give enough information", "Pantheon menubar gets swallowed up by Outlook"). **(1) Delays root-caused and fixed (#238)**: the warm gemma broker had survived a launcher kickstart for 2 days holding the 31B's wired Metal weights — killing it freed **627 MB → 30.9 GB** instantly. Two durable fixes: the resolver's DEFAULT-pick budget is now 35% of physical RAM (the old `total−16` arithmetic kept re-selecting a 28.8 GB model a 51.5 GB box cannot hold beside a desktop — owner hybrid policy 2026-06-14: fleet-safe default, big model on-demand), and the step-down bounce now SIGTERMs `gemma-capped-server.py` itself + re-warms through the RAM-gated launcher (kickstarting the one-shot `ai.sirsi.gemma` launcher provably restarts nothing). **(2) Drill-down information density (#239)**: the shared ResultView renderer truncated every evidence value to one line and clipped action subtitles mid-sentence — labels/values now wrap fully and are selectable, subtitles wrap, and the Go-side evidence caps rose 10→50 rows (seshat summaries 60→160 chars), so Seshat/Ra/ghosts stop hiding their substance behind "+N more". Verified live: installed-app snapshots show full titles, visit counts, timestamps. **(3) Eye visibility (#239)**: a freshly-launched status item sits leftmost and is the first macOS hides when Outlook's transient items crowd the bar; a stable `statusItem.autosaveName` now persists the owner's Cmd-drag position across relaunches (one drag to the right of the crowd sticks forever). **(4) QA floor raised (#239)**: the snapshot harness renders ALL 17 top-level screens (was 4) — a screen the harness doesn't draw is a screen that can ship broken past a walked sign-off (the #221 lesson, now structural). Refs: ADR-031, PANTHEON_RULES.md A10; #221, #235; Changelog: Unreleased
- **`router doctor` loop-dead is per-agent, not per-session** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-16; claude-home router item 20260714-210359). The doctor flagged EVERY live claude.app session-thread without a `/loop` watcher as `loop-dead` — three concurrent claude-home CCD sessions (all OS-alive owner sessions) each raised a separate "NOT armed" warning, though an agent only needs ONE armed watcher to consume its inbox. **Fix**: new `router.AgentLoopDead` — an agent warrants the alarm only when it has open inbox items AND zero armed watchers across all its live threads (the same per-agent verdict `computeStranded`/`AgentArmed` already use — the doctor's per-thread listing now scopes to genuinely loop-dead agents instead of duplicating per session). Redundant same-agent sessions are suppressed; the warning copy now points at both arming paths (`/loop` or `sirsi router wake-install`). Per-thread `armed` stays honest on node-status/menubar (it reports that thread's true state); only the ALARM aggregates per agent ([[feedback_surfaces_current_actionable_only]] — alarm only what is current and fixable). Table-driven test: one-armed-of-three suppresses siblings, zero-armed-with-items alarms, no-items never alarms, unknown agent never alarms. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27; #79/#80, #178; router item 20260714-210359; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Wake-loop owns worker-thread liveness — keyed-singleton adoption across restarts (P-heartbeat-owner)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-16; claude-home router item 20260714-210322, owner directive 2026-07-14 "the local LLM/Pantheon should be responsible for keeping the heartbeats alive"). `RunWakeLoop` already heartbeats its worker thread every tick (A27), but it registered by `(agent, pid)` — and launchd hands the loop a NEW pid on every bootout/bootstrap, so each restart minted a fresh `surface=worker` thread and left the old record active until a reap pass; agents accumulated duplicate workers (claude-finalwishes carried two), and the only OTHER heartbeat sources were interactive `/loop` sessions or per-thread watcher scripts in `~/.sirsi/` **pinned to specific thread ids** that rotted the moment a session cycle minted a new id (both live examples were pinned to dead threads — they hit their "thread gone → exit 0" guard forever). **Fix**: new `adoptWorkerThread` (`internal/router/wake.go`) — the keyed singleton on `(agent, surface=worker, machine)` per the #178 pattern: on loop start, re-point the newest live worker record at the calling process (thread id stays stable across restarts), retire any other OS-dead worker duplicate for that agent (ADR-022 — live and suspended records never touched, foreign-machine records out of scope per #223), and mint fresh only when no record exists. The rotting-watcher-script class is retired operationally: `~/.sirsi/claude-home-watcher.sh` (pinned thr-e69b8b0f15899121, dead) and `~/.sirsi/watch-claude-home-thr-afa1a26bc9b45159.sh` (pinned thr-afa1a26bc9b45159, dead) verified script-content + no-running-process + thread-absent, then deleted; no in-repo code path generates that class (the live `~/.sirsi/watchers/` scripts are parameterized by argument, not pinned, and were untouched). Worker liveness now comes from exactly two honest sources: the always-on wake-loop (infra) or a live interactive `/loop` — never a pinned script. Tests: stable-across-restart adoption, dead-duplicate reaped + live-duplicate and other-agent untouched, empty-registry mints fresh, foreign-machine record neither adopted nor reaped. `go build`/`vet`/`gofmt`/full `internal/router` suite green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A16, A27; ADR-022; #178, #223; router item 20260714-210322; Changelog: Unreleased
- **`sirsi launchd refresh` — LWCR crash-loops after a binary re-sign get a durable deploy-path fix** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-16; claude-home router item 20260716-150734, P0). Every deploy re-signs the sirsi binary (the AMFI cdhash cache demands it — rm+cp+codesign), but launchd pins each job's Lightweight Code Requirement at bootstrap time, so after a re-sign every ai.sirsi.* job that runs the binary is SIGKILLed with "Launch Constraint Violation" in a crash-loop: `ai.sirsi.conduit.tick` hit **531 crash-loops** (OS_REASON_CODESIGNING) and `ai.sirsi.router.wake.claude-pantheon` throttled at 32 before claude-home's sweep healed them by hand. `kickstart -k` does NOT clear a stale LWCR (same constraint → same kill); only bootout + bootstrap re-derives it from the binary as currently signed. **Fix**: new `setup.RefreshSirsiLaunchAgents` (`internal/setup/launchdrefresh.go`) boots out and re-bootstraps every `~/Library/LaunchAgents/ai.sirsi.*.plist` job (`gui/<uid>` domain; `.bak`/`.quarantined` copies never match; bootout tolerated when the job wasn't loaded, a bootstrap failure is reported per-label), exposed as **`sirsi launchd refresh`** and wired into **`make install`** so the deploy path that CAUSES the stale pin now clears it in the same breath — the new binary re-derives its own constraints. Table-driven tests: bootout-then-bootstrap ordering per job, backup/quarantine/non-sirsi exclusion, bootout-failure tolerated, bootstrap-failure reported, empty-dir no-op. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A16; router item 20260716-150734; [[reference_macos_amfi_cp_sigkill]]; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Menubar panel yields focus and reopens fresh — no more permanent floating window with days-stale screens** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-16; owner-reported live QA defect). Two panel-behavior bugs compounded into "the RTK screen still shows the tutorial": the `NSPanel` (`level = .floating`, ADR-030) had **no focus-loss handling** — once opened it floated over every other window until manually toggled — and a drilled-in `ResultView` loads its command output once (`.task`) and lives forever in the custom `Nav` stack, so a screen opened days ago kept rendering output from a since-replaced `sirsi` binary. (The RTK savings fix #221 was live in the CLI the whole time; the panel was showing pre-install output. Deploy-gap lesson recorded: "verified live" must mean the owner's installed app, not the CLI + snapshot harness.) **Fix**: (1) the previously-declared-but-unused `panelObserver` now observes `NSWindow.didResignKeyNotification` → `orderOut` — click anywhere else and the panel gets out of the way like every other menubar surface; attached sheets (Apply confirmations) are guarded so the panel never hides beneath its own dialog. (2) New `SirsiEngine.reopenTick` bumps on every open; `RootView` pops navigation to Home on change, so every open starts fresh and no screen can show stale command output again — the class of bug, not the instance. Panel stays movable/resizable with frame autosave (owner, 2026-07-09) while visible. Refs: ADR-030; PANTHEON_RULES.md A10; #221; [[feedback_verify_in_browser_not_tests]]; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Reaper scopes thread liveness by stable machine id, not hostname — root cause of the 1d16h stranded inbox** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-16; claude-home diagnosis, router item 20260715-143922). `router.ReapDeadThreads` retires an `active` thread record whose recorded PID is dead by OS truth (ADR-022) — but it scoped that OS-truth check with a hostname-equality guard (`t.Host != host`, `host = os.Hostname()`). A hostname is **mutable**: this laptop reports three names across networks — `Mac.lan`, `MacBook-Pro-2.local`, `Mac.hsd1.md.comcast.net` — all one machine. Records written under a prior name were treated as a *foreign host's process table* and **never reaped**, so dead worker/codex PIDs sat `active` and the router believed a live consumer was reading items no one could read; nine real items (autonomous-mode gate, GateAction wiring, P-heartbeat-owner, reconcile loop-dead, registry-police, a gemma deliverable) aged 1d16h. Worse than reported: because `os.Hostname()` is non-empty and most records' `Host` didn't match it, the guard skipped **58 of 62** active records — the reaper was near-inert. The guard's intent is right (only judge a PID against its *own* machine's process table), but hostname equality is the wrong primitive. **Fix**: key on a stable machine identity — new `internal/router/machineid.go` `MachineID()` (macOS `IOPlatformUUID`, Linux `/etc/machine-id`; `""` when unavailable so an absent id never causes a false match; injectable per A16) + `SameMachine(record, this)`: a **different** id is provably foreign (skip); an **id-less legacy** record is local (the registry is per-machine runtime state, never git-tracked, never shared), which un-breaks the strand across every past hostname at once. `Thread.MachineID` is stamped at registration (mint + reuse-backfill); the vestigial `host` param is dropped from `ReapDeadThreads` (3 prod + 5 test call sites) rather than left as a lying signature; hostname is now cosmetic. **Verified live on the real registry**: 62 active / 58 dead-PID → after one `node-status` pass, **0** dead-PID actives, 84 reaped, 4 genuinely-live threads remain. Tests: foreign-machine-untouched, stale-hostname-still-reaped (the direct regression), `SameMachine` truth table, machine-id injection, register-stamps-id; the nodestatus stale-thread test now uses a live-PID/old-heartbeat thread (a stale *phantom* PID is correctly reaped under the #29 policy the old host guard was masking). `go build ./...` + `go test ./internal/router -race` + dashboard green; gofmt + golangci-lint(0) clean on touched packages. Refs: ADR-022; PANTHEON_RULES.md A16, A27; claude-home item 20260715-143922; Changelog: Unreleased
- **RTK — Output Filter reports what it actually saved, instead of teaching CLI syntax** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-15; owner-reported live QA defect). The menubar's "RTK — Output Filter" screen was a dead end: a tutorial card explaining that RTK "is a pipe filter… Pipe something through it to measure savings", two lines of copyable shell syntax, and a Refresh button that re-ran a command whose output could never change. It told the operator to leave the GUI and go type in a terminal — a screen that cannot act, which is exactly what [[feedback_surfaces_current_actionable_only]] forbids. The card was the previous fix (#180) for a worse bug (no-stdin `rtk stats` "measured" an empty string and dumped nonsense), and it traded a wrong screen for a dead one. **Root cause: the savings were real and being thrown away.** `sirsi rtk stats` is stdin-only and the popover shells it with no stdin, so the screen had nothing to show — but `internal/agentguard` applies the RTK filter to the output of *every guarded command an agent runs* (`SafeRun`), already computes `OriginalBytes`/`FilteredBytes`, and inscribed **nothing**. The one path that did inscribe (`internal/mcp.handleFilterOutput`, the opt-in `filter_output` MCP tool) had never been called: the live 171 MB Stele held 600,520 entries and **zero** `rtk_filter` records. So the deity built to prove context economy was the only one with no evidence. **(1)** `agentguard.SafeRun` now inscribes each filter result to the Stele (`stele.TypeRTKFilter` — a type constant that already existed and nothing wrote), using the same field names as the MCP handler so one aggregate reads both paths. **(2)** New `stele.TailByType`/`TailByTypeFrom` reads a bounded window from the tail of the append-only ledger — a full scan of an unbounded 171 MB file on every popover open is not acceptable, and a `stele.Reader` would consume the offset a real consumer depends on; the path-taking variant satisfies Rule A16 and a torn or future-schema line is skipped rather than fatal. **(3)** The no-stdin branch of `rtk stats` now renders measured savings ("Sirsi trimmed 61.5 KB of noise out of 285.7 KB of tool output — 22% less for the AI to read") with outputs-filtered and duplicate-lines-removed as evidence; an unreadable ledger warns honestly and a filter that has not run yet says so in plain English rather than reporting a false zero. The piped path (`some-command | sirsi rtk stats`) is unchanged. Verified end-to-end on the live ledger: 0 `rtk_filter` entries → three `sirsi agent safe-run` invocations → 3 entries → the card renders real measured bytes. Table-driven tests cover type filtering, mid-file-seek partial-line discard, over-large windows, corrupt-line tolerance, the empty result, and the missing-ledger error. gofmt/vet/golangci-lint(0)/`go test ./internal/stele/ ./internal/agentguard/` green. Note for the record: task #18 "Walk RTK — Output Filter" was closed against the tutorial card — a walked screen that ends in instructions is not a walked screen. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md §0 (RTK ⚡ The Sieve), A10, A16; #180; [[feedback_surfaces_current_actionable_only]], [[feedback_plain_english_gui]], [[feedback_context_economy]]; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Memory-hog finding gets its relieve lever — and the ADR-033 enforcement list can't rot** (claude-pantheon MEMHOG-FIX lane, 2026-07-02; owner-reported live QA defect). The menubar's "Top Memory Consumers" screen was a dead end: a YELLOW "Memory hog detected: Python at 6.2 GB" whose footer claimed "Informational — no one-click fix for this signal", with the hog list rendered as one tiny caption-monospaced pipe-string. **(Go, the lever)** `remediationCommand`/`remediationKind` keyed the memory mapping on the phantom name "Memory Processes" — the doctor's PROGRESS label — while the finding is emitted as "Top Memory Consumers", so the warn-level hog never matched and shipped alarm-without-action (the exact ADR-033 violation `sirsi relieve --memory`, the #124 lever, exists to fix). Warn+ now maps to `sirsi relieve --memory` (fixKind `relief`); the healthy severity-0 reading stays info with no fix. **(Go, the meta-hole)** The P4 enforcement tests (#128) were merged into the `fix/real-memory-remediation` branch AFTER #124's squash to main, so they never landed on main at all — and their hand-maintained `allFindingChecks` list contained both phantom names and was missing "Top Memory Consumers". The enforcement suite now lands on main with the list DERIVED from the doctor itself: a new `doctorChecks` registry (progress label + declared `emits` + runner) is the single table `DoctorWithOpts` runs, `FindingChecks()` derives the catalog from it (+ `binary-drift` from the external emitters list), and `TestFindingChecksMatchEmittedFindings` runs the real doctor against the mock platform to prove every emitted finding is registered — a new check cannot ship without declaring its remediation contract, and stale exemptions fail loudly. `TestRemediationNeverAMonitor` + `TestEveryAlarmingCheckHasLeverOrGuidance` pass WITH "Top Memory Consumers" included (it has a lever; no exemption). **(Swift, legibility + honesty)** `FindingView` now parses pipe-separated details ("Python (6.2 GB) | node (1.1 GB) | …") into one readable row per process — name in `.callout`, size right-aligned `.callout.monospaced` (handles parenthesised process names like "Claude Helper (Renderer)") — and prose details wrap at `.callout`, never `caption2` ([[feedback_live_data_not_greyed]], legibility rule). Footer honesty: warn/critical with a fix keeps the Fix button; warn/critical WITHOUT a fix now says "This needs attention but has no one-click fix yet." — a surface never again calls an alarm "Informational"; only severity<2 reads "Informational." BackBar + Fix-button flow unchanged. `go test ./internal/guard -race` green, gofmt/vet/golangci-lint clean, swift build green. Refs: ADR-033, #124, #128, [[feedback_surfaces_current_actionable_only]]; PANTHEON_RULES.md A10, A16; Changelog: Unreleased
- **TUI P2 polish: viewport windowing, error-path golden test, mid-op quit guard (review 20260702-030841 P2#6/#7/#8)** (claude-pantheon TUI-P2 lane, 2026-07-02). Three deferred P2 findings from claude-home's PR #142 review, closed. **(P2#6)** `renderTable` appended every row, so on lists taller than the viewport the cursor scrolled below the frame — the operator could select rows they could not see. New `renderTableWindow` gives every selectable screen (Waste/Ghosts/Health/Activity) an exact line budget (each screen builds its tail chrome first): the scroll window follows the selection (top/bottom windows one-sided, middle centered) and clipped rows are declared by honest "↑ N more" / "↓ N more" TokDim indicator lines (ASCII `^`/`v` fallback; two new closed-set glyphs), never silently dropped. The window is a pure function of selection+budget recomputed per render, so `tea.WindowSizeMsg` resize is safe by construction; an exhaustive invariant test (`TestTableWindowInvariants`, ~50K cases) pins selection-always-visible, honest counts, and exact budget use, plus golden frames at 100x30 and post-shrink 80x24 (`TestViewportKeepsSelectionVisible`, `TestViewportResizeSafe`). **(P2#7)** No test drove the injected runner's ERROR path; `TestGoldenErrorStateEveryScreen` now fails every screen's load through a `failRunner` and asserts the golden error frame (BLOCK banner with the reason + "press u to retry"), never spinner-forever or a panic, and `TestErrorStateRetryRecovers` proves the retry hint is honest (u after recovery reaches ready). **(P2#8)** `App.quitting` was set but never read, and `q` quit instantly mid-operation. The dead field is replaced by a wired two-step guard (`quitArmed`): `q` while the active screen is busy (new `Screen.Busy()` — scan loading or a dispatched clean/fix/relieve unresolved) raises a WARN toast "operation running — q again to quit, esc to stay"; a second `q` quits, `esc` stays (consumed), any other key disarms and runs normally, the arm expires with its toast (never a silent later quit), and `ctrl+c` stays immediate. Idle `q` still quits instantly. Also fixed in passing: the Activity footer advertised the pre-P1#2 "r refresh" binding (r is relieve — a dead hint); it now reads "u update". Non-TTY `sirsi` help verified byte-identical against origin/main binaries. `go test ./internal/tui/... -race` green; gofmt/vet/golangci-lint clean. Refs: ADR-020; review 20260702-030841 P2; PANTHEON_RULES.md A1, A10, A16, A21; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Dashboard data honesty: real RAM stats, node-status wired, no dead-end Ra tab, centered layout** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-01). GET /api/stats served `total_ram`/`used_ram`/`free_ram` as live zeros next to a real `ram_percent` — the RAM fields are now derived from the guard NodeCapacity self-model (ADR-031-B, the same real-system source the menubar uses since PR #123), with all four fields consistent from ONE sample and an honest legacy fallback (percent only, never fabricated totals) when hardware detection fails. `sirsi dashboard` never wired `Config.NodeStatusFn`, so GET /api/node-status returned an error blob in the shipped binary — it now wires the SAME `router.CollectNodeStatus` collector `sirsi router node-status` uses (ADR-026: one read-model, no re-aggregation). The Ra sidebar tab dead-ended on fetches to a backend that doesn't exist — it now renders a plain-info "Fleet orchestration — coming with the Ra backend" panel (no alarm styling: nothing there is user-fixable, so nothing may alarm). The dashboard shell gains a centered 1400px max-width container so ultra-wide viewports don't strand content top-left; the RAM stat also shows real used/total bytes and drops a literal `%%` that reached the browser. Unit-tested via an injectable capacity sampler + contract round-trip. Refs: ADR-026, ADR-031-B; data-honesty canon ("live data never renders zeros/greyed"); PANTHEON_RULES.md A10, A16/A21.
- **Real disk-reclaim lever: `sirsi reclaim-snapshots` (ADR-033 P2)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-30). New action that thins local Time Machine (APFS) snapshots to free disk — the biggest painless disk win when space is low. NAMES the count + shows disk free, then `tmutil thinlocalsnapshots` via macOS admin-auth (password to the OS, never sirsi), with **before/after disk-free proof**. Only `com.apple.TimeMachine.*` snapshots (reclaimable) — non-purgeable `os.update` snapshots and external/network backups are untouched. New `Local Snapshots` doctor finding surfaces reclaimable snapshots as actionable **INFO** (never an alarm — snapshots are useful) carrying the one-click reclaim; absent when there are none. Shared `adminShell` helper (osascript admin-auth) + `diskFreeBytes` (statfs) that the remaining ADR-033 levers reuse. Verified live (preview + honest no-op) + unit-tested. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1, A10, A11; ADR-033; Changelog: Unreleased
- **7-day-trend findings no longer show a present-tense alarm on a GREEN dot** (claude-pantheon, 2026-07-01; owner-flagged). Jetsam/App-Hangs/Kernel-Panic "(7d)" findings are demoted to green Info (history), but their message kept the pre-demotion alarm text — a green "Jetsam Events" row literally read "sustained trend, system under RAM pressure" while RAM was healthy, and App Hangs read "sustained main-thread saturation," contradicting "ALL SYSTEMS HEALTHY". The dynamic counts were correct; the CHARACTERIZATION was a static present-tense claim. Reworded the `isTrend` messages to read as the history they are: "N … on M of the last 7 days — a 7-day record (<cause> at the time); not happening now." Message now matches the green status. Tests updated. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10, [[feedback_surfaces_current_actionable_only]]; Changelog: Unreleased
- **SURFACES HONESTY: red "Swap Usage — system is thrashing" + the bogus "RAM: 2.8 MB" stat fixed at the source** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-30; owner-reported disconnect). (1) `checkSwapUsage` alarmed on swap BYTES (>12 GB -> Critical "thrashing") ignoring current memory pressure; macOS keeps swap allocated after use, so 12.7 GB swap with normal pressure is residue, not thrashing. New `gateSwapOnPressure` caps the Swap alarm at the live RAM Pressure severity + clears the relief/7-day banner when there is no live problem (verified live: severity 3->0, score->100 green). (2) `guard.GetStats` reported `runtime.MemStats.Alloc` (the CLI Go heap, ~2.8 MB) as RAM usage with PressureLevel hardcoded "Normal"; now real system RAM + live pressure via `SampleNodeCapacity` (verified live: "RAM: 2.8 MB" -> "RAM: 13.0 GB / 48.0 GB"). `go test -race ./internal/guard` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10; Changelog: Unreleased
- **`cleaner.CleanFile` now has a `dryRun` preview (Rule A1 parity)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-30; full-repo-audit P1). `CleanFile` — the decision-logging deletion path — unconditionally trashed/deleted, unlike `DeleteFile`/`DeleteFileReversible` which take `dryRun`, contradicting its own "no auto-delete" contract and Rule A1 ("every destructive op has a no-op preview"). Added a `dryRun bool` param: when set, it reports the size, does NOT touch the file, and records a `"dry-run"` decision (never a `"trash"`/`"delete"`, so the ledger cannot claim a removal that did not happen). Migrated the 6 existing call sites (all tests) + a new test asserting dry-run previews without removing. `go test ./internal/cleaner` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Isis watchdog `AutoRenice` opt-in actually fires now (was dead code), via an A21-safe seam** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-30; full-repo-audit P1). The auto-renice block was gated on `hotStreak[p.PID] == 0`, but it sits INSIDE the `hotStreak >= SustainCount` branch — so the streak was never 0 there and the opt-in never ran. Replaced the dead guard with a `reniced` set that fires once per hot streak (cleared when the process cools, so a re-spike renices again). Because the watch loop runs renice from a goroutine, added an A21-safe `get/setReniceByPIDFn` seam (mutex accessors) and the loop captures the fn synchronously so the goroutine never reads the global. New test drives a sustained hot process and asserts auto-renice fires exactly once for the right PID; `go test -race ./internal/guard` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1, A21; Changelog: Unreleased
- **`platform.Kill` (macOS) now SIGTERMs first and waits before SIGKILL, instead of both back-to-back** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-30; full-repo-audit P1). `Darwin.Kill` sent `kill -15` immediately followed by `proc.Kill()` (SIGKILL), defeating the "SIGTERM first" contract and risking data loss in a slayed editor/tool. It now sends SIGTERM, polls liveness (signal 0) up to a bounded `killGrace` (2s), and only SIGKILLs if the process is still alive. Safe because `platform.Kill`s sole caller is `SlayWith` (user-facing slay) — the Hapi emergency OOM path uses a direct syscall (`hapiKill`), not this, so the grace never slows an emergency kill. 2 tests: a normal process terminates; a SIGTERM-ignoring process is force-killed only after the grace (proving the window is bounded). `go test ./internal/platform` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Updater failure-path test coverage + placebo removal** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-30; full-repo-audit P2). `fetchNewestRelease` — the network entry point for the whole update flow — had its failure branches untested; added httptest cases for non-200, empty release list, and malformed JSON. Replaced the placebo `TestCheck_DevVersion` (which only `t.Log`-ged "skipped" and asserted nothing) with `TestCheck_DevVersionNeverUpdates`, proving a `dev` build never reports an update even when the server advertises a newer release. Added `TestExtractSirsiBinary_NeutralizesPathTraversal` — a tar entry named `../sirsi` lands INSIDE destDir (filepath.Base neutralizes the escape) and nothing is written to the parent, verifying the doc-comment claim. Tests only. `go test ./internal/updater` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A16; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Failure-path test coverage for the kill + trash engines (Rule A16), via `platform.Mock` error injection** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-30; full-repo-audit P1). The `Mock` always returned nil from `Kill`/`MoveToTrash`, so the safety-critical FAILURE branches were never exercised: `SlayWith`s `result.Failed++`/`Errors` (a process-kill module), `CleanFile`s "move to trash failed" path, and `DeleteFile(useTrash=true)` (untested entirely). Added `TrashErr`/`KillErr`/`KillCalls` to `Mock` (records the call, then returns the injected error — nil keeps existing behavior) + 4 tests: kill-failure aggregates into the result; a failed trash surfaces the error AND is never recorded as a successful "trash" decision; trash-delete success moves the file. `go test -race ./internal/guard` + cleaner + platform green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A16; Changelog: Unreleased
- **Canon reconciled with reality (full-repo audit, claude-pantheon 2026-06-30)** — stale-canon findings the audit confirmed: **(1)** `PANTHEON_RULES.md`/`GEMINI.md` Rule A3/A6/§5/§7 referenced a nonexistent `anubis-agent` binary + `cmd/anubis`/`cmd/anubis-agent` build targets (the code ships `sirsi`/`cmd/sirsi` + `sirsi-agent`/`cmd/sirsi-agent`, which is what CI actually builds) — the A6 pre-merge build check as written was unrunnable; renamed the binary/build-target refs (the deity name "Anubis" and the `sirsi anubis` command are untouched). **(2)** `VERSION` was `0.23.1-beta`, 7 tags behind the shipped `v0.23.8-beta` (Rule A13) → bumped. **(3)** `docs/ADR-INDEX.md` registry stopped at ADR-025 with a stale "ADR-024+ next available" row while ADR-026→032 existed on disk (incl. the cornerstone Mac-first ADR-032) — added the 7 missing rows (+ ADR-031-A/B) and corrected the count/next-available/last-updated header. **(4)** the §0 Horus row still defined Horus as only "code graph," contradicting accepted ADR-015/026 (Local Lord + ops dashboard) → rewritten. Docs only; no code. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A13, ADR-015/026/032; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Hapi governor intervention seams are now Rule-A21 race-safe** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-30; full-repo-audit P1). `internal/guard/hapi.go` exposed `killFn`/`suspendFn`/`resumeFn`/`governedFn` as raw package globals reassigned directly in tests (`hapi_test.go`) while the live `MemGovernor.Start` goroutine reads them — a latent data race (passed `-race` only because the tests are not parallel). Added `hapiFnMu` + `get*Fn`/`set*Fn` accessors mirroring the existing `hapiSampleMu`/`setHapiSampleFn` pattern; all 6 read sites (5 in hapi.go + `nodecapacity.go` SampleNodeCapacity) and the test seam-swap now go through them. (Audit also flagged `renice.go` — VERIFIED a false positive: its tests use the `reniceWith(...)` arg-injection path and never touch the globals.) `go test -race ./internal/guard` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A21; Changelog: Unreleased
- **SECURITY: `sirsi update --cli` now verifies the downloaded binary's SHA-256 before installing, and the cleaner is symlink-safe** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-30; full-repo-audit P0s, owner directive). Two confirmed security findings: **(1)** the CLI self-update path (`cmd/sirsi/update.go` → `internal/selfupdate.SafeReplace`) downloaded the release tarball and replaced the running binary with **zero authenticity check** — `SafeReplace` only ad-hoc `codesign --force --sign -`'s it, which authenticates nothing (the Developer-ID/notarization property belongs ONLY to the `--app` DMG path). A poisoned release asset or MITM = arbitrary code execution. Now `installCLIRelease` downloads the release `checksums.txt`, verifies the tarball's SHA-256 via new `updater.VerifyChecksum`/`ChecksumsAsset`, and **fails closed** if the manifest is absent or the digest mismatches — before extraction. (A signed manifest with a binary-pinned pubkey, for full release-compromise resistance, is a tracked follow-up.) **(2)** `internal/updater.Download` streamed `io.Copy` with **no size limit** → disk-exhaustion surface; now bounded by `maxDownloadSize` (1 GB), erroring with no partial file on overflow. **(3)** `internal/cleaner.ValidatePath` resolved only `filepath.Abs`, never `EvalSymlinks` — a symlink with an innocent name (or a symlinked parent dir) pointing into a protected location bypassed the Rule A1 protected-path guarantee; it now checks BOTH the lexical path and the symlink-resolved real target (leaf + parent). 6 new tests (checksum match/mismatch/fail-closed; download cap + partial-file cleanup; symlink-leaf + symlinked-parent + plain-path). `go build/vet/test` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1 (Safety First), A11 (no telemetry), ADR-032; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **The native menubar's Anubis clean-result screen no longer dead-ends — after a clean you can return** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-29). The SwiftUI popover (`macapp/`, ADR-030) hosts every screen in a `NavigationStack`, but an `NSPopover` has no toolbar, so the native back button is invisible — which is exactly why `BackBar` (an in-content `‹ Back` calling `@Environment(\.dismiss)`) is documented as mandatory at the top of every pushed view. `CleanReviewView` was the ONE pushed view missing it: after "Move N items to Trash" completed, the result state rendered only the green ✓ + "Moved to Trash — recoverable until you empty it." with no back control and no Done button, trapping the user ("no progression, no return" — owner, 2026-06-29). Added the mandatory `BackBar(title: "Review & Clean")` at the top of the view (now 9/9 pushed views covered) plus an explicit prominent **Done** button in the result state that dismisses back to Anubis. `swift build -c release` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10 (UI fidelity), ADR-030 (native SwiftUI surface), [[feedback_surfaces_current_actionable_only]] (zero dead-ends); Changelog: Unreleased.

### Added
- **Surface JSON contracts — `sirsi vitals`, `clean --yes`, structured `ghosts --json`, `sirsi activity` (TUI design proof gaps V1–V4)** (claude-pantheon CLI-CONTRACTS lane, 2026-07-01). The four CLI contracts every interactive surface (TUI, menubar, dashboard) needs, built as plain CLI additions — no `internal/tui` code (ADR-020 gate pending). **(V1) `sirsi vitals [--json] [--top N]`** — fast memory snapshot: total/used/free RAM, live pressure + source (same kernel-dispatch→cache→bootstrap chain as `SampleNodeCapacity`), swap used (the doctor's own `vm.swapusage` probe, extracted as `guard.SwapUsedBytes`), top memory hogs `{name,pid,rss_bytes}` from `guard.SampleMemory`. No new probing — existing guard internals exposed; and the Hapi sampler's TotalRAM now uses the extracted `seba.TotalRAMBytes()` (same `hw.memsize` sysctl) instead of full `DetectHardware`, dropping the ~600ms `system_profiler` GPU detour a memory sample never needed → `vitals --json` runs in ~30ms warm (<100ms budget); human render is the gold lipgloss dashboard+table. **(V2) `sirsi clean --yes`** — with `--confirm`, skips ONLY the interactive `[y/N]` stdin prompt (which would deadlock a subprocess dispatcher that rendered its own confirmation); pure `decideCleanAction` gate proves `--yes` without `--confirm` STAYS a dry-run and scope stays a function of severity + `--include-caution` alone (Rule A1 untouched — `selectCleanTargets` has no yes/confirm parameter). **(V3) `sirsi ghosts --json`** now emits the structured report `{ghosts[]{app_name,bundle_id,total_size_bytes,total_files,in_launch_services,residuals[]{path,type,size_bytes,file_count}}, total_waste_bytes,…}` (size-descending, arrays never null) instead of CommandResult display strings — field names match the dashboard's `/api/ghosts` shape; follows the `scan --json` raw-payload convention; verified no consumer parsed the old `ghosts --json` (menubar/macapp run plain `ghosts`). **(V4) `sirsi activity [--json] [--limit N]`** — reads the operations ledger (`~/Library/Logs/sirsi/operations.log`) newest-first as `{time,action,target,bytes,source}`; `internal/oplog` gains `Path()/ParseLine()/ReadLast()` — the ONE parser of the free-text line format (writer and reader share `Path()`), so no surface ever parses log lines privately. Injectable providers + mutexes for all system reads (A16/A21), no `t.Parallel()` in global-swapping tests (lessons #129/#131); 13 new tests incl. end-to-end `runActivity` against a fixture ledger; verified live with the built binary. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md Rule 0, A1, A16, A21; ADR-020 (docs/TUI_DESIGN_PROOF.md §5 gap list), ADR-033; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`sirsi thread register` now records a session→agent marker so the PRD-keepalive Stop hook can find a CCD session's agent** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-21; thread-liveness guarantee piece 1, claude-home 20260621-015502). The Stop hook that blocks an agent from going idle while its PRD has open work resolves the session's agent via `$SIRSI_AGENT_ID`, then a session→agent marker, then a cwd match. CCD sessions run with `cwd=$HOME`, so the cwd match can't tell them apart — the marker is what lets the hook fire for them. `thread register` now writes `~/.claude/run/agent-by-session/<$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID> = <agent_id>` and `thread close` removes it (both best-effort — a marker failure never fails register/close). New `internal/router/sessionmarker.go` (Write/Read/Remove + session-id sanitization that blocks path traversal), injectable marker dir for tests. 3 tests (write→read→remove + idempotent remove; empty inputs are a no-op; crafted `../` session id stays a flat in-dir file). `go test ./internal/router` + gofmt + golangci-lint(0) green. Piece 2 (`sirsi supervise` LaunchAgent backstop) follows. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27, claude-home 20260621-015502; Changelog: Unreleased.

### Fixed
- **The menubar/`sirsi surface` no longer reports Claude Code + Codex as "not installed" while showing GUI IDEs installed — the diabolical inversion where Pantheon couldn't see the agents building it** (claude-home, 2026-06-22). `internal/workstream/launcher.go` decided "installed" via bare `exec.LookPath`, which trusts `$PATH`. The menubar runs under launchd with a MINIMAL PATH (`/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`) that omits `~/.local/bin` — exactly where the `claude` and `codex` CLIs install — so they read as not-installed while VS Code (found via its `/Applications` bundle) read as installed. New `resolveBinary` searches an AUGMENTED candidate path: the inherited PATH, the user's login-shell PATH (`$SHELL -lc 'printf %s "$PATH"'`), and the common user-bin dirs a truncated launchd PATH drops (`~/.local/bin`, `/opt/homebrew/bin`, `~/.cargo/bin`, …). Every `Installed()`/`Launch()` check now uses it; GUI IDE launchers additionally accept an `/Applications/*.app` bundle. Detection is now independent of the launchd-truncated PATH. Regression test injects a PATH that excludes the tool's dir and asserts the augmented resolver still finds it. `go test ./internal/workstream` + vet + golangci-lint(0) + gofmt green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A25 (deity registry/surface), ADR-032 (Mac-first), claude-home realm-sweep finding; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`node-status` wake-readiness now matches what the wake pass actually does — the surface and the action can no longer disagree** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-20; PR #89 follow-up, claude-home-acked). `CollectNodeStatus` built `wake_health` from a PERMISSIVE inline view (`cfg.WakeMechanism()`): a bare `Command` array read as "cli-spawn ready" and an unwired `mcp-notification` read as ready — but the wake pass (`WakePass`/`router doctor`) uses the HONEST `ProbeWakeReadiness`, which refuses to blind-spawn a legacy-command agent and reports mcp as not-yet-wired. So node-status could show an agent "ready" that the wake pass declares `wake-unavailable`. Replaced the 21-line permissive block with a single `ProbeWakeReadiness(cfg)` call (Rule 0) so one readiness view feeds both the surface and the action: a legacy command-only agent now shows not-ready with no explicit mechanism, and an unwired mcp agent shows not-ready. Test updated + extended (legacy-command agent asserts not-ready). `go test ./internal/router` + gofmt + vet + golangci-lint(0) green; full build green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27, #89 (the deferred scope-boundary follow-up claude-home OK'd), [[feedback_liveness_proof_surface_native]]; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **The release no longer burns 6 hours on a wedged DMG signing step, and a pantheon release no longer downgrades or 404s the `thoth-init` npm package** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-20; assigned by claude-home after sweeping the hung v0.23.2-beta run 27873488895). Two release-pipeline robustness fixes: **(1) DMG job timeout** — `release.yml`'s `menubar` (macOS DMG) job had NO `timeout-minutes`, so when the signing path wedged (the owner-gated Developer-ID cert / `KEYCHAIN_PWD` / app-specific-password secrets are not yet valid, so `security import`/`codesign`/`notarytool --wait` hang), it ran to the **6h GitHub-Actions ceiling** before being canceled — burning 5h20m of CI. Added `timeout-minutes: 30` to the job + `--timeout 20m` to the `notarytool submit --wait` in `scripts/build-dmg.sh` (defense-in-depth) so a hung signing step **fails fast**. Pure robustness, no Apple dependency. **(2) npm version decouple** — the `publish-npm` job overrode `thoth-init`'s `package.json` version with the pantheon release tag (`npm version 0.23.2-beta`), which would **DOWNGRADE** the npm `latest` tag (`thoth-init` is independently at 2.0.0) and 404'd every release on a PUT to the existing package. Removed the tag-version override (the two `Extract version`/`Update package version` steps); the job now publishes ONLY the version already in `tools/thoth-init/package.json` and **skips when that version is already on npm** (`npm view` guard) — so an unchanged `thoth-init` is a green no-op, never a downgrade and never a failed release. A genuinely-new `thoth-init` version still needs the owner-gated npm **Trusted Publisher** (or an `NPM_TOKEN`) configured to actually publish; that and the 7 Developer-ID signing secrets (`docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md`) remain owner actions. Apple Developer Program is ACTIVE (Team 9D382WV988) — the DMG hang is the CI signing-secrets path, not an Apple gate. `python3 -c yaml.safe_load` valid + `bash -n scripts/build-dmg.sh` clean. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A13 (release), ADR-032 (Mac-first), docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md, PR #42 (signing infra), claude-home item 20260620-193953; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Health can finally show GREEN — the at-a-glance light is no longer perpetually amber on a healthy machine** (claude-home, 2026-06-19). Two bad standards pinned every real dev machine at yellow: (1) the 7-day TREND checks (Jetsam, kernel panics, app hangs) emitted `Warn` on a SINGLE historical event, so one Jetsam kill 6 days ago kept the machine amber for a week; (2) Swap warned at just 1 GB used, but macOS pages idle memory to swap proactively and a busy machine (browsers + multiple AI agents) routinely sits at a few GB while perfectly healthy. New explicit GREEN STANDARD: a handful of ISOLATED events (`<= transientEventTolerance` = 3, not a multi-day trend) is normal background → `SeverityOK`/`Info` (green); only an elevated count → `Warn` (amber); a sustained multi-day trend → `Critical` (amber, never red — patterns aren't act-now). Swap: `< 4 GB` routine → green, `4–12 GB` → warn, `> 12 GB` → critical thrashing. Live-critical RAM/disk still force red; the standard is documented at `transientEventTolerance` + `checkSwapUsage`. New `TestClassifyHealth_GreenIsReachable` proves the contract (isolated background events + routine swap → green; sustained trend → amber; live RAM-critical → red). `go test ./internal/guard` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10 (UI fidelity), the green/amber/red rubric (#50/#51), owner report "it's always yellow, we need standards"; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **The release builds Mac-first (darwin + linux) — Windows dropped because the CLI doesn't cross-compile to it** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-19). After the `/bin/zsh` test fix (#82), the first signed/notarized release reached GoReleaser but FAILED building the **Windows** target: `cmd/sirsi` uses POSIX-only syscalls — the Hapi governor's `syscall.Kill`/`SIGSTOP`/`SIGCONT` (#63) and gemma's `syscall.Flock`/`SysProcAttr.Setpgid` — which are `undefined: syscall.*` on Windows; macOS-only CI (#64) never caught it, the release cross-compile is the first Windows build. Per ADR-032 (Mac-first; Win/Linux deferred), dropped `windows` from both `.goreleaser.yaml` builds (darwin + linux only) so GoReleaser succeeds and the macOS sign+notarize DMG job (`needs: release`) runs (the `windows-installer` job was already gated off behind `ENABLE_WINDOWS_BUILD`). As a down-payment for when Windows ships, split the Hapi POSIX-signal primitives into `hapi_signals_unix.go` (`//go:build unix`) + a Windows stub (`hapi_signals_windows.go` — teeth report unsupported; Windows has no SIGSTOP), so `internal/guard` now cross-compiles to Windows (gemma's syscalls remain to be tagged when `ENABLE_WINDOWS_BUILD` is set). Removed the dead Windows row from the release-notes template. darwin+linux builds + `go test ./internal/guard` + vet + golangci-lint(0) green. Unblocks the first **signed + notarized** release. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A3/A13, ADR-032 (Mac-first), #82; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **The release pipeline is unblocked — a LaunchAgent test hard-coded `/bin/zsh`, which the Linux release runner lacks** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-19). `TestCollectLaunchAgentsInventoriesKnownHelpers` wrote a test plist with `Program=/bin/zsh` and asserted `ProgramFound`, but the `release.yml` goreleaser job runs `go test` on **ubuntu-latest**, where `/bin/zsh` doesn't exist → `ProgramFound:false` → the whole release aborts at the test step and the macOS sign/notarize DMG job is skipped (the reason the v0.23.0/v0.23.1-beta release runs failed — never reached signing). Switched the fixture to `/bin/sh`, which exists on both macOS and the Linux runner, so the test validates the same inventory logic everywhere. `go test ./internal/router` green. Unblocks the first signed + notarized release (Developer ID, Team 9D382WV988). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A6/A13, ADR-032 (Mac-first), #42 (signing pipeline); Changelog: Unreleased.
- **The menubar "waste" title no longer counts protected AI model weights — the "76 GB waste" false alarm** (claude-home, 2026-06-19). The menubar ran a full `AllRules()` scan and showed `res.TotalSize` as the waste title, which **included 67 GB of cold HuggingFace/Gemma model weights** (CategoryAI, caution-tier, which the cleaner NEVER one-click removes) → an alarming "76 GB waste" the user could not act on (real reclaimable was ~8.7 GB; `8.7 + 67 ≈ 76`). Root cause: `TotalSize` is the full inventory, not the reclaimable headline. Fix: new `ScanResult.ReclaimableSize` = sum of findings EXCLUDING warning-tier (data/config) and `CategoryAI` model weights (caution + expensive to regenerate); the menubar title now shows `ReclaimableSize`. `TotalSize` is unchanged (full inventory). AI weights stay caution-tier (existing `effectiveSeverity` design + `TestAIModelCachesAreCaution` intact) and cleaner-protected — this only stops them inflating the casual "waste" number. Regression test `TestScan_ReclaimableSizeExcludesWeightsAndWarnings` (10 GB safe + 67 GB AI-weights + 5 GB warning → TotalSize 82, ReclaimableSize 10). `go test ./internal/jackal/...` + gofmt + vet green; menubar builds. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1 (one-click surfaces only trash trivially-regenerable items), feedback_build_for_use_not_demo, the 30 GB HuggingFace catch (#33); Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`node-status` now reports truthful per-thread `armed`/`loop_state` — a heartbeat-fresh thread whose watcher loop is DEAD is no longer silently "live"** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-19, owner priority #1 router hardening, PR #1; codex-validated). The router lied: `node-status`/`thread list` judged a thread "live" on heartbeat freshness alone. `EffectiveStale` only prevents false-STALE (a live loop with a harness-gated heartbeat isn't stale); it never caught the REVERSE — a thread heartbeat-fresh but whose loop is dead read as armed when nothing watches its inbox (the owner's "claims not idle but IS idle" — exactly what happened to a claude thread this session when its watcher died). **Precursor:** replaced `dedupeWatches` with `normalizeWatches(agentID, watches)` (self FIRST, then stable-dedupe, drop blanks) on both register paths — liveness truth requires every thread to watch its own inbox (A27). **Honest liveness:** `ThreadSummary` gains `watcher_type` (= `WatcherFor().Type`), `loop_state` (`alive`/`dead`/`na`/`unknown`), `armed` (bool), `armed_reason` (`loop-alive`/`app-heartbeat-fresh`/`resident-runloop-fresh`/`loop-dead`/`heartbeat-stale`), classified by `watcher_type` (NOT a broad "process surface" bucket): **loop-monitor (Claude) ONLY** → `armed == a live pgrep -f thr-<id> loop`, so a heartbeat-fresh-but-loop-dead thread is `armed:false, loop_state:"dead"` (Claude is unique: its `/loop` is thr-id-keyed AND its heartbeat is harness-gated, so it can be fresh while the loop is dead). **app-heartbeat** (Codex), **native-runloop** (resident UI menubar/TUI/IDE), **surface-loop** (gemini/gemma/qwen), and **pull-loop** (mcp/api/webhook/worker) → `loop_state:"na"`, heartbeat freshness IS the armed proof — surface-loop/pull-loop are loop-DRIVEN heartbeats (a dead loop stops the heartbeat → stale → caught by `armed=!stale`) and their cmdlines aren't contractually `thr-id`-pgrep-able, so requiring the loop probe would FALSE-NEGATIVE healthy workers (codex-home SME verdict on #79, bound by claude-home; strengthening them is a future PR that first defines a per-watcher process-identity contract). Never false-flags the Codex fleet or a resident UI. `armed` is a SEPARATE field from `.stale` — it answers "is the declared wake mechanism present?", not "should this be called stale?" `node-status` JSON carries all four; the human table shows `loop=…` and a loud `⚠ NOT ARMED (<reason>)`. **Auto-reap (criterion 3) already holds** — `CollectNodeStatus` calls `ReapDeadThreads`, retiring any non-terminal thread whose PID is `DeadByOSTruth` (PIDGone/PIDRecycled) while preserving ADR-022 safety (never PIDAlive/PIDUnknown/suspended/terminal/remote-host) — now covered by a test. Tests (codex's 5-matrix): loop-monitor live→armed/alive; loop-monitor dead→not-armed/dead; codex app-heartbeat→armed/na; menubar native-runloop→armed/na (no inbox-worker expectation); OS-dead→auto-reaped. `go test ./internal/router` + gofmt + vet + golangci-lint(0) green. PR#2 (configured-wake-or-unavailable) + PR#3 (stranded GC + `router doctor`) follow. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27, ADR-022/ADR-024, [[feedback_liveness_proof_surface_native]], owner directive 2026-06-19; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Every router thread is now guaranteed to watch its OWN inbox, even when `--watch` omits self** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-19). Follow-up to #76 (claude-home caught it on the bind): `dedupeWatches` now takes the thread's own agent id and appends it when the declaration omits it — a `sirsi thread register --watch other-agent` that dropped self would have left the thread blind to its own inbox, violating the A27 "every thread watches its own inbox" contract. Applied on BOTH the fresh and reuse register paths; the empty-input case collapses to just `[self]`, so the old `len(Watches)==0` special-case is gone (Rule 0). Regression test: a register AND a reuse-path re-register that declare only OTHER agents both still carry self. `go test ./internal/router` + gofmt + vet + golangci-lint(0) green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27, #76; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Re-registering a router thread with new `--watch` values no longer silently drops them — the reuse path now authoritatively re-declares watches/metadata** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-19). `RegisterThread`'s idempotent `(agent_id, pid)` reuse branch updated only `LastSeenAt`/`CurrentItem` and returned — so a live agent that re-registered with MORE watches (codex-home adding `--watch claude-home`) kept its OLD watch set, and its monitor under-declared what it watches (part of the "Codex only sees conduit traffic when asked" complaint; found by codex-home, confirmed by claude-home). Fix: in the reuse branch, **REPLACE-when-non-empty** — a non-empty incoming `Watches` authoritatively re-states the live declaration (which also lets an agent NARROW its set, matching "re-register tightens the declaration"), while an empty incoming set is a bare heartbeat-style register and KEEPS the existing watches (never wiped). `Workstream`/`WakeMechanism`/`Repo` update the same way (replace-when-non-empty). Union was rejected — it accretes watches forever and can never narrow. `Watches` is now stable-de-duped on both the reuse and fresh paths (`dedupeWatches`, drops blanks). Regression test asserts: same thread_id on reuse, added watches persist, a smaller set narrows (replace not union), and an empty re-register doesn't wipe. `go test ./internal/router` + gofmt + vet + golangci-lint(0) green.
