Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27 (heartbeat/supervision truthfulness), Rule 0/15 (lean/additive), codex-home item 133134; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **The cold `sirsi gemma` path now refuses through the same NodeCapacity self-model as the warm broker — one node-derived budget, no duplicate constants** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-19). The warm broker was re-pointed onto `NodeCapacity` in #71, but the cold one-shot path (`sirsi gemma "<prompt>"`) still used its own `gemmaSafeConcurrency(...)` with a flat hardcoded `8 GB headroom` and a private `gemmaFreeRAMBytes`/`gemmaAgentReserveBytes` pair — two gates that could drift apart and that ignored the measured node (an N GB box and a 256 GB box budgeted identically). The cold path now calls `guard.SampleNodeCapacity()` and refuses unless `nc.Fits(modelBytes)` — the **same** gate, which after the option-(a) fix requires `2×model + DynamicReserve` (resident model + one model of working memory + OS/agents/margin that **scales with the box** and is cross-agent-aware). So the cold path keeps its #63 2×model conservatism while becoming node-proportional, and there is exactly one budget to reason about. Deleted the now-dead `gemmaSafeConcurrency`, `gemmaFreeRAMBytes`, and `gemmaAgentReserveBytes` (Rule 0 — the logic lives in `NodeCapacity`/`internal/guard` now); `gemmaEstimateModelBytes` stays (both paths use it). The cold path's refuse-rather-than-OOM invariant moved from `TestGemmaSafeConcurrency` into the `NodeCapacity.Fits` tests + the `TestGemmaNeverAgainInvariants` cold-path case (12 GB model on a 30 GB-free/48 GB node still refuses — the 2×model budget that the old 1×model gate would have wrongly admitted). `go test ./cmd/sirsi` + gofmt + vet + golangci-lint(0) green. Broker stays DISABLED. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1, ADR-031-B (cold-path re-point onto NodeCapacity), #71; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **The warm broker's node-derived refuse gate keeps the 2×model working-memory budget — the re-point onto NodeCapacity had silently loosened it** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-19). PR #71 re-pointed `sirsi gemma serve` onto `NodeCapacity` (ADR-031-B) but in doing so dropped the refuse threshold from ADR-031-A/#63's `2×model + headroom` (one resident model + one model of working memory) down to `1×model + reserve`, and `DynamicCap` floored at exactly `1×model` — so a launch in the ~28–32 GB window that #63 *refused* would now be ADMITTED with zero KV/working-memory headroom, hit the MLX cap, and evict immediately (host-safe via the hard cap, but too tight to actually serve). Surfaced by claude-home's source-deep binding review of #71; author's call was option **(a)**: restore the conservatism, made node-proportional, across all three `NodeCapacity` methods so the warm broker matches the cold path. `Fits(model)` now requires `2×model + DynamicReserve`; `MaxConcurrency` budgets `(1+C)×model` (one resident model + one working model per concurrent slot — the 2026-06-18 balloon: concurrency 4 grew a 12 GB model to ~64 GB → Jetsam), subtracting the resident model before dividing; `DynamicCap` floors at `2×model`. A half-fix to only `Fits` was rejected — it would have moved the over-commit into `MaxConcurrency` (a node passing `Fits` at exactly `2×model` of free-reserve would still report C=2). New maximality invariant test locks the `(1+C)` accounting (it would have caught the loosening on the 256 GB case, where the old code returned one slot too many); the `DynamicCap` floor test was updated to `2×model`. `go test ./internal/guard ./cmd/sirsi` + gofmt + vet green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1, ADR-031-A/#63, ADR-031-B (Fits refuse gate), claude-home binding review 20260619-065443; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **The menu-bar Eye of Horus is now actually visible — it was rendering invisible on dark menu bars** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-17). The owner reported "it's not running at all"; accessibility confirmed the status item existed (40×24 at its slot) but its icon was blank. Root cause: AppKit's **template-image tinting silently did not engage** for the runtime-drawn `NSImage` (across both the `drawingHandler` and `lockFocus` forms), so the Eye rendered its literal black — invisible on a dark menu bar, only faintly visible on a lighter external display. Fix: stop relying on template tinting and **draw the wedjat directly in its health colour** (`makeEye(_ color:)`: white when healthy, amber on warnings/trends, red on live-critical), `isTemplate = false`, redrawn on every health change. Verified live: the amber Eye now shows clearly in the menu bar. Refs: ADR-030, the green/amber/red rubric.
- **The menu-bar Eye of Horus is bigger, bolder, and self-tints to health** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-17). The first wedjat cut (20×16 px, 1.35 pt strokes) rendered too slight to read in the bar. Sized up to 24×19.2 with 2.6 pt strokes; and the Eye now runs `diagnose` at launch and on the 90 s refresh tick so it tints amber/red on its own — a health glyph that only colors when you click it is half-useful. (Verified live on an external display; on a crowded notched built-in display macOS may push it into the hidden overflow — a menu-bar real-estate limit, not a render bug.) Refs: ADR-030, the green/amber/red rubric.
- **App Hangs now tells the truth: real app freezes vs. background OS housekeeping** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-17). The check counted every `.hang`/`.spin`/`.cpu_resource.diag` report and labelled the lot "sustained main-thread saturation (freezes, beachballs, dropped frames)" at Critical — but on a real machine the dominant entries are `spotlightknowledged` and `fileproviderd`: **background daemons** (Spotlight indexing, cloud sync) that routinely trip CPU budgets during housekeeping. The user never saw a beachball; the finding alarmed them about a non-event. Now each report is tagged with its process and split: only **user-facing app** freezes (Chrome, your editor, the Dock…) drive the Warn/Critical "freeze" finding and the trend; **background daemons** get a calm `Info` — *"No app freezes — N background CPU-budget events from system housekeeping (indexing/sync), not your apps"* — which scores zero and offers no false one-click. When real hangs coexist with daemon noise, the daemon events are acknowledged as ignored rather than inflating the offender list. New `isBackgroundCPUDaemon` allowlist + per-event `hangEvent` scan; `go test ./internal/guard` green; verified on live host (4 real daemon reports → `Info`/green, where the old code said Critical). Refs: feedback_build_for_use_not_demo, the green/amber/red rubric (#50/#51).
- **The menu-bar icon is the Eye of Horus, not a bare colored dot** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-17). The status item set `button.title` to a text string that `titleLabel()` overwrote with a traffic-light **emoji** (`🟢`/`🟡`/`🔴`) — so the entire product showed in the macOS menu bar as a generic colored dot, unbranded and indistinguishable from any other app (and the fallback hieroglyph `𓁢` would have been a tofu box on any Mac whose menu-bar font lacks Egyptian Hieroglyphs). Replaced with the **Eye of Horus (wedjat) — the watchful protector — drawn as a vector `NSBezierPath` into a template `NSImage`**: all in code (no bundled asset), crisp on Retina, guaranteed to render, and **tinted by the health band** via `contentTintColor` — native monochrome when healthy (the icon stays quiet), amber/red only when something needs you. The reclaimable-waste figure (≥1 GB) rides beside the Eye. `swift build` green. Refs: ADR-030, the green/amber/red rubric (#50/#51).
- **The Horus "Fix it" button now tells the truth — and actually changes the status where it can** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-17). The owner caught the worst kind of dishonesty: *"when I try to fix, it never leads to a fix — it says it's fixing but the status stays the same."* Three real causes, all fixed. **(1)** The `App Crashes (7d)` remediation was `sirsi clean` — but crash reports are caution-tier, so safe `clean` never touched them and the count never dropped. Now `sirsi clean --include-caution`, which clears the crash-report backlog so the count actually falls. **(2)** Findings now carry a `FixKind` — `instant` (the command provably changes state now: crash backlog, disk, binary-drift), `relief` (eases a LIVE cause; a 7-day trend is a *historical record* that decays over clean days, it cannot drop the instant you click), or `guidance` (only bites a live issue, e.g. a Spotlight storm in progress). The menubar labels the button per kind — "Fix it" vs "Relieve the live cause" vs "How to address" — and shows an honesty banner *before* the click so a week-old pattern never wears an instant-cure costume. **(3)** After a fix actually applies, the menubar **re-runs `diagnose` and reports the REAL post-fix status** of that finding (✓ Resolved, or an honest reason it persists) instead of an unconditional "fixed." Also folds in the menubar glyph-threshold fix: the tray icon reflects overall health, surfacing waste only at ≥1 GB (a 230 KB sliver no longer paints the icon amber). `FixKind` emitted in `diagnose --json`/`anubis status`, so every surface reads one honest mapping. Verified end-to-end on a live host: `App Crashes (7d)→clean --include-caution/instant`, `Jetsam (7d)→guard/relief`, `App Hangs (7d)→spotlight-exclude/guidance`. `go test ./internal/guard` + `swift build` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1, feedback_verify_in_browser_not_tests, the green/amber/red rubric (#50/#51); Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`reniceByPID` can no longer deprioritize a critical system process or sirsi itself — A1 safety floor under the App-Hangs relief arc** (claude-home, 2026-06-17). The watchdog auto-renice (`AutoRenice`) and the planned one-click hang-relief both route through `reniceByPID`, whose only guard was `pid <= 1`. So an enabled auto-renice could lower the scheduler priority of **WindowServer, kernel_task, launchd, the session UI, coreaudiod, or sirsi itself** — starving exactly the processes whose health *is* responsiveness, making a freeze **worse**, not better (the inverse of relief; an A1 violation since a remediation must never harm a critical process). Added a **hardcoded, un-overridable protected-process allowlist** (`protectedReniceNames` / `isProtectedReniceTarget`) mirroring `internal/cleaner` safety.go's protected-path model — `reniceByPID(pid, name)` now refuses any protected name (case-insensitive substring) in addition to `pid <= 1`, and the watchdog caller threads the process name through. Refactored to an injectable core `reniceByPIDWith` (A16/A21) so the refusal is unit-tested without touching real processes. 3 tests: protected names refused (reniceFn never called), normal background process still deprioritized (+10), low-PID refused. Scope note: this is the critical floor (named system processes + self); **frontmost-app protection and full nice-restore reversibility are tracked follow-ups** (nice is non-destructive and self-resets on process exit, so the named allowlist is the genuinely A1-blocking piece). Unblocks safely wiring hang-relief to the live offender per the claude-home steer. Verified: `gofmt`/`go vet`/`golangci-lint` 0 + `go test ./internal/guard/` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A16/A21, the App-Hangs relief arc (#48), claude-home reniceByPID steer; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Release pipeline now signs + notarizes correctly — the clean-install / no-FDA-churn contract** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-10). `scripts/build-dmg.sh` had real bugs that would have shipped an unusable build: it **notarized the DMG before the DMG was created** (the `notarytool submit` ran ~40 lines before `hdiutil create`, so it operated on a nonexistent file), used a `--keychain-profile "sirsi-notarize"` that CI never creates, and built the **legacy fyne systray** rather than the native SwiftUI app. Rewritten to the correct order and a Developer-ID path: build the **native `macapp/` menu bar app when present** (fall back to fyne), sign **inside-out** (inner executables then the bundle) with the **hardened runtime** (`--options runtime`) + secure `--timestamp`, `codesign --verify --deep --strict`, **then** create the DMG, sign it, **notarize it (`xcrun notarytool submit --wait` via Apple-ID + app-specific password)**, and **staple** (`xcrun stapler staple`/`validate`). The `release.yml` step now passes the `APPLE_APP_PASSWORD` secret. New `docs/RELEASE_SIGNING.md` documents the exact secrets to add. **Why it matters:** a stable Developer ID = a stable TCC identity, so a user grants Full Disk Access **once** and `brew upgrade` keeps it — no Gatekeeper warning, no re-grant, no duplicate FDA rows (the exact mess Pantheon exists to prevent). Falls back to ad-hoc only when no identity is present, with a loud DEV-ONLY warning. Verified: `bash -n` clean, YAML valid, the script is correct against the documented secrets (the real notarization runs on the next Developer-ID-tagged release). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A13 (release), feedback_dont_churn_menubar_fda, ADR-030; Changelog: Unreleased.

### Added
- **Wake-or-declare-unavailable — a stranded inbox is now either woken or honestly marked, never silently stuck, and an interactive agent is never blind-spawned (router hardening PR #2)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-19; design bound by claude-home + codex SME). The honest-liveness work (#79/#80) and the stranded-inbox surface (#85) tell us WHICH agents have work waiting and whether they're armed; this closes the loop. On a **supervisor/doctor tick** (constraint 1 — never from `router send`), `WakePass` walks every open inbox item and, per its target agent, either (a) confirms the agent is **armed** (a non-terminal, heartbeat-FRESH thread — constraint 2, heartbeat freshness ONLY, never the loop-monitor pgrep gate, so it never widens #79/#80 to pull-loops) and leaves it, (b) wakes it via an **explicitly-configured, reachable** adapter exactly once, or (c) records `wake_status: wake-unavailable` + a reason **on the item's own frontmatter** (constraint 6 — delivery truth in the item, not a sidecar). The cli-spawn adapter is **EXPLICIT-only** (constraint 4): a legacy `Command` array — which `AgentConfig.WakeMechanism()` defaults to cli-spawn — is NOT treated as wake intent (`ExplicitWakeMechanism` reads only `Wake.Mechanism`), so a legacy agent is declared wake-unavailable rather than blind-spawned. **Interactive claude-\*** are never daemonized/blind-spawned even with explicit cli-spawn (constraint 3) — they arm their own `/loop` or route via the claude-home conduit. The wake-mechanism enum gains `launchagent` + `none` (constraint 5); `sirsi router wake-install <agent>` installs a per-agent **pull-loop LaunchAgent** (a `pull-loop` watcher armed by heartbeat freshness) whose write is **idempotent** by content (constraint 7), and re-invocation per item is bounded by `wake_attempted_at`. The invoker is injectable (A16) + mutex-guarded (A21). Wired into `sirsi router doctor`: report mode shows each stranded inbox's wake-readiness ("would wake via X" / "wake-unavailable: reason"); `--fix` runs the pass after the OS-dead reap. **Tests (codex's 6):** armed→no-op; ready-adapter→invoked-once (idempotent across two ticks); no-adapter→wake-unavailable; legacy-Command→no-blind-spawn→wake-unavailable; explicit cli-spawn ready→allowed / unready→wake-unavailable (+ claude→never-spawned); LaunchAgent install idempotent. **codex SME impl-review (FAIL→PASS hardening) addressed 4 real runtime findings before merge:** (1) the LaunchAgent plist called a non-existent `thread heartbeat --agent` flag → replaced with a real `sirsi router wake-loop <agent>` verb — a foreground bounded pull-loop (A27) that registers a concrete worker thread + heartbeats with a stable pid (not OS-reaped between beats), KeepAlive-managed by launchd; (2) cli-spawn `cmd.Start()` left an unreaped child → now detaches (Setsid) + `Process.Release()`, matching the established `cmd/sirsi` router-event spawn pattern; (3) `mcp-notification` readiness claimed "ready" while its invoker always failed → `ProbeWakeReadiness` now reports NOT-ready until an MCP adapter is wired (report view and acted-on pass agree); (4) the plist was shell-built (`/bin/sh -c`) with unquoted interpolation → now a DIRECT argv (no shell) with XML-escaped bin path + agent id (spaces/metacharacters can't break or inject). 4 added tests (plist-renders-real-argv, wake-loop registers+closes, cli-spawn releases, mcp not-ready). `go test ./internal/router ./internal/work -race` + gofmt + vet + golangci-lint(0) green; full suite green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A26/A27, ADR-022/ADR-024, [[feedback_liveness_proof_surface_native]], #79/#80/#85/#86, claude-home bind 20260619-160554, codex SME impl-review 20260619-223139; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`sirsi router doctor` — the router's self-check (router hardening PR #3)** (claude-home, 2026-06-19). Aggregates the contract violations the honest-liveness work (#79/#80) and the stranded-inbox surface (#85) expose into ONE report: live threads that claim live but whose watcher loop is dead (`armed:false`), inboxes with open items but no armed watcher (stranded work), and stale records whose heartbeat aged out. Report-only by default with clear next-steps; `--fix` runs the SAFE, non-destructive repair — reap OS-dead records via ADR-022 OS-truth (never PIDAlive/suspended/terminal). Stranded/unarmed agents are surfaced as needing an operator, never blind-spawned. Verified live: reported real stranded queues + 52 stale records. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27, ADR-022/ADR-024/ADR-026; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`node-status` now surfaces "stranded inboxes" — open items routed to an agent with no armed watcher are visible, not silently lost** (claude-home, 2026-06-19, router hardening PR #2). Work routed to an agent whose thread is unarmed (loop dead, or no thread at all) used to sit unread until a human noticed. New `AgentArmed` (reuses the #79/#80 honest-liveness verdict — loop-monitor needs a live thr-id loop, every other surface is armed by a fresh heartbeat) + `computeStranded` add a `NodeStatus.StrandedInbox []StrandedAgent` listing each backlogged-but-unarmed agent and its item count; the human table prints a loud `⚠ Stranded inbox` section. Reuses the already-computed `PendingByAgent`, so zero extra scan; zero spawn/orphan risk (this slice only makes stranding VISIBLE — the wake ACTION, configured adapters + per-agent LaunchAgent pull-loops, is the next slice). Verified live: surfaced real stranded queues (claude-finalwishes/claude-pantheon/claude-deck). Regression test covers loop-dead + no-thread → stranded, live-loop + app-heartbeat → not. `go test ./internal/router` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27, ADR-024/ADR-026 (relay completion), codex-home wake-design validation 20260619-152314; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **The menubar now surfaces all five missing deities — Osiris, Seshat, Net, Vault, RTK** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-19). The menu bar showed Anubis/Horus/Ma'at/Thoth/Ra but five deities had no surface (the "deity menubar gaps"). Added a navigable row for each, every one routing to a real, actionable view (Mole bar — no dead-end clicks, [[feedback_menubar_broken]]): **Osiris 𓁹 → `osiris risk`** (uncommitted-work checkpoint: branch, risk level, change counts, last commit), **Seshat 𓁆 → `seshat list`** (ingested knowledge items), **Net 𓁯 → `net status`** (plan-vs-build-log alignment), **Vault 🏛️ → `vault stats`** (context sandbox + code-index stats), **RTK ⚡ → `rtk stats`** (output-reduction statistics). Each uses the same `ResultView(args:)` pattern as the existing Ma'at/Thoth/Ra rows, and each subcommand was verified to produce output before wiring (RTK/Vault are agent-internal, so they route to their read-only `stats` view rather than their stdin/query-driven verbs — informative, never a dead-end). Pure Swift `Views.swift` addition; `swift build` clean. **No menubar redeploy** (the rows ship on the next deliberate signed release — [[feedback_dont_churn_menubar_fda]]). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10 (UI fidelity)/A25 (deity registry glyphs), docs/DEITY_REGISTRY.md; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Kernel memory-pressure watcher — NodeCapacity's pressure is now the node-proportional kernel level, not a hardcoded free-% seed** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-19). The last ADR-031-B #4 slice. macOS computes memory pressure RELATIVE to the machine, so subscribing to the kernel's `DISPATCH_SOURCE_TYPE_MEMORYPRESSURE` source is node-proportional "for free" — the right replacement for the bootstrap free-% seed (which `sysctl kern.memorystatus_vm_pressure_level` can't supply unprivileged — "Operation not permitted" — and `memory_pressure -Q` only gives a point-in-time percent, the very hardcoded-percent trap #4 removes; codex-pantheon SME verdict). New `internal/guard/pressure.go` defines a tiny `PressureWatcher` interface (`Subscribe(ctx, chan<- PressureEvent)`); the Darwin impl (`pressure_darwin.go` + `pressure_darwin_callback.go`, behind `//go:build darwin && cgo`) is a libdispatch shim: a serial queue + memory-pressure source masked `NORMAL|WARN|CRITICAL`, `dispatch_resume` after handler registration, the handler reads the coalesced `dispatch_source_get_data` **bitmask and picks the most-severe flag**, and forwards it to Go via an exported callback with **primitive values only — no Go pointers retained in C**. The handler **enqueues only** (a tiny atomic store + non-blocking fan-out); it never traverses/purges caches (that aggravates VM pressure) — **all policy stays in Hapi's bounded loop**, which now combines the kernel level with its RSS accounting (`memTierFromPressure` RAISES the tier floor; free-% remains the fallback and the only path to a kill). `!darwin || !cgo` builds get a no-op watcher (`pressure_stub.go`) and fall back to the bootstrap snapshot — **never a fabricated threshold** (the agent binary stays `CGO_ENABLED=0`, Rule A3). Because dispatch pressure is transition-based and per-process, the Hapi daemon re-stamps a tiny `~/.sirsi/pressure.json` cache every tick so one-shot readers (the broker's pre-launch gate, `sirsi hapi status`, the menubar) see the live level via `effectivePressure` (this process → fresh cache → bootstrap); a stale/missing stamp correctly means "no live watcher." `sirsi hapi status` now shows `Pressure source: kernel-dispatch` when the daemon is up. The governor also reacts the instant the kernel escalates (subscribes in `MemGovernor.Start`), not only on the next tick. Tests: bitmask→most-severe decode, cache round-trip + staleness, `effectivePressure` prefers the kernel cache over bootstrap, the unknown-without-total path, and Subscribe-is-safe; `go test ./internal/guard ./cmd/sirsi` (cgo) + `CGO_ENABLED=0 go build` (fallback) + gofmt + vet + golangci-lint(0) all green. Broker stays DISABLED (owner holds `sirsi gemma serve`). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A3/A16, ADR-031-B #4 (pressure source RESOLVED), codex-pantheon SME (router 20260619-052200); Changelog: Unreleased.
- **The menubar surfaces Hapi's memory-pressure level AND its source — no Swift rebuild, no FDA churn** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-19). The `diagnose` "RAM Pressure" check now appends the `NodeCapacity` pressure level + its **source** to the finding detail — e.g. `… | Pressure: normal (source: kernel-dispatch)` — and the menubar's `FindingView` already renders `finding.detail`, so the Hapi pressure signal reaches the menubar with **zero Swift change** (the app shells `sirsi diagnose --json` at runtime; only the CLI updates). The `source` distinguishes the authoritative kernel `DISPATCH_SOURCE_MEMORYPRESSURE` level (`kernel-dispatch`, when the always-on Hapi daemon's watcher from #72 is live) from the bootstrap free-% estimate (`bootstrap-snapshot`), so a glance tells you whether pressure is node-proportional truth or a fallback seed. Darwin-only (the mock-platform diagnose tests stay deterministic). Verified live: `sirsi diagnose --json` → `RAM Pressure` detail carries `Pressure: normal (source: …)`. gofmt + vet + golangci-lint(0) + `go test ./internal/guard` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10 (terminal/UI fidelity), ADR-031-B #4, feedback_dont_churn_menubar_fda; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`sirsi gemma serve` — the warm Pantheon inference broker (RAM-gated)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-17). Loads the local model ONCE onto the GPU and keeps it resident (`mlx_lm.server`), so `sirsi gemma` (and the router gemma worker) answers instantly with no per-call 12 GB reload — the original waste was a cold reload per item on a single serial thread. The CLI prefers the warm broker when up (`gemmaServerBase` health-check → `/v1/chat/completions`) and falls back to the cold path otherwise. **Safety is central (Hapi/A1):** an early concurrency-4 default OOM-crashed the 48 GB machine — MLX's pool grows ~a full model per concurrent decode slot, so a 12 GB model × 4 ballooned to ~64 GB → Jetsam. Now the broker **RAM-gates before launch** (`gemmaSafeConcurrency`): default concurrency **1**, each slot budgeted at a full model + 8 GB headroom for the OS/foreground/other agents (Claude/Codex), capped to fit, and it **refuses to start rather than OOM** when the model won't fit free RAM. Unit-tested (cap-to-1 for a 12 GB model on 40 GB free; refuse on insufficient RAM). `sirsi gemma serve --status|--stop`. Honest hardware note: MLX is GPU-only (`Device(gpu,0)`), not the ANE. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1 (safety), ADR-031 (inference broker), project_pantheon_ai_resource_broker; the 2026-06-18 OOM incident.
- **`sirsi relieve` — on-demand hang relief: renice the live CPU offender** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-17). Completes the App-Hangs relief arc (detect #47 → classify #53 → A1 floor #52 → **relieve**). Finds the process currently saturating the CPU — the *live* cause of a beachball / dropped frames — and lowers its scheduler priority so your foreground app gets the cycles back. Gentle and reversible: nothing is killed, the process keeps running (just yields CPU), and its priority resets when it exits. Routes through the #52-hardened `reniceByPID`, so WindowServer / kernel / audio / session-UI / sirsi-itself are **never** touched (defense-in-depth — `FindReliefTarget` also skips protected processes). **Dry-run by default (A1):** `sirsi relieve` previews the top hog; `--confirm` applies; `--min-cpu` and an optional process-name filter narrow it. Emits the `output.CommandResult` shape, so the menubar's "Relieve the live cause" button (App-Hangs findings now map to `sirsi relieve`) renders a preview → Apply with zero menubar rebuild. Reuses the watchdog's injectable `sampleTopCPU` (A16/A21); tests cover top-hog selection, protected-skip, min-cpu, name-hint, and protected-refusal. Verified live (found `XprotectService` at 44% CPU). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A16/A21, #52 reniceByPID floor, App-Hangs arc #47/#48/#53; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`sirsi gemma "<prompt>"` — a one-line way for a human to talk to the local model** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-17). Gemma (local MLX, zero API tokens, nothing leaves the Mac) was only reachable by *agents* — the router `to: gemma` inbox + the `sirsi-gemma` MCP tools — so a person had no direct way in (and the worker daemon isn't always running). New `sirsi gemma` is a thin synchronous wrapper over the **same `mlx_lm.generate` path the gemma-worker uses** (same model resolution: `--model` flag > `GEMMA_MODEL` > `~/.sirsi/gemma-model[-max].conf` > fallback), so CLI and daemon speak to the identical model. Reads args and/or piped stdin (`git log | sirsi gemma --task summarize "what shipped?"`), with `--task {classify|summarize|draft|analyze|plan|extract}`, `--max` (largest model), `--max-tokens`. The current model is a **reasoning model** that emits a `<|channel>thought …</channel>` chain before its answer; the cleaner extracts only the text after the final channel marker so the user sees the answer, not the monologue. Verified live: `"what are you?"` → "I am a large language model… on Google's infrastructure"; a piped `--task summarize` produced an accurate one-line digest. Partially lands the gemma-worker productization (human access); the daemon-not-running and a friendly `sirsi gemma chat` REPL remain follow-ups. Refs: reference_gemma_worker_surface, sirsi-gemma MCP, gemma-worker.sh.
- **`sirsi uninstall` — Pantheon ghost-hunts itself, no clutter left behind** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-10). The cleaner that exists to remove mess must be able to remove *itself* cleanly. New `sirsi uninstall` deterministically plans + removes Pantheon's runtime footprint — LaunchAgents (`ai.sirsi.pantheon`, `ai.sirsi.horus.agent-router`), dev binaries under `~/.local/bin`, `~/.config/{pantheon,sirsi,anubis}`, the menubar `.app` — and crucially **resets its own Full Disk Access grant** (`tccutil reset SystemPolicyAllFiles ai.sirsi.pantheon`), the thing that previously accreted duplicate TCC rows. Safety-first per A1: **dry-run by default** (`sirsi uninstall` previews; `--confirm` + an interactive `[y/N]` to apply), apps go to the **Trash** (recoverable), never a hard delete — which is also the only A19-respecting way to clear a bundle. Two removals macOS forces to be manual are surfaced as explicit guidance, not silently skipped: Homebrew installs (`brew uninstall --cask`) and `/Applications/*.app` (A19 forbids automated removal); bare-binary *path-keyed* FDA rows can't be reset by `tccutil` (no bundle id) so the user is pointed at System Settings. `internal/setup.PlanUninstall`/`Uninstall` with an injectable, mutex-guarded exec seam (A16/A21); 3 tests lock footprint coverage, the dry-run-has-no-side-effects guarantee, and Trash-via-Finder. Pairs with the bundle-only-FDA install direction so a clean install ↔ clean uninstall round-trips with zero residue. Verified: `go test ./internal/setup` green, `golangci-lint` 0, dry-run run end-to-end. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A16/A19/A21; the FDA-clutter cleanup 2026-06-10; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`sirsi insight` — cross-deity platform state-of-the-union, AI-OPTIONAL** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-10). Pantheon is more than scan/clean/diagnose, and its intelligence layer must operate with **no AI backend** yet **use one when present**. New `sirsi insight` aggregates a deterministic, platform-wide picture across the deities — Anubis (reclaimable bytes), Horus (health worst-severity + concrete remediations from `diagnose`), Thoth (memory freshness), the router (open hand-offs), Ma'at/Ra (last-run state) — and emits a **priority-sorted list of concrete next actions** with the exact command for each (e.g. `sirsi self-update` to heal the self-crashing binary, `sirsi clean` for the crash backlog). Two new packages: `internal/insight` (the deterministic aggregator — imports **no** AI package, so the core is structurally incapable of depending on a model; `Build()` always reports `source=rules`) and `internal/gemma` (the **optional** local-MLX-Gemma backend — every call gated by `Available()`, an injectable+mutex-guarded exec seam per A16/A21). When `~/.venvs/mlx` + the model are present, `sirsi insight` adds a plain-language narration and flips `source` to `rules+gemma`; with `--no-ai` (or no backend installed) it prints the identical deterministic recommendation. `--json` for surfaces (the native menubar consumes this). Verified live both ways: `--no-ai` → 6 deity signals + 4 ranked actions, `source=rules`; default with Gemma → same + a 2-sentence on-device narration, `source=rules+gemma`. `go test ./internal/gemma ./internal/insight` green, `golangci-lint` 0 issues. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A11 (local-only, zero telemetry)/A16/A23, ADR-030; sirsi-gemma (cmd/sirsi-gemma); Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`binding-hold` required-check gate — a safety/binding-review hold native auto-merge can't bypass** (claude-pantheon, blessed by claude-home/Ma'at-gate owner, 2026-06-10). Closes the class where a safety-tier PR merges before its binding verdict (the #33 bypass: a rebase-push tripped armed auto-merge before review). GitHub native auto-merge respects required STATUS CHECKS, not labels — so a label alone can't hold a PR. New `.github/workflows/binding-hold.yml` job `binding-hold` **passes when unlabeled and FAILS when the PR carries the `binding-hold` label**; once it's a required status check, branch protection blocks the merge (auto-merge waits) until a binding reviewer (codex / claude-home root-authority) removes the label on PASS. Applied at open to any A1/clean-path/binary-mutating/security/migration PR (or any PR explicitly held for arch/binding review). Lives in its own workflow so toggling the label re-runs ONLY this lightweight gate, not the full lint/test/build matrix (commit SHA unchanged → heavy-job results stay valid). Injection-safe: `if:` compares the labels array to a literal constant, `run:` steps are static strings. Extends the Ma'at quality gate (A25/A28) with a binding-review hold; pairs with the canonical-router fix (ADR-029 Amendment 1) so review requests actually reach the reviewer. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A25/A28, ADR-029 Amendment 1; router 20260610-204000 (claude-home bless + rollout authorization); Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Native macOS menubar — NSStatusItem + NSPopover + SwiftUI (ADR-030), the durable replacement for the systray dropdown** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-10). The `fyne.io/systray` tray menu structurally cannot keep a panel open, drill in with a back button, render a scrollable manifest, or show progress/result inline — which is why "Clean Waste" felt like a dead click and the user asked for "everything in the menubar… which can go backwards." New SwiftPM app at `macapp/` (built with `swift build`, packaged as a Dock-less `.app` with stable `CFBundleIdentifier=ai.sirsi.pantheon` for TCC continuity) hosts a SwiftUI `NavigationStack` in an `NSPopover` anchored to the status item. **Hard constraint: the surface owns zero business logic** — the Go `sirsi` binary stays the single source of truth and the only code that deletes; `SirsiEngine` only *reads* the persisted scan (`~/.config/pantheon/findings/latest-scan.json`) to render and *shells* `sirsi` to act. **Anubis** clean flow ships end-to-end inline (Home → Review & Clean → safe manifest → trash safe set → result in place, native back button, never kicking out); **Horus — Ops** is a live health view (shells `sirsi diagnose --json`, renders each check with a severity dot + expandable detail). Ma'at (sirsi maat audit) and Thoth (sirsi thoth status) render inline via a reusable CommandView — all four deity rows are live, no dead ends. Installed via LaunchAgent (`ai.sirsi.pantheon`) replacing the systray binary on macOS; systray stays for Linux/Windows. Verified: `swift build -c release` clean; `.app` ad-hoc signed; launches Dock-less, stable single instance, reads the live scan + diagnose. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A19/A23/A27, feedback_tui_is_the_session, feedback_menubar_broken, ADR-030 (supersedes systray GUI on macOS); Changelog: Unreleased.
### Fixed
- **`sirsi clean` no longer one-click-trashes active-project `node_modules` + dev caches, and `diagnose`'s crash-report remediation actually clears crashes** (claude-home, 2026-06-16). Two related footguns in the clean/diagnose hygiene flow, surfaced when a real `sirsi clean` preview listed **active-project `node_modules`** (SirsiNexusApp 806 MB, FinalWishes 550 MB) plus `~/.npm/_cacache` and `~/go/pkg/mod/cache` in its default (one-click) set. (1) **Active dependencies were one-click "safe":** `NewNodeModulesRule`, `NewGoModCacheRule`, and `NewNpmGlobalCacheRule` set no severity → defaulted to `SeveritySafe` → included in the default `sirsi clean` set, so `sirsi clean --confirm` would trash active-project `node_modules`, forcing a reinstall before those projects build. This is the same class #33 fixed for AI weights (which deliberately left `node_modules`/Go cache as safe); extended here to active dev deps — all three now set explicit `severity: jackal.SeverityCaution`, surfacing as reviewable caution-tier (reachable only via `--include-caution`), never one-click. AI weights remain caution via the `CategoryAI` default (#33), untouched. (2) **`diagnose` recommended a command that couldn't do the job:** the "App Crashes" remediation returned bare `sirsi clean`, but `crash_reports` is itself caution-tier (non-regenerable crash evidence), so safe-only `sirsi clean` never touches it — the recommended action was a no-op for the very finding it addressed (observed: crash count unchanged after running it). Now returns `sirsi clean --include-caution` with an accurate description (preview → re-run with `--confirm`). Regression test `TestActiveDevDepsAreCaution` locks node_modules/go/npm → caution; existing `TestAIModelCachesAreCaution` stays green. Verified: `gofmt`/`go vet`/`golangci-lint` 0 + `go test ./internal/jackal/... ./cmd/sirsi/` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A23, the #33 AI-cache precedent; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Canonical router — `sirsi router`/`thread` from a worktree no longer hits a stale copy (ADR-029 Amendment 1)** (claude-pantheon, blessed by claude-home, 2026-06-10). ADR-029 per-agent worktrees fixed `core.bare` corruption but introduced a silent regression: `.agents/idea-router/` is git-tracked, so every worktree carries its **own snapshot copy** of the router, and `router.FindRepoRoot` resolved it by walking up from `os.Getwd()` — so any `sirsi router`/`thread` command run from a worktree cwd read/wrote that **stale local copy**, invisible to the live root router and the agents watching it. A binding-review request routed from a worktree was **silently dropped** — a broken review gate (the 2026-06-10 audit found exactly one lost item: a #33 safety-review request that never reached the binding reviewer). Fix: `FindRepoRoot` now resolves the **main worktree root** first (the shared `.git`'s parent via `git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir`), trusting it only when the live `.agents/idea-router/` actually lives there, else falling back to the original cwd walk-up (non-git checkouts, tests) — cwd no longer decides which router you hit. Git seam is injectable (`gitCommonDirFn`, A16) + RWMutex-guarded (A21); 3 tests cover prefers-canonical / no-git-fallback / git-root-without-router-fallback. Dogfooded: the fixed binary run from a worktree resolves `Router home` to the repo root, not the worktree copy. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A16/A21/A26, ADR-029 Amendment 1; router 20260610-202300 (claude-home bless); Changelog: Unreleased.
- **AI/ML model caches are never one-click "safe" — caught a one-click Trash of 30.7 GB of HuggingFace weights** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-10). During a pre-flight QA pass of the menubar Clean flow, the "safe" (one-click-deletable) set contained **30.7 GB of HuggingFace model weights** (`~/.cache/huggingface/hub`). Root cause: `baseScanRule.effectiveSeverity()` defaulted any rule with no explicit severity to `SeveritySafe`, and **no AI cache rule set a severity** — so downloaded model weights (HuggingFace, PyTorch, Ollama, MLX, vLLM, Stable Diffusion, JAX, …) were classified one-click-safe, the same tier as `node_modules`. Model weights cost hours + bandwidth to regenerate and are categorically not "junk." Fix: `effectiveSeverity()` now returns `SeverityCaution` for `CategoryAI` rules with no explicit severity, so AI caches surface as **caution (reviewable, excluded from one-click clean)** even when genuinely cold — the age/env guards still suppress *recently-used* caches entirely, this is the defense for cold ones (Rule A1: a one-click surface may only trash trivially-regenerable items). After the fix + a rescan, the one-click SAFE set dropped from 36.2 GB → **5.5 GB** (node_modules, Go module cache, system caches only); the HF cache is excluded. Regression test `TestAIModelCachesAreCaution` locks AI→caution / non-AI→safe / explicit-severity-wins. Verified: `go test ./internal/jackal/...` green, `go build ./...` green, live rescan confirms HF no longer one-click-safe. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A23, ADR-030 (menubar surface that exposed it); Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Menubar "Clean Waste…" no longer feels like a dead click — visible feedback + a usable confirm window** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-10). The user reported "go into the menubar, ask Anubis to clean waste… nothing happens." Root cause: clicking **Anubis → Clean Waste…** runs only a *preview* (`anubis clean --include-caution`, dry-run) whose output is captured into the in-app store (never surfaced), and its sole visible effect was un-hiding a *separate* `✓ Confirm Clean` item — but (a) the macOS menu closes the instant you click, so you never see the armed item appear; (b) it auto-hid after just **25s**; and (c) the actual delete only happens on a *second* click of that item. Net: no banner, no window, no progress — the click looked dead, and the one action that would delete was invisible. Fix (stopgap within `fyne.io/systray`, additive): every click now fires a **macOS banner** via `notify.Toast` — on preview success, `"<amount> ready to clean — reopen the Sirsi menu → Anubis → ✓ Confirm Clean"`; on nothing-to-clean / error, an explicit banner so the click is never silent; and a **completion banner** after apply. The confirm-arm window is widened from 25s → **2 minutes** (`confirmArmWindow`) so the reopen-and-click round-trip actually fits, while still never leaving a standing one-click delete forever. The durable fix remains the ADR-030 NSPopover/SwiftUI persistent surface (preview→confirm→result inline); this makes the current surface usable today. Verified: `go build`/`go vet`/`golangci-lint` 0 issues + `go test ./cmd/sirsi-menubar ./internal/notify` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A10/A23, feedback_tui_is_the_session, feedback_menubar_broken, ADR-030; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Menubar Clean Waste is now safe-only + fully reviewable — you can trust what it deletes** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-10). Follow-up to the visible-feedback fix, prompted by the user: "can I trust either option? I have zero visibility into what's being cleaned." Two real safety gaps fixed: (1) **Blast radius** — the menubar quietly passed `--include-caution`, so one click moved **474 items / 39.2 GB** including 398 *caution-tier* judgment calls (app remnants), not just the **74 safe / 5.0 GB** of regenerable caches/`node_modules`/build artifacts. A one-click surface must only ever touch clearly-regenerable, trash-first items, so both the preview and the apply now run **safe-only** (`anubis clean` / `anubis clean --dry-run=false`, no `--include-caution`); caution-tier items stay reachable only via the deliberate CLI flow (`sirsi anubis clean --include-caution --confirm`). (2) **Consent needs visibility** — even the preview truncated to the top 10 of 474 items, so there was no manifest to review. New **"↳ Review what will be cleaned…"** menu item (`reviewCleanList`) reads the same persisted scan the cleaner uses and opens a complete, sorted manifest: every SAFE item that *will* be trashed (full path + size) and every CAUTION item that is *excluded* (with the exact CLI to clean them deliberately) — so the list is exactly what Confirm moves. The preview banner now routes the user to Review before Confirm. Verified: `go build`/`go vet`/`golangci-lint` 0 issues + `go test ./cmd/sirsi-menubar ./internal/notify` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1 (dry-run + verifiability), A23, feedback_tui_is_the_session, feedback_menubar_broken, ADR-030; Changelog: Unreleased.
### Added
- **Codified the fresh-inode binary-write invariant (systemic prevention of the #1-crasher class)** (claude-pantheon, routed by claude-home, 2026-06-09). After the pre-merge AMFI catch on the menubar `.app`-bundle install (a `WriteFile` over a live exec inode would have reintroduced the macOS AMFI stale-cdhash → SIGKILL-137 class — the very bug making `sirsi` its own top crasher), added a **Binary-write invariant** section to `CONTRIBUTING.md` under Safety Rules: every code path that installs/replaces an executable MUST land it on a fresh inode — `internal/selfupdate.SafeReplace` for allow-listed CLI dirs (staged `.new` → codesign → `rename(2)`; refuses `.app` by design, A19), or `os.Remove`+`WriteFile`+`codesign` for paths SafeReplace won't take (e.g. a user-owned `~/Applications/*.app` bundle). A plain `WriteFile`/`cp` over an existing binary path is flagged as a regression of the #1-crasher class, to be caught in review (cross-reference `reference_macos_amfi_cp_sigkill` on any PR touching a binary-install path). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A19, reference_macos_amfi_cp_sigkill, PR #19 SafeReplace / #26 menubar bundle; router 20260609-054400/054800; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`sirsi continue` (alias `sirsi resume`) — surface the continuation prompt on one command** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). Pantheon is the memory/resume tool, but resuming a session meant *hunting* for `docs/CONTINUATION-PROMPT.md`. Now `sirsi continue` prints it (styled banner + the doc), `sirsi resume` aliases it, and `--json` emits `{path, content}` for tooling/hooks. Resolves repo root via `router.FindRepoRoot` (falls back to CWD); if no prompt exists, fails with one actionable line ("write your session state there, or run `sirsi thoth sync`"). Enables the SessionStart hook to auto-surface resume context (follow-up). Verified by running: `continue`/`resume` print the prompt, error path is clean. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A15 (session boundary), A10 (terminal UX), feedback_thoth_over_compact; Changelog: Unreleased.
### Fixed
- **Menubar updates live on state changes instead of every 4 hours** (claude-pantheon, routed by claude-home, 2026-06-09). The user's pain: "the menubar hasnt updated in 10 minutes… 4 hours is lunacy. fix this issue permanently." `startPeriodicScan` ran one scan on launch then `time.After(4 * time.Hour)`, so after an external state change (e.g. a `sirsi clean` from the CLI re-persisting findings) the tray label stayed stale for up to 4h. New `startLiveRefresh` watches the findings dir with **fsnotify** and, on a debounced write to `latest-scan.json`, refreshes the tray LABEL from the persisted file — a cheap read, **no rescan and no re-persist**, so it can never loop with the watch that triggers it. A **SIGUSR1** is an explicit manual nudge (e.g. post-clean). Debounce (1.5s) coalesces the multi-write bursts a clean makes — deliberately NOT per-write, which would re-amplify the very `mds_stores` write-storm Rail B addresses. The label refresh is wholly separate from the resident-surface liveness heartbeat (A27, ≥60s). The 4h periodic scan stays as the full-rescan fallback. Additive + safe: if the watcher can't start, the menubar simply keeps the old 4h behavior. Verified: `go build`/`go vet`/`golangci-lint` 0 issues on `cmd/sirsi-menubar`. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27, feedback_tui_is_the_session; router 20260609-044722; Changelog: Unreleased.
### Fixed
- **Menubar stops re-asking for Full Disk Access on every rebuild** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). The menubar "kept asking for FDA" and looked like duplicate apps because a plain `go build` ad-hoc-signs `sirsi-menubar` with a **content-hash-derived identifier** (`sirsi-menubar-<hash>`) — macOS TCC keys Full Disk Access on the code-signing identifier, so **every rebuild/reinstall minted a new identity → a fresh FDA prompt**, and the dev binary's identity never matched the `/Applications/Pantheon.app` bundle (`ai.sirsi.pantheon`) → two TCC entries for one app. `InstallMenubar` now re-signs the binary with the **stable identifier `ai.sirsi.pantheon`** (best-effort, ad-hoc, darwin-only, idempotent) before loading the LaunchAgent, so one FDA grant persists across reinstalls and the dev binary shares the bundle's TCC identity. Operationally cleaned up the live duplicates (killed churning instances, re-signed, reloaded one clean instance). Aligns with feedback_permissions_in_install (FDA is install-time, not mid-use). Verified: `go build`, `go vet`, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `go test ./internal/setup` green; live menubar re-signed to `Identifier=ai.sirsi.pantheon`. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A19 (no bundle mutation — bundle untouched), feedback_permissions_in_install; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Two more command-completion arcs — `sirsi permissions` and `sirsi quickstart` now end cleanly** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). Continues the command-completion arc (builds on the `SilenceUsage`/`SilenceErrors` root fix). (1) **`sirsi permissions`** previously trailed off with a bare `Opening System Settings...` line and exited — no result, no next-step, no confirmation it had done anything (it did not use the `output.CommandResult` contract every other command renders). Now it closes with a `CommandResult`: a one-line summary ("Add the path above to Full Disk Access, then re-run a scan to confirm"), evidence (the exact binary path + whether System Settings opened, or a manual-path warning on failure), and next actions (`sirsi scan`, `sirsi setup`); the non-macOS branch likewise returns a clean `CommandResult` instead of a stray `fmt.Println`. (2) **`sirsi quickstart`** recommended `sirsi judge --dry-run` / `--confirm` as its "safe to clean" next step — but `judge` is a **hidden legacy alias** (`judgeCmd.Hidden = true`), invisible in `sirsi --help`, so the guided first-run pointed new users at a command they can't discover; switched to the canonical `sirsi clean` (preview by default) / `sirsi clean --confirm`. Audited all 28 top-level commands in a safe non-interactive preview pass; the rest already complete with a visible result + next-step. Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./cmd/sirsi/`, `golangci-lint run ./cmd/sirsi/` (0 issues), `go test ./cmd/sirsi/` green; `permissions`/`quickstart` re-run and confirmed to end with a clear outcome. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10 (terminal UX), feedback_menubar_broken (test real flows), Rule 0/A12 (additive); Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Every command now visibly resolves — failed commands read as failures, not help dumps** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). Root cause of "most commands don't seem to work or complete": `rootCmd` had neither `SilenceUsage` nor `SilenceErrors`, so **any** `RunE` error made cobra print the full `Usage:` block — a failed command looked like it "just printed help and did nothing." Set both on root and render the failure as one clean styled line in `main()` (`output.Error`, JSON-aware), so all 28 commands now end with an unambiguous outcome. Two concrete completion-arc fixes on top: (1) **`sirsi risk`** outside a git repo printed `osiris: not a git repository: exit status 128` + a usage dump → now a single actionable line ("risk assessment needs a git repository — run inside a project, or pass a repo path"); (2) **`sirsi duplicates`** with no path defaulted to hashing all of `$HOME` (3-phase hash → minutes, looked hung with a spinner and no completion) → now defaults to the **current directory** (scoped, completes promptly, names the target and hints `pass a path to widen`); the result render (Summary + Evidence + NextActions) was already correct, only the default scope was wrong. Verified by running each: `risk`/`duplicates` exit cleanly with a visible result; `go vet`, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `go test ./cmd/sirsi ./internal/osiris ./internal/mirror` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10 (terminal UX), feedback_menubar_broken (test real flows), Rule 0; Changelog: Unreleased.
### Added
- **Binary-drift self-heal: AMFI-safe atomic replace contract (flagship Rail A — safety core)** (claude-pantheon, routed by claude-home, 2026-06-09). `internal/selfupdate/selfheal.go` adds `SafeReplace(src, dst)` — the remediation half of ADR-023 that answers `sirsi diagnose`'s binary-drift finding (sirsi has been its own #1 crasher, 21/61 reports, via this exact failure). On macOS, `cp`-ing a fresh Go binary over an existing one leaves a stale code-signing cdhash bound to the old inode → next exec is SIGKILL'd (137) by AMFI, silently killing LaunchAgents/heartbeats. The fix stages a fresh inode (`dst.new`), `codesign --force --sign -`s it, then `rename(2)`s it over the target. Built to claude-home's three guardrails (router 035409): **(1) detect ≠ apply** — `SafeReplace` never prompts; the caller previews+confirms (A1); **(2) atomic, no half-states** — staged-rename means an interrupt leaves the OLD working binary in place, never a gap; **(3) allow-list, not deny-list** — writes ONLY to the known CLI bin dirs (`~/.local/bin`, `~/go/bin`, `/opt/homebrew/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`), and **never inside a `.app` bundle** (Rule A19 absolute, with an explicit loud rejection). codesign is darwin-only; the staged-rename path also works for linux CLI installs. Exec is injectable (`healExecFn`, A16); allow-list is injectable for tests. Tests include the **AMFI-137 regression guard** (the replaced binary actually executes) and the codesign-failure-leaves-old-binary case. **Wired to the `sirsi self-update` one-click** (`cmd/sirsi/selfupdate.go`): `DetectCLIDrift` walks the allow-list and content-hashes each CLI copy against the running binary (read-only, idempotent — a converged copy is never a target); the command previews drift by default, `--json` is a pure read-only report (safe for CI/hooks/menubar tick), and `--confirm` applies (interactive `[y/N]`, no `--yes` auto for the rewrite), then **verifies convergence post-heal** and prints a **restart note** (a process already on the old inode keeps it until restart). Built to claude-home's 5 CLI gates (router 040600). Dogfooded live: `sirsi self-update` correctly flags this host's stale `~/.local/bin/sirsi` copy. Verified: `go test -race ./internal/selfupdate` green (8 tests), `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `go build ./...` + linux cross-compile green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A16/A19, ADR-023; router 20260609-035409/040600; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Binary-drift self-heal: AMFI-safe atomic replace contract (flagship Rail A — safety core)** (claude-pantheon, routed by claude-home, 2026-06-09). `internal/selfupdate/selfheal.go` adds `SafeReplace(src, dst)` — the remediation half of ADR-023 that answers `sirsi diagnose`'s binary-drift finding (sirsi has been its own #1 crasher, 21/61 reports, via this exact failure). On macOS, `cp`-ing a fresh Go binary over an existing one leaves a stale code-signing cdhash bound to the old inode → next exec is SIGKILL'd (137) by AMFI, silently killing LaunchAgents/heartbeats. The fix stages a fresh inode (`dst.new`), `codesign --force --sign -`s it, then `rename(2)`s it over the target. Built to claude-home's three guardrails (router 035409): **(1) detect ≠ apply** — `SafeReplace` never prompts; the caller previews+confirms (A1); **(2) atomic, no half-states** — staged-rename means an interrupt leaves the OLD working binary in place, never a gap; **(3) allow-list, not deny-list** — writes ONLY to the known CLI bin dirs (`~/.local/bin`, `~/go/bin`, `/opt/homebrew/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`), and **never inside a `.app` bundle** (Rule A19 absolute, with an explicit loud rejection). codesign is darwin-only; the staged-rename path also works for linux CLI installs. Exec is injectable (`healExecFn`, A16); allow-list is injectable for tests. 5 tests including the **AMFI-137 regression guard** (the replaced binary actually executes) and the codesign-failure-leaves-old-binary case. Pure library + tests — no CLI mutation surface yet; the `sirsi self-update` one-click wiring (preview cdhash drift + confirm) is the immediate follow-up that calls this reviewed primitive. Verified: `go test -race ./internal/selfupdate` green, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `go build ./...` + linux cross-compile green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A16/A19, ADR-023; router 20260609-035409; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`sirsi continue` (alias `sirsi resume`) — surface the continuation prompt on one command** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). Pantheon is the memory/resume tool, but resuming a session meant *hunting* for `docs/CONTINUATION-PROMPT.md`. Now `sirsi continue` prints it (styled banner + the doc), `sirsi resume` aliases it, and `--json` emits `{path, content}` for tooling/hooks. Resolves repo root via `router.FindRepoRoot` (falls back to CWD); if no prompt exists, fails with one actionable line ("write your session state there, or run `sirsi thoth sync`"). Enables the SessionStart hook to auto-surface resume context (follow-up). Verified by running: `continue`/`resume` print the prompt, error path is clean. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A15 (session boundary), A10 (terminal UX), feedback_thoth_over_compact; Changelog: Unreleased.
### Fixed
- **Menubar stops re-prompting for Full Disk Access on every reinstall — the structural .app-bundle fix** (claude-pantheon, routed by claude-home, 2026-06-09). The user's recurring daily pain ("im tired of approving these continually… i do this for no other even when i reinstall") had two compounding causes (A23-diagnosed at source): (1) ad-hoc `codesign --sign -` carries no Team ID, so TCC saw a new identity each re-sign — already addressed by signing with the stable `--identifier ai.sirsi.pantheon` (PR #17); and (2) **the structural remainder** — the menubar shipped as a BARE Mach-O at `~/.local/bin/sirsi-menubar`, which has only a *content-hash* TCC identity, so every rebuild looked like a brand-new app. `InstallMenubar` now packages the menubar as a real **`.app` bundle** at `~/Applications/Sirsi Menubar.app` with an `Info.plist` carrying a stable `CFBundleIdentifier = ai.sirsi.pantheon` (matching the LaunchAgent label + Pantheon.app), ad-hoc-signs the bundle (`codesign --deep --sign - --identifier`), and points the LaunchAgent at `Contents/MacOS/sirsi-menubar` — so macOS TCC keys Full Disk Access on the stable bundle id and **one grant survives reinstalls**. `LSUIElement=true` keeps it a Dock-less agent app. Additive + safe: falls back to signing the bare binary if bundle creation fails (never worse than before); no `/Applications/*.app` mutation (A19 — writes only to user `~/Applications`). Verified what's verifiable without mutating the live install: bundle scaffold + Info.plist unit-tested in temp dirs; the ad-hoc bundle sign produces `Identifier=ai.sirsi.pantheon` and `codesign --verify --deep --strict` passes on a temp bundle. **Acceptance gate (operator-run): grant FDA once → reinstall → confirm NO re-prompt** (the Menubar-QA "test the real flow" lesson — this PR makes the binary a stable TCC subject; the no-reprompt is confirmed on a real reinstall). `go test -race ./internal/setup` green, `golangci-lint` 0 issues. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A19/A23, feedback_permissions_in_install; router 20260609-050932; Changelog: Unreleased.

### Fixed
- **`sirsi-gemma` — MCP server exposing local MLX-Gemma to any MCP client** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-08). New binary at `cmd/sirsi-gemma/` that speaks MCP over stdio and registers exactly two tools — `gemma_chat` (multi-turn, system prompt + history rendered into Gemma's `<start_of_turn>` template) and `gemma_complete` (single-shot raw-prompt completion) — backed by chip A's `~/.venvs/mlx/bin/mlx_lm.generate` console script against a locally-installed Gemma 2 27B bf16-4bit (`mlx-community/gemma-2-27b-it-bf16-4bit`; the `bf16-` prefix is required on mlx-lm 0.31+). Built on `internal/mcp` via a new `NewBareServer` constructor so it advertises ONLY its two tools — not the full Anubis toolset — avoiding tool-name collisions when a client also runs the main `sirsi-pantheon` MCP server. Subprocess hidden behind an injectable `Runner` interface (Rule A16) so unit tests don't need a live Python install. Startup runs a 1-token health probe; failure swaps in `disabledRunner` so the MCP handshake still works and every tool call returns an actionable error pointing the operator at the setup doc — discoverable from Claude Code even when Gemma is misconfigured. Config at `~/.config/sirsi/gemma.toml` (flat key=value, zero external deps); defaults match chip A's install. Output is cleaned of mlx banner framing + Gemma turn-tokens (`<end_of_turn>`/`<eos>`). Static binary per A3 (`CGO_ENABLED=0` cross-compiles green for darwin/arm64, linux/amd64, windows/amd64); no telemetry per A11. Ships: `cmd/sirsi-gemma/README.md` (dev-facing, A8), `docs/user-guides/sirsi-gemma.md` (user-facing, A8, honest about Gemma's depth limits vs Claude per A23), `docs/setup/MCP_CONFIG_SIRSI_GEMMA.md` (copy-pasteable `~/.claude/mcp.json` snippet). **Verified live end-to-end** against chip A's local MLX install: health probe `MLX-Gemma alive`; `gemma_complete` → `PONG`, `gemma_chat` (system+history) → clean text; handshake advertises exactly `[gemma_chat, gemma_complete]`. Perf (chip A `gemma-smoke.sh`, M5 Max, A14): ~33.4–33.8 tok/sec generation, ~234 tok/sec prompt eval, ~15.5 GB peak. `go test -race` green (11 tests), `golangci-lint` 0 issues. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A3/A6/A8/A11/A12/A14/A16/A23/A28; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Health surface: Spotlight write-amplification — detect + guide (flagship Rail B complete)** (claude-pantheon, routed by claude-home, 2026-06-09). Agent file-write bursts in heavy dev trees trigger Spotlight reindexing that pins `mds_stores`/`mdworker` and feeds the RAM-pressure → Jetsam loop (≈half this host's 8 kills/7d). **Detect** (read-only): new `sirsi diagnose` signal `Spotlight Storm` (`internal/guard/checkSpotlightStorm`) sums `%CPU` across the Spotlight indexer family from the process table `diagnose` already gathers (≥30% aggregate → `Warn` with the busy procs in Detail; else `OK`) — certain, no Spotlight-internals probing, injectable `platform.Platform` (A16, tested via `platform.Mock`). **Guide**: new `sirsi spotlight-exclude [path]` (default `~/Development`) — per claude-home's A23 mechanism ruling (router 045700), it does NOT mutate system state (`mdutil` is volume-level over-scope; the Privacy plist is SIP-protected + version-fragile). Instead it detects the storm, opens System Settings ▸ Spotlight ▸ Privacy (`open x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.Spotlight-Settings.extension?Privacy`) and tells the user the exact folder to add; reversible the same way; **idempotent on the actionable condition** (no-op when not storming — never claims to have changed a pref); `--json` is read-only state; non-darwin no-ops cleanly. The post-fix `sirsi diagnose` (storm subsiding) is the measurable proof. Pure detect+guide = trivially A1/A19-safe (no destructive write). Dogfooded live: idle host → "nothing to exclude"; `--json` reports `storming:false`. Verified: `go test -race ./internal/guard ./cmd/sirsi` green, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `go build ./...` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A16/A19/A23, permissions-in-install; [[feedback_spotlight_write_amplification]]; router 20260609-033900/045700; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Health surface: Spotlight write-amplification storm detection (flagship Rail B — read-only surfacing)** (claude-pantheon, routed by claude-home, 2026-06-09). New `sirsi diagnose` signal `Spotlight Storm` (`internal/guard/checkSpotlightStorm`): agent file-write bursts in heavy dev trees trigger Spotlight reindexing that pins `mds_stores`/`mdworker` and feeds the RAM-pressure → Jetsam loop (≈half this host's 8 kills/7d). The check sums `%CPU` across the Spotlight indexer family from the process table `diagnose` already gathers (≥30% aggregate → `Warn` with the busy processes in Detail; otherwise `OK`) — a **certain, read-only** signal that needs no Spotlight-internals probing. This is the surfacing-first half of Rail B; the opt-in, reversible `~/Development` Privacy exclusion that *fixes* the storm lands behind its own confirm-gate once the exact per-directory exclusion mechanism is settled (mdutil is volume-level; per-folder exclusion goes through the Spotlight Privacy plist — routed to claude-home rather than guessed, Rule A23). Uses the injectable `platform.Platform` (A16) so it's tested via `platform.Mock` (storm/idle/no-indexer cases). Dogfooded live: this host currently shows `Spotlight indexer idle`. Verified: `go test -race ./internal/guard` green, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `go build ./...` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A16, A23; [[feedback_spotlight_write_amplification]]; router 20260609-033900; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **`sirsi continue` (alias `sirsi resume`) — surface the continuation prompt on one command** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). Pantheon is the memory/resume tool, but resuming a session meant *hunting* for `docs/CONTINUATION-PROMPT.md`. Now `sirsi continue` prints it (styled banner + the doc), `sirsi resume` aliases it, and `--json` emits `{path, content}` for tooling/hooks. Resolves repo root via `router.FindRepoRoot` (falls back to CWD); if no prompt exists, fails with one actionable line ("write your session state there, or run `sirsi thoth sync`"). Enables the SessionStart hook to auto-surface resume context (follow-up). Verified by running: `continue`/`resume` print the prompt, error path is clean. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A15 (session boundary), A10 (terminal UX), feedback_thoth_over_compact; Changelog: Unreleased.### Fixed- **Scan truthfulness, completed — 5 remaining AI cache rules were still unguarded** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). The first truthfulness pass env-guarded the six AI rules in `ai.go`, but `internal/jackal/rules/expanded.go` had **five more `CategoryAI` rules still registered in `AllRules()` as bare `baseScanRule`** with no liveness guard — `onnx_cache`, `vllm_cache` (compiled kernels, hours to rebuild), `jax_cache`, `stable_diffusion` (frequently multi-GB, actively used), `langchain_cache` — so on any machine with those tools the scan still over-reported actively-used caches as reclaimable. All five now carry `minAgeDays=30`; the three whose runtimes expose a canonical cache-dir env var additionally wrap in `envGuardedRule` with the **real** var (`ORT_CACHE_DIR`, `VLLM_CACHE_ROOT`, `JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR`) — no fabricated env vars for Stable Diffusion / LangChain, which have none (A23: don't imply support we lack; the mtime guard alone protects those). Also fixed a silent guard-defeating bug in `ai_liveness.go`: `liveTargetsFromEnv` called `ExpandPath(val, "")` with an **empty home**, so a common `~`-prefixed value like `HF_HOME=~/.cache/huggingface` expanded to a *relative* path that never matched an absolute finding — the live cache was reported as waste anyway; now resolves via `os.UserHomeDir()`. Locks with 4 tests (structural guard presence, real pin-var wiring, tilde→absolute resolution, behavioral pin-suppression). Verified: `go build ./...`, `go test -race ./internal/jackal/...` green, `golangci-lint` 0 issues. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A23 (scan truthfulness), A16; PR #21 review (claude-home advisory); Changelog: Unreleased.- **Menubar stops re-asking for Full Disk Access on every rebuild** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). The menubar "kept asking for FDA" and looked like duplicate apps because a plain `go build` ad-hoc-signs `sirsi-menubar` with a **content-hash-derived identifier** (`sirsi-menubar-<hash>`) — macOS TCC keys Full Disk Access on the code-signing identifier, so **every rebuild/reinstall minted a new identity → a fresh FDA prompt**, and the dev binary's identity never matched the `/Applications/Pantheon.app` bundle (`ai.sirsi.pantheon`) → two TCC entries for one app. `InstallMenubar` now re-signs the binary with the **stable identifier `ai.sirsi.pantheon`** (best-effort, ad-hoc, darwin-only, idempotent) before loading the LaunchAgent, so one FDA grant persists across reinstalls and the dev binary shares the bundle's TCC identity. Operationally cleaned up the live duplicates (killed churning instances, re-signed, reloaded one clean instance). Aligns with feedback_permissions_in_install (FDA is install-time, not mid-use). Verified: `go build`, `go vet`, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `go test ./internal/setup` green; live menubar re-signed to `Identifier=ai.sirsi.pantheon`. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A19 (no bundle mutation — bundle untouched), feedback_permissions_in_install; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Two more command-completion arcs — `sirsi permissions` and `sirsi quickstart` now end cleanly** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). Continues the command-completion arc (builds on the `SilenceUsage`/`SilenceErrors` root fix). (1) **`sirsi permissions`** previously trailed off with a bare `Opening System Settings...` line and exited — no result, no next-step, no confirmation it had done anything (it did not use the `output.CommandResult` contract every other command renders). Now it closes with a `CommandResult`: a one-line summary ("Add the path above to Full Disk Access, then re-run a scan to confirm"), evidence (the exact binary path + whether System Settings opened, or a manual-path warning on failure), and next actions (`sirsi scan`, `sirsi setup`); the non-macOS branch likewise returns a clean `CommandResult` instead of a stray `fmt.Println`. (2) **`sirsi quickstart`** recommended `sirsi judge --dry-run` / `--confirm` as its "safe to clean" next step — but `judge` is a **hidden legacy alias** (`judgeCmd.Hidden = true`), invisible in `sirsi --help`, so the guided first-run pointed new users at a command they can't discover; switched to the canonical `sirsi clean` (preview by default) / `sirsi clean --confirm`. Audited all 28 top-level commands in a safe non-interactive preview pass; the rest already complete with a visible result + next-step. Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./cmd/sirsi/`, `golangci-lint run ./cmd/sirsi/` (0 issues), `go test ./cmd/sirsi/` green; `permissions`/`quickstart` re-run and confirmed to end with a clear outcome. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10 (terminal UX), feedback_menubar_broken (test real flows), Rule 0/A12 (additive); Changelog: Unreleased.

- **Router registry self-compacts terminal records on register (A28 residue — the compaction job)** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). `RegisterThread` now opportunistically GCs terminal (closed/reaped) thread records older than `TerminalRetention` (3 days) on every register, via the existing `PruneClosed` primitive — no extra write (register already saves). Safe by construction: `PruneClosed` only removes terminal records, never active or suspended (ADR-025). With the per-resume thread-mint ROOT fixed (the SessionStart ancestry-walk fix), this keeps the post-reap terminal-record residue from re-accreting in `threads.json` — the write-amplification → Spotlight `mds_stores` → Jetsam class the health surface measures. +1 test (old reaped → compacted; recent closed → kept; ancient ACTIVE → never touched). Verified: `go test -race ./internal/router` green, `golangci-lint` 0 issues. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27/A28, ADR-024/025; router 20260609-052000; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Health surface: Jetsam/panic trend-vs-transient classification (flagship Rail C — read-only surfacing)** (claude-pantheon, routed by claude-home, 2026-06-09). `sirsi diagnose`'s crash-log check (`internal/guard/doctor.go`) previously treated any Jetsam/panic count in a flat 7-day window as Critical, so a single bad afternoon screamed the same as a week-long problem — alert fatigue, and the SessionStart fail-loud line cried wolf. Now the check buckets events by calendar day and distinguishes a **transient spike** (clustered in <3 days → `Warn`) from a **sustained trend** (events across ≥3 of the last 7 days → `Critical`), with the finding carrying `trend`/`activeDays` fields and a message that says which (`"9 Jetsam kills across 6 of 7 days — sustained trend"` vs `"…clustered in 1 day — transient spike, watch for a trend"`). Because the SessionStart health line + fail-loud hook already gate on severity≥2, escalation now fires **only on trends** — no hook change needed, the gating lives in one place. Crash-log scanning is now injectable (`crashEventScanFn`, Rule A16) so the classifier is unit-tested without real host crash logs. This is the foundation slice of the health→cause→one-click-remediation flagship (binary-drift + Spotlight remediations follow behind A19/A1 confirm-gates); pure read-only, zero system mutation. Dogfooded live: this host shows a real sustained Jetsam trend (6/7 days), correctly Critical. Verified: `go test -race ./internal/guard` green (+3 trend tests), `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `go build ./...` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A12, A16; router 20260609-033900/033815; Changelog: Unreleased.- **`sirsi continue` (alias `sirsi resume`) — surface the continuation prompt on one command** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). Enables the SessionStart hook to auto-surface resume context (follow-up). Verified by running: `continue`/`resume` print the prompt, error path is clean. ### Fixed
- **Reaper drains pid≤1 phantom thread records (A28 pid-sanity-floor)** (claude-pantheon, routed by claude-home, 2026-06-09). `ReapDeadThreads` previously `continue`d past any record with `PID < minAgentPID` ("unverifiable PID"), so the pid=0/1 phantoms the SessionStart empty-PID mint produced were never reaped — they accreted forever and drove the recurring registry-police A27 "phantom pid=0/os=unknown" alarms. A PID below `minAgentPID` (0/1 = kernel/launchd) can never be a live agent process, and the `(pid, start_time)` reap-key can't catch a PID that was never a real process. The reaper now retires such records to terminal `reaped` — but **only once they are also past the stale window** (`DefaultThreadStaleAfter`), so a legitimately pid-less surface that is actively heartbeating is never mistaken for a phantom. Strictly conservative: it can only reap records that are BOTH pid≤1 AND not heartbeating (never a live thread). Pairs with the per-resume mint root fix (#24, stops new phantoms) and terminal-record compaction (#25, GCs them once reaped) to make the registry trustworthy end-to-end. +1 test (stale pid=0 + pid=1 reaped; fresh pid-less surface preserved). Verified: `go test -race ./internal/router` green, `golangci-lint` 0 issues. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27, ADR-022/024; router 20260609-052000; Changelog: Unreleased.
### Fixed
- **Surface-agnostic loop-evidence ends the registry-police "not-looping" false alarms (A28, completes the registry-trust foundation)** (claude-pantheon, routed by claude-home, 2026-06-09). The registry-police trusts the `.stale` field from `sirsi thread list --json` (it deliberately doesn't reinvent heartbeat math), and that field was `thread.IsStale` = `now.Sub(LastSeenAt) > window`, refreshed only by `thread heartbeat`/`RegisterThread`. So a process surface whose watcher loop is actively running but whose heartbeat is harness-gated (CLI sessions, mid-long-turn) aged out of the 5-min window and got flagged "registered-but-not-looping" — the recurring daily A27 false alarm. New `router.EffectiveStale` (used by `thread list`) treats a thread as stale only when its heartbeat aged out **AND** no live watcher loop exists for it (`router.WatcherAlive` = `pgrep -f thr-<id>`, the same identity ADR-024 arms / ADR-022 reaps on); non-process surfaces fall back to heartbeat freshness. **Write-free by design (option 2, claude-home-nodded)**: it does NOT bump `LastSeenAt` on every inbox tick (option 1), which would add a `threads.json` write per agent per ~60s — re-introducing the exact `mds_stores` → Jetsam write-storm the health surface (Rail B) exists to eliminate. Also adds the reap-key-wired regression test: a record whose PID is recycled onto a different process (same pid, different `start_time`) mints a fresh thread rather than adopting the stale one (proving the `(pid, start_time)` composite is consumed). With this, the trustworthy-registry foundation is complete end-to-end: #24 (per-resume mint root) → #29 (pid-sanity-floor) → #25 (terminal compaction) → loop-evidence. `crashEventScanFn`-style injectable probe (A16). Verified: `go test -race ./internal/router` green, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `thread list --json` dogfooded. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A16, A27, ADR-022/024; router 20260609-061000/061500; Changelog: Unreleased.

### Added- **`sirsi-gemma` — MCP server exposing local MLX-Gemma to any MCP client** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-08). New binary at `cmd/sirsi-gemma/` that speaks MCP over stdio and registers two tools — `gemma_chat` (multi-turn, system prompt + history rendered into Gemma's `<start_of_turn>` template) and `gemma_complete` (single-shot raw-prompt completion) — backed by a `python -m mlx_lm.generate` subprocess against a locally-installed Gemma 2 27B 4-bit (per chip A's `docs/setup/MLX_GEMMA_LOCAL.md`). Reuses `internal/mcp.Server` for framing/dispatch (zero re-implementation of JSON-RPC plumbing). Subprocess hidden behind an injectable `Runner` interface (Rule A16) so unit tests don't need a live Python install. Startup runs a 1-token health probe; failure swaps in `disabledRunner` so the MCP handshake still works and every tool call returns an actionable error pointing the operator at the setup doc — discoverable from Claude Code even when Gemma is misconfigured. Config at `~/.config/sirsi/gemma.toml` (flat key=value, zero external deps); defaults match chip A's install. Static binary per A3 (`CGO_ENABLED=0` cross-compiles green for darwin/arm64, linux/amd64, windows/amd64); no telemetry per A11. Ships: `cmd/sirsi-gemma/README.md` (dev-facing, A8), `docs/user-guides/sirsi-gemma.md` (user-facing, A8, honest about Gemma's depth limits vs Claude per A23), `docs/setup/MCP_CONFIG_SIRSI_GEMMA.md` (copy-pasteable `~/.claude/mcp.json` snippet). Verified: `go build`/cross-compile green, `go test -race` green (9 tests), `golangci-lint` 0 issues. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A3/A6/A8/A11/A16/A23/A28; Changelog: Unreleased.
### Fixed
- **Scan truthfulness, completed — 5 remaining AI cache rules were still unguarded** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). The first truthfulness pass env-guarded the six AI rules in `ai.go`, but `internal/jackal/rules/expanded.go` had **five more `CategoryAI` rules still registered in `AllRules()` as bare `baseScanRule`** with no liveness guard — `onnx_cache`, `vllm_cache` (compiled kernels, hours to rebuild), `jax_cache`, `stable_diffusion` (frequently multi-GB, actively used), `langchain_cache` — so on any machine with those tools the scan still over-reported actively-used caches as reclaimable. All five now carry `minAgeDays=30`; the three whose runtimes expose a canonical cache-dir env var additionally wrap in `envGuardedRule` with the **real** var (`ORT_CACHE_DIR`, `VLLM_CACHE_ROOT`, `JAX_COMPILATION_CACHE_DIR`) — no fabricated env vars for Stable Diffusion / LangChain, which have none (A23: don't imply support we lack; the mtime guard alone protects those). Also fixed a silent guard-defeating bug in `ai_liveness.go`: `liveTargetsFromEnv` called `ExpandPath(val, "")` with an **empty home**, so a common `~`-prefixed value like `HF_HOME=~/.cache/huggingface` expanded to a *relative* path that never matched an absolute finding — the live cache was reported as waste anyway; now resolves via `os.UserHomeDir()`.
