- **SessionStart hook stops minting a new thread every resume — the registry-accretion root** (claude-pantheon, routed by claude-home, 2026-06-09). The router supervisor hook (`.claude/hooks/router_inbox_check.py`) anchors a session's thread identity on `(agent_id, pid)` and adopts the existing record on each wakeup. But `claude_session_pid()` resolved that pid by a **fixed ancestry depth** (grandparent of the hook script) — and the launch chain (`claude → … → shell → python3`) carries a **variable number of ephemeral wrapper shells, a fresh one per hook fire** — so the lookup returned a *different* pid every resume. The `(agent_id, pid)` adopt therefore never matched and a fresh thread + watcher was minted on every SessionStart: ~130-record registry accretion, daily false A27 alarms, duplicate watchers, and the write-churn that itself feeds the `mds_stores` → Jetsam loop the health surface (Rail C/B) measures. **The register code's idempotent fast-path was already correct** (reuse live `(agent_id, pid≥minAgentPID)` with a `(pid,start_time)` composite) — the hook was the driver, passing an unstable pid. Fix: `claude_session_pid()` now **walks the parent chain to the actual `claude` process** (basename match), yielding the stable per-session identity regardless of how many wrapper shells sit between; returns `None` (caller falls back to freshness) only if no `claude` ancestor is found — never a wrong/ephemeral pid. Cycle-guarded, walk-capped, exec injectable for tests. +5 tests incl. the load-bearing one: *different ephemeral shells across two resumes both resolve to the same claude pid* (so adopt matches, zero mint). All 24 hook tests green (`python3 .claude/hooks/router_inbox_check_test.py`). This is the foundation fix that closes the loop with the flagship — fixing the churn makes the host measurably healthier, which Rail C's thermometer then shows. Locks with 4 tests (structural guard presence, real pin-var wiring, tilde→absolute resolution, behavioral pin-suppression). Verified: `go build ./...`, `go test -race ./internal/jackal/...` green, `golangci-lint` 0 issues. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A23 (scan truthfulness), A16; PR #21 review (claude-home advisory); Changelog: Unreleased.- **Menubar stops re-asking for Full Disk Access on every rebuild** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). The menubar "kept asking for FDA" and looked like duplicate apps because a plain `go build` ad-hoc-signs `sirsi-menubar` with a **content-hash-derived identifier** (`sirsi-menubar-<hash>`) — macOS TCC keys Full Disk Access on the code-signing identifier, so **every rebuild/reinstall minted a new identity → a fresh FDA prompt**, and the dev binary's identity never matched the `/Applications/Pantheon.app` bundle (`ai.sirsi.pantheon`) → two TCC entries for one app. `InstallMenubar` now re-signs the binary with the **stable identifier `ai.sirsi.pantheon`** (best-effort, ad-hoc, darwin-only, idempotent) before loading the LaunchAgent, so one FDA grant persists across reinstalls and the dev binary shares the bundle's TCC identity. Operationally cleaned up the live duplicates (killed churning instances, re-signed, reloaded one clean instance). Aligns with feedback_permissions_in_install (FDA is install-time, not mid-use). Verified: `go build`, `go vet`, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `go test ./internal/setup` green; live menubar re-signed to `Identifier=ai.sirsi.pantheon`. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A19 (no bundle mutation — bundle untouched), feedback_permissions_in_install; Changelog: Unreleased.- **Two more command-completion arcs — `sirsi permissions` and `sirsi quickstart` now end cleanly** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). Continues the command-completion arc (builds on the `SilenceUsage`/`SilenceErrors` root fix). (1) **`sirsi permissions`** previously trailed off with a bare `Opening System Settings...` line and exited — no result, no next-step, no confirmation it had done anything (it did not use the `output.CommandResult` contract every other command renders). Now it closes with a `CommandResult`: a one-line summary ("Add the path above to Full Disk Access, then re-run a scan to confirm"), evidence (the exact binary path + whether System Settings opened, or a manual-path warning on failure), and next actions (`sirsi scan`, `sirsi setup`); the non-macOS branch likewise returns a clean `CommandResult` instead of a stray `fmt.Println`. (2) **`sirsi quickstart`** recommended `sirsi judge --dry-run` / `--confirm` as its "safe to clean" next step — but `judge` is a **hidden legacy alias** (`judgeCmd.Hidden = true`), invisible in `sirsi --help`, so the guided first-run pointed new users at a command they can't discover; switched to the canonical `sirsi clean` (preview by default) / `sirsi clean --confirm`. Audited all 28 top-level commands in a safe non-interactive preview pass; the rest already complete with a visible result + next-step. Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./cmd/sirsi/`, `golangci-lint run ./cmd/sirsi/` (0 issues), `go test ./cmd/sirsi/` green; `permissions`/`quickstart` re-run and confirmed to end with a clear outcome. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10 (terminal UX), feedback_menubar_broken (test real flows), Rule 0/A12 (additive); Changelog: Unreleased.- **Every command now visibly resolves — failed commands read as failures, not help dumps** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-09). Root cause of "most commands don't seem to work or complete": `rootCmd` had neither `SilenceUsage` nor `SilenceErrors`, so **any** `RunE` error made cobra print the full `Usage:` block — a failed command looked like it "just printed help and did nothing." Set both on root and render the failure as one clean styled line in `main()` (`output.Error`, JSON-aware), so all 28 commands now end with an unambiguous outcome. Two concrete completion-arc fixes on top: (1) **`sirsi risk`** outside a git repo printed `osiris: not a git repository: exit status 128` + a usage dump → now a single actionable line ("risk assessment needs a git repository — run inside a project, or pass a repo path"); (2) **`sirsi duplicates`** with no path defaulted to hashing all of `$HOME` (3-phase hash → minutes, looked hung with a spinner and no completion) → now defaults to the **current directory** (scoped, completes promptly, names the target and hints `pass a path to widen`); the result render (Summary + Evidence + NextActions) was already correct, only the default scope was wrong. Verified by running each: `risk`/`duplicates` exit cleanly with a visible result; `go vet`, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `go test ./cmd/sirsi ./internal/osiris ./internal/mirror` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A10 (terminal UX), feedback_menubar_broken (test real flows), Rule 0; Changelog: Unreleased.
### Added- **`sirsi-gemma` — MCP server exposing local MLX-Gemma to any MCP client** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-08). New binary at `cmd/sirsi-gemma/` that speaks MCP over stdio and registers two tools — `gemma_chat` (multi-turn, system prompt + history rendered into Gemma's `<start_of_turn>` template) and `gemma_complete` (single-shot raw-prompt completion) — backed by a `python -m mlx_lm.generate` subprocess against a locally-installed Gemma 2 27B 4-bit (per chip A's `docs/setup/MLX_GEMMA_LOCAL.md`). Reuses `internal/mcp.Server` for framing/dispatch (zero re-implementation of JSON-RPC plumbing). Subprocess hidden behind an injectable `Runner` interface (Rule A16) so unit tests don't need a live Python install. Startup runs a 1-token health probe; failure swaps in `disabledRunner` so the MCP handshake still works and every tool call returns an actionable error pointing the operator at the setup doc — discoverable from Claude Code even when Gemma is misconfigured. Config at `~/.config/sirsi/gemma.toml` (flat key=value, zero external deps); defaults match chip A's install. Static binary per A3 (`CGO_ENABLED=0` cross-compiles green for darwin/arm64, linux/amd64, windows/amd64); no telemetry per A11. Ships: `cmd/sirsi-gemma/README.md` (dev-facing, A8), `docs/user-guides/sirsi-gemma.md` (user-facing, A8, honest about Gemma's depth limits vs Claude per A23), `docs/setup/MCP_CONFIG_SIRSI_GEMMA.md` (copy-pasteable `~/.claude/mcp.json` snippet). Verified: `go build`/cross-compile green, `go test -race` green (9 tests), `golangci-lint` 0 issues. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A3/A6/A8/A11/A16/A23/A28; Changelog: Unreleased.
### Removed
- **Deleted the dead push-model router cluster — −2,626 LOC** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-08; codex-arch-approved `044213`, surgically corrected after the wholesale-delete attempt broke the build). Rule A26/A27 retired the push-model router (pull-model CLI, no daemon verbs), but the implementation lingered, reachable only from its own tests. Removed `internal/router/{daemon,executor,runner,ledger,smoke,wake}.go` + their tests, and the dead launchd-install funcs in `service.go` (`RenderLaunchAgentPlist`/`InstallLaunchAgent`/`Launchctl`/`xmlEscape`). **Preserved** the live symbols that were interleaved in the same files — the `Wake{CLISpawn,APICall,MCPNotification}` surface-classification constants (moved to a focused `wakemechanism.go`) and the pull-model service helpers `ResolveStableBinary`/`DefaultServiceOptions`/`IsGoRunBinary`/`LaunchAgentProgram` (+ `serviceSlug`/`xmlUnescape`) used by node-status/register. The build gate caught that a file-level delete would have removed those live symbols — so this is an extraction, not an `rm`. Preserved `notify.go` `NotifyAgent` (live via MCP). Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, full `go test -short ./...` (48 pkgs) green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A26/A27, ADR-024, Rule 0; Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Dropped 5 redundant standalone deity binaries — ~14 MB off the release package** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-06). The release shipped `sirsi-anubis`, `sirsi-guard`, `sirsi-maat`, `sirsi-scarab`, and `sirsi-thoth` as separate ~2.7–3.2 MB binaries — but `anubis`/`guard`/`maat`/`scarab` were **non-functional help-only stubs** (`Run = cmd.Help()`, zero subcommands) and `thoth` was a strict **subset** of `sirsi thoth`. Every deity is fully reachable as a `sirsi <deity>` subcommand (`sirsi maat audit`, `sirsi thoth sync`, `sirsi anubis scan`, `sirsi guard`/`sirsi monitor`, `sirsi net`), so removing the standalone binaries loses **zero** functionality and aligns with ADR-005 ("Pantheon is the product; deities are sub-systems"). Deleted `cmd/{anubis,guard,maat,scarab,thoth}` (148 LOC of stubs + thoth's duplicate) and their entries in `.goreleaser.yaml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `scripts/build-windows-installer.sh`, `scripts/windows-installer.nsi`. The release now ships `sirsi` (15 MB stripped) + `sirsi-agent` only. Evidence: each `cmd/<deity>/main.go` vs its `sirsi` subcommand; size measured per-binary. Verified: `go build ./...` green, all `sirsi <deity>` subcommands intact. Refs: ADR-005, Rule 0 (minimal code), A14 (verifiable numbers); Changelog: Unreleased.

### Added
- **`sirsi setup` auto-arms the 𓆄 Ma'at pre-push gate** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-05). The repo ships a pre-push gate (`.githooks/pre-push`: gofmt + go vet + golangci-lint matching CI + diff tests — Rule A17, Ma'at the QA Sovereign), but git only runs it when `core.hooksPath` points at `.githooks`; a fresh clone defaults to `.git/hooks` (empty), so the gate ships **disarmed** — exactly how a govet shadow recently slipped to CI and nearly rode through a merge. `setup.ArmMaatGate()` sets `core.hooksPath=.githooks` for the current source clone (idempotent; skips cleanly outside a clone that ships the gate, so end-user `brew` installs are unaffected); wired into setup Step 4 (cross-platform, unlike the darwin-only supervisor). New `SurfaceMaatGate` for a uniform install report. Self-proven: this change's own push was gated by the newly-armed gate (`✅ Pipeline gate passed`). Division of labor (codex router `230044`): setup auto-arm = claude-pantheon; `thoth_sync` git-128 cwd bug = codex source-audit lane. Verified: `go build`, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `go test ./internal/setup` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A17 (Ma'at QA Sovereign), A6 (CI gate); Changelog: Unreleased.
- **Resident Horus agent-router supervisor is live — one LaunchAgent replaces per-thread heartbeat glue** (claude-pantheon + codex-pantheon, 2026-06-05). Integrated the two parallel-built halves: codex's `sirsi horus supervise` engine (`internal/router/supervisor.go` — `SuperviseOnce`/`SuperviseReport`: inventory `agents.json`, refresh a resident CTR thread, heartbeat, pull local inboxes, classify each surface `wakeable`/`stale`/`blocked`/`manual`; read-model only, no dispatch/merge) wired into the CLI (`cmd/sirsi/horus.go` — `--once` + resident `--interval` loop, default 60s, SIGTERM/Interrupt + context cancel, thread reuse across ticks, JSON mode), guarded by claude's install side (`internal/setup.InstallSupervisor()` → LaunchAgent `ai.sirsi.horus.agent-router`, repo-root WorkingDirectory, installed by `sirsi setup` Step 4). One resident process now does inventory + register + 60s-heartbeat + inbox-pull for every locally-owned agent — the durable codex↔claude continuous-loop fix replacing the Monitor + `/tmp` shell loops (Rule A27). Verified live: `go build`/`vet`/tests green both packages; reloaded LaunchAgent runs (PID stable, empty stderr), registers its own thread, reports `status=active live=85 stale=1`, and surfaces each agent's pending inbox. No `/Applications` writes (Rule A19). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A26/A27, ADR-024; Changelog: Unreleased.

### Fixed
- **Supervisor LaunchAgent exports an explicit PATH so the wakeability probe resolves agent CLIs** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-05). On first go-live the supervisor classified every claude/codex agent as `blocked — claude not found in PATH`: launchd hands the LaunchAgent a minimal PATH, and `/bin/zsh -l` (non-interactive login) sources `.zprofile`/`.zshenv` but **not** `.zshrc`, where `~/.local/bin` (home of the agent CLIs) is added — so `exec.LookPath("claude")` failed and every surface was under-reported. Fix: emit an `EnvironmentVariables`/`PATH` key leading with the sirsi binary's own directory (agent CLIs install beside it), then `~/.local/bin`, package-manager prefixes, and system dirs (de-duped). Verified live: reloaded LaunchAgent flips claude-pantheon from `blocked` to `wakeable pending=5`. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27, A19; Changelog: Unreleased.

## [0.23.1-beta] — 2026-06-05

### Fixed
- **`sirsi clean` is now functional — the documented `--confirm` path works and actually cleans** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-05). The top-level `clean` was a *second, divergent engine* (`runClean`, positional `safe/all`, **no `--confirm` flag**) — so the product's own printed next-step `sirsi clean --confirm` returned `unknown flag`, and even bare `sirsi clean` was a no-op (`cleanupApplyPaused()` "demo safety" brake). Rewired `sirsi clean` onto the one A1-correct engine (`runJudge`): preview by default, `--confirm` to apply (interactive `[y/N]`, trash-first), `--include-caution` for scope (preview = apply, Rule A1), `clean all` kept as a back-compat alias. Deleted the dead `runClean` (Rule 0) and removed the demo brake from the clean path — the real safety is scan-derived findings + protected paths + `[y/N]` + trash-first, not an env gate. Verified by running: `sirsi clean` → 23 items/4.0 GB preview; `sirsi clean --confirm` → `[y/N]` gate; `sirsi clean all` → 9.3 GB caution scope; `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `go test -run Clean|Judge` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1 (PARAMOUNT), ADR-016; Changelog: Unreleased.

### Fixed
- **Extension package footprint no longer tracks generated binaries** (codex-pantheon, 2026-06-05). Removed the stale 21 MB `extensions/vscode/sirsi` embedded CLI copy and the generated Gemini bridge `.vsix` from source control; extension code already resolves the configured/installed `sirsi` binary instead of using an embedded executable. Added ignore guards for future extension VSIX artifacts and embedded CLI copies. Verified: package audit with `git ls-files` and extension source reference search. Refs: package/service audit, Rule 0/A12; Changelog: v0.23.
- **PID sanity floor for router reaping and orphan cleanup** (codex-pantheon, 2026-06-05). PID 1 is now treated as below the valid agent/process floor: router liveness reports it as unverifiable, thread reaping skips it instead of applying composite reap-key logic to launchd/init, and orphan scanning excludes PID 1 before it can appear in `sirsi fix` preview or kill candidates. Verified: `go test ./internal/router ./internal/guard ./cmd/sirsi`. Refs: router backlog `20260605-184302`, ADR-024 Amendment 1, Rule A1/A12; Changelog: v0.23.
- **Router LaunchAgent inventory now matches the pull-model CLI** (codex-pantheon, 2026-06-05). `NodeStatus` now reports known macOS helpers (`ai.sirsi.pantheon`, `com.sirsi.idea-router`, sweep, registry-police, and the legacy router-daemon label) through `launch_agents`, `sirsi router node-status` renders them as Router/App helpers instead of a misleading missing daemon, `sirsi setup --json` includes the same launch-agent inventory on macOS, and watcher specs/canon docs no longer tell headless surfaces to run the removed `sirsi router daemon` verb. Verified: `go test ./internal/router ./internal/setup ./cmd/sirsi`. Refs: ADR-024, ADR-026, router handoff `20260605-183532`; Changelog: v0.23.
- **Install/workstream drift hardening** (codex-pantheon, 2026-06-05). `sirsi setup --json` now works as a cross-platform read-only status report before the macOS-only interactive guard, MCP registration writes the resolved `sirsi` binary path instead of relying on IDE PATH, Codex workstream auto-approve uses the current CLI bypass flag, release scripts stamp `internal/version.Version`, and `agentguard` tests no longer fail because host crash logs are present. Verified: `go test ./internal/setup ./internal/workstream ./internal/agentguard ./cmd/sirsi`, `go vet ./...`, `go build ./cmd/sirsi ./cmd/sirsi-menubar ./cmd/sirsi-gui`. Refs: workstream/setup audit, ADR-023, Rule A7/A12; Changelog: v0.23.

## [0.23.0-beta] — 2026-06-04

> Cuts as **v0.23** per Codex review `20260521-codex-pantheon-tui-elimination-phase0-review`. Interactive surface direction reopened on 2026-05-29 (ADR-020) and closed as **Hybrid C**: a new Mole-grade TUI ships first cross-platform; Mac native follows later as the polish-bar upgrade. The `v1.0-alpha.0` slot now belongs to the first installable TUI cut, not the first Mac app.

### Added
- **The Mac GUI is now a real app — `cmd/sirsi-gui`** (claude-home, 2026-06-05). The "Ferrari" surface from ADR-015 existed only on paper. Built it Go-native per Critical Design Decision #2 (GUI = Go HTTP + embedded HTML, no Electron/React): a native macOS window (`github.com/webview/webview_go` → WKWebView) over the existing `internal/dashboard` server. One more face over the same engine — if the menubar is already serving the dashboard on the fixed port, the GUI **reuses** it; otherwise it starts its own. `sirsi surface use gui` launches it; `gui` and `menubar` are now distinct surfaces. macOS-only (darwin build file + a `!darwin` stub so `go build ./...` and the Linux CI stay green without pulling WebKit/cgo onto Linux). Verified: `go build ./...` (darwin, 17 MB arm64 Mach-O) **and** `CGO_ENABLED=0 GOOS=linux` stub cross-compile both green. Honest scope: the window renders the live dashboard; richer GUI chrome and shipping it in the DMG are follow-ups. Refs: ADR-015 (deity hierarchy / Ferrari GUI), CDD #2, feedback_go_standard; Changelog: v0.23.
- **TUI now renders live workstation data, not fixtures** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-05). `sirsi tui` builds its views from real router state: a **Threads** view (active threads from `LoadThreadRegistry`, filtered to genuinely-live nodes — verified 5) and a **Router Inbox** view (open `items/` read the same way `sirsi router pull` does, via `internal/work` — verified 33). New `tui.NewView` constructor + `tui.RunViews` seam feed real data through the same AppState/Reduce/Renderer contract the fixtures used; falls back to fixtures when the router can't be resolved. Follow-up: refresh-on-`r` reload + a live Scan view (needs the jackal engine wired). Verified: `go build ./...`, `go test ./internal/tui ./cmd/sirsi` green. Refs: ADR-016, ADR-024 (one inbox), feedback_go_standard; Changelog: v0.23.
- **The TUI is now a real, runnable app — `sirsi tui`** (claude-pantheon, 2026-06-05). The surface picker was offering a TUI that didn't exist: `internal/tui/` was a rendering *contract* (AppState model + pure `Reduce` + `Renderer`) with nothing to launch it. Added the live Bubbletea (`charm.land/bubbletea/v2`) event loop — `internal/tui/program.go` — a thin adapter over that Elm-shaped contract: keypress → registry → `Reduce` → re-render; `tab` cycles views, `q` quits, resize reflows. New `sirsi tui` command + `sirsi surface use tui` launches it in-terminal. Renders the three canonical views (Scan, Ra Fleet, Router Inbox). Tested without a terminal (`internal/tui/program_test.go`: real content renders, resize applied, view cycling, quit wired). Honest scope: views are fixture-backed for now — wiring live scan/router data is the next iteration; this is the runnable foundation, not the final data layer. Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `go test ./internal/tui` green; runs + exits cleanly under a PTY. Refs: ADR-016 (TUI-as-session), ADR-020 (design proof — this builds on the shipped contract), feedback_go_standard; Changelog: v0.23.
- **Surface-selectable install: one engine, swappable faces (CLI / TUI / IDE; menubar by default)** (claude-home, 2026-06-04). The setup wizard now leads with a **multi-select surface picker** — install any combination of CLI, TUI, and IDE, and on macOS the **menubar is installed by default** (LaunchAgent `ai.sirsi.pantheon`). The selection/install/permission logic lives in the `internal/setup/` engine so **all three callers drive identical code**: the `scripts/install.sh` bootstrap, the macOS GUI installer, and `sirsi setup`. New surface model (`Surface`, `Selectable`, `Install`, `InstallMenubar`, `RegisterIDE` via `claude mcp add`, `MenubarBinaryPath`) + switching (`sirsi surface` / `sirsi surface use <cli|tui|gui|ide>`, persisted to `~/.config/sirsi/surface`). `scripts/install.sh` now also places `sirsi-menubar` from the release archive and hands off to `sirsi setup` when attached to a terminal (pipes print the next step instead). Flags: `sirsi setup --surfaces cli,ide` (non-interactive selection), `--install` (all), `--json`. IDE access is the existing `sirsi mcp` server registered into the AI IDE. Honest gap surfaced in-product: the live full-screen TUI app is ADR-020-gated, so `surface use tui` routes to the rich terminal for now. Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `go test ./internal/setup ./cmd/sirsi` green; surface picker, install, and switch smoke-tested. Note: shipping `sirsi-menubar` in releases needs a goreleaser darwin/CGO build entry (release-pipeline follow-up). Refs: ADR-005 (Pantheon is the product), ADR-010 (menubar), ADR-016 (TUI-as-session), feedback_lean_ethos; Changelog: v0.23.
- **`sirsi setup` is now a guided first-run wizard, backed by a shared `internal/setup/` engine** (claude-home, 2026-06-04). Turns the old dependency *report* into a step-by-step flow — **Step 1 Dependencies → Step 2 Full Disk Access → Step 3 Agent wake** — ending in a clear "Pantheon is ready / setup incomplete" verdict. In a real terminal it prompts before each action (install a missing tool, open the FDA pane); non-interactive contexts (pipe, file, `/dev/null`, CI) fall back to a report and never open System Settings or block. One engine, two surfaces (Critical Design Decision #5): the menubar config row already spawns a terminal running this same `sirsi setup`, so the CLI and the menubar-launched wizard can never drift. TTY detection uses `golang.org/x/term` (correctly treats `/dev/null` as non-interactive — `os.ModeCharDevice` did not). Flags: bare = guided, `--install` = non-interactive auto (install + open pane), `--json` = machine-readable status. Engine functions (`FullDiskAccessGranted`, `NeedsFDA`, `BinaryPath`, `RegisterAgentWake`) are now the single source of truth and feed `main.go`'s scan-command FDA pre-check too. Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `go test ./internal/setup ./cmd/sirsi ./internal/router` green; `setup --json` confirms 0 missing deps; report-mode and PTY-interactive smoke-tested. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md (Rule 0 minimal code, A23 advanced simplicity), [[feedback_permissions_in_install]] (permissions belong in first-run), [[feedback_lean_ethos]] (replace, don't accrete); Changelog: v0.23.
- **Both delivery paths carry the menubar + auto-run the wizard** (claude-home, 2026-06-04). (1) The menu bar app runs `sirsi setup` on **first launch** (one-time marker `~/.config/sirsi/.setup-launched`), so a DMG/GUI install literally drives the same surface+permission wizard as the CLI. (2) The macOS release job now builds + Developer-ID-signs a **standalone `sirsi-menubar_<ver>_darwin_arm64.tar.gz`** asset (the CGO/Cocoa menubar can't ride in the ubuntu-cross-compiled goreleaser archive). (3) `scripts/install.sh` on macOS fetches that asset when the menubar isn't already present — so the curl path ships the menubar too. Same Developer-ID cert as the DMG keeps FDA grants alive across updates. Additive to the working DMG step; `bash -n`, YAML parse, `go build ./...` green.
- For the VC build (Monday 2026-06-08): a freshly-downloaded `sirsi` now has a single, obvious first-run command that drives dependencies + permissions + agent wake to a verified "ready" state.

### Fixed
- **`sirsi setup --json` is now truly observational** (codex-pantheon, 2026-06-05). The JSON status mode promised "no prompts, no actions" but still called agent-wake registration, which could mutate `.agents/idea-router/agents.json` while producing a machine-readable report. Removed that side effect and corrected stale TUI surface text now that `sirsi tui` is a real app. Verified: `go test ./internal/setup ./internal/tui ./cmd/sirsi ./cmd/sirsi-menubar` and `go build ./...` green. Refs: setup review item `20260605-041031`, Rule A1/A12 trust boundary; Changelog: v0.23.
- **`sirsi setup` no longer reports three false "missing" dependencies** (claude-home, 2026-06-04). The dependency list looked up `thoth-init` / `thoth-sync` / `thoth-compact` as npm binaries, but Thoth ships *inside* the sirsi binary (`sirsi thoth`) — so a freshly-installed user was told they were missing tools that came in the same download. Removed the npm shims from `setup.Dependencies()`; a regression test guards against their return. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A14 (statistics/claims integrity); Changelog: v0.23.
- **`anubis clean` — `--confirm` no longer widens scope (Rule A1: preview must match apply)** (claude-home; demo-blocker found by codex-pantheon review, 2026-06-04). `--confirm` did double duty in `cmd/sirsi/anubis.go`: it both *applied* the clean AND *expanded* the target from `safe` to `safe+caution`. So the dry-run preview (`anubis clean`) showed the safe-only amount (e.g. 50 items / 3.5 GB) but `anubis clean --confirm` moved safe+caution (349 items / 8.4 GB) to Trash — the user (and the menubar's armed "Confirm Clean — X → Trash") saw an amount that did NOT match what got deleted. A latent A1 violation for CLI users too. Fix decouples *what* from *whether*: new `--include-caution` flag governs scope (applies to BOTH preview and apply); `--confirm` now means only "apply". Default target = `safe` for preview and apply alike → the shown amount is exactly what's trashed. Verified live: `anubis clean` = 50/3.5GB, `anubis clean --include-caution` = 349/8.4GB; `--confirm` now applies the safe-only previewed set. Paired with codex's `c63ade4` (menubar Confirm uses `--dry-run=false`, safe-only). `go build ./cmd/sirsi` + focused `go test ./cmd/sirsi -run Clean|Judge` + `golangci-lint` (0 issues) green; CLI rebuilt + re-signed + reinstalled. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1 (PARAMOUNT — dry-run must match apply), ADR-016; Changelog: v0.23.

### Added
- **Menubar "About Pantheon" — surfaces & integrations (the TUI surface view, in the menubar)** (claude-home; user 2026-06-04: "it needs to show how it links into Codex and Claude Code IDE… think back to the TUI version which shows all the surfaces it's installed in… that should be in the Pantheon About section"). New `addAboutSection` (cmd/sirsi-menubar/menu.go) renders an **About ▸** submenu: version + every surface Pantheon lives in — **𓂀 this menubar (active)**, the **CLI** path, and each detected **AI assistant / IDE** (Claude Code, Codex, VS Code, Cursor, Zed, Gemini) with ✓/✗ installed status via `workstream.ScanInventory` (the same inventory the TUI surface view uses). The **MCP bridge** row reports honest **linked vs available** (Rule A23/A14): `claudeMCPLinked` greps `~/.claude.json` / `~/.config/claude/mcp.json` / `~/.cursor/mcp.json` for the `"sirsi"` server registration → live machine shows **✓ linked to Claude Code**. A **⚙ Configure Integrations…** row routes to `sirsi setup`. Verified detection matches reality (claude+codex installed, MCP linked in ~/.claude.json). Also unified the on-disk install: purged stale/broken/bak `sirsi`/`sirsi-menubar` copies (`.broken-v021`, `.bak-pre-adr024`, `~/go/bin/*`) and refreshed every remaining PATH copy + /Applications/Pantheon.app to the current signed build (full removal of the brew-formula duplicate is a flagged post-demo `brew uninstall sirsi-pantheon`). `go build ./cmd/sirsi-menubar` + `go test ./cmd/sirsi-menubar` + `golangci-lint` (0 issues) green; bundled, deep-signed, redeployed, relaunched — no panic, registered. Refs: ADR-016 (menubar interface), ADR-015 (Horus = whole-workstation), PANTHEON_RULES.md A23/A14, feedback_contextual_insights; Changelog: v0.23.
- **Menubar redesigned into deity-grouped carrot sections with clickable event detail** (claude-home; user 2026-06-04: "every click and every view needs to lead to a complete activity… condensed into carotted sections that open when clicked… an event response shown for every action that is then clickable to read the discovery of detail"). The flat menubar became native macOS **submenus per deity** (𓂀 Horus·Ops, 🐺 Anubis·Hygiene, 𓆄 Ma'at·Quality, 𓁢 Thoth·Memory, 𓇶 Ra·Fleet, 𓋹 Insight) — each a collapsible caret that expands to its **clickable services**. New `cmd/sirsi-menubar/menu.go` owns the reusable UX primitives: `wire(item, handler)` (one goroutine per item — retires the monolithic 25-case `select`, lets submenus scale, Rule 0); `resultRow` + `newDeityResult` (a per-deity inline "↳ last result" row that fills with ✓/✗ + summary after any action and is **clickable to open the full discovery detail**); `runService` (safe-action runner: ⏳→✓/✗ feedback, records to Recent Activity, fills the result row); `openDetail` (writes the complete output to a temp file and `open`s it — no truncation, no TCC prompt). **Recent Activity** items are now clickable → the same detail viewer, so *every* action yields a drill-in response (no dead ends). Destructive flows keep their safety boundary (Rule A1): Clean = dry-run preview → armed Confirm (auto-disarms); Ra Deploy/Kill = Terminal. `onReady` reordered so infra (dashboard server, guard bridge, periodic scan, router registration) initializes before the wired handlers capture it; the app returns from `onReady` and lives on the systray runloop + the background refresh goroutine (stats, Ra scopes, recent activity, ops rows, FDA tier). Dead `runActionInPlace` removed (superseded by `runService`). `go build ./cmd/sirsi-menubar` + `go test ./cmd/sirsi-menubar` + `golangci-lint` (0 issues) green; bundled, deep-signed, deployed to /Applications/Pantheon.app, relaunched — no panic, registered with the router (proves `onReady` built the full menu). GUI click-through pending live verification (Menubar QA lesson). Refs: ADR-016 (menubar interface), ADR-026 (Horus ops surface), PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A10/A27, feedback_mole_quality/feedback_contextual_insights; Changelog: v0.23.
- **`sirsi diagnose` now sees userspace app crashes (App Crashes check + crashloop guard)** (claude-home; user 2026-06-03: a Codex-spawned Chrome SIGABRT'd and "pantheon neither saw it nor stopped it"). Root cause (two blind spots): `internal/guard/doctor.go` `checkRecentCrashLogs` only counted filenames containing `panic`/`JetsamEvent`, and only scanned the **system** `/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports` — but userspace aborts (`EXC_CRASH`/`SIGABRT`, e.g. `Google Chrome-…163345.ips`) match neither substring and land in the **user** `~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports`. So an aborting app was invisible to every check. New `checkAppCrashes` (9th check) scans both dirs (user + system), counts `.ips` reports that are neither panic nor Jetsam, ranks the worst offender, and runs `detectCrashloop` (≥3 aborts for one process within a 5-min sliding window ⇒ Critical "crashloop — stop the offending process"). **Surface + crashloop guard only — never auto-kills** a process it doesn't own (user-chosen scope; Rule A1/A5: killing a third-party app like Codex's Chrome would be a safety violation). Injectable dir provider (`appCrashDirsFn`, Rule A16) → fully unit-tested with `t.TempDir()`: name parsing (spaces/hyphens), 7-day cutoff via `os.Chtimes`, Jetsam exclusion, crashloop window. Live proof: diagnose went 8→9 checks and surfaced `App Crashes (7d): 4 — top: "Google Chrome" (2)`, honestly dropping the score 80→70 to reflect crashes that were always occurring but unseen. `go build ./cmd/sirsi` + `go test ./internal/guard` + `golangci-lint` (0 issues) green; rebuilt + redeployed (freeze lifted by user for this fix). Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A5/A16/A17/A23, feedback_pantheon_health_surface (surface Jetsam/panics → extend to app crashes), ADR-015 (Horus = whole-workstation visibility); Changelog: v0.23.
- **Disk visibility as a spectrum (all / some / none) — surfaced in the menubar** (claude-pantheon; user 2026-06-03: "isn't that the point of sirsi/horus to see everything and remediate?" + "directory access… all vs some vs none"). A hygiene tool blind to protected folders isn't doing its job — and access isn't a boolean: macOS grants it in tiers (Full Disk Access = ALL; piecemeal per-folder TCC = SOME; nothing = NONE). New `internal/platform` detector (`fda.go` types + `fda_darwin.go` probe): `CheckDiskAccess()` returns a `DiskAccessLevel` (`AccessNone`/`AccessSome`/`AccessFull`) plus which probed locations are readable vs blind — full-gates (TCC.db / Mail / Safari, readable ⇒ FULL) and folder-gates (Desktop / Documents / Downloads, readable-without-full ⇒ SOME). `HasFullDiskAccess()` kept as the `AccessFull` convenience. The menubar surfaces the tier as a first-class item (`applyFDAState`): hidden at full, "◐ Partial Visibility — See Everything…" at some, "⚠ No Disk Access — Grant Access…" at none — re-checked each refresh so it disappears the instant access is granted; clicking opens System Settings → Privacy → Full Disk Access and records the binary path to add. Live probe confirmed the model (test-runner = SOME: Desktop/Documents/Downloads readable, TCC.db/Mail/Safari blocked). **Honest constraints (A23):** macOS forbids self-granting access (TCC) — the user toggles it once; and grants are keyed to the code-signature hash, so a rebuilt/ad-hoc-re-signed binary loses them — durable access needs a stable Developer-ID signature (Apple enrollment pending). This makes the gap visible + the grant one-click; the signing-stability blocker is a separate product finding routed to claude-home. `go build ./...` + `go test ./internal/platform` (incl. spectrum + native-trash) green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A23, feedback_permissions_in_install, ADR-015 (Horus = whole-workstation); Changelog: v0.23.

### Fixed
- **`make install` re-signs the binary in place — no more silent killed-on-exec** (claude-home; user 2026-06-03). On macOS arm64, `cp bin/sirsi $(INSTALL_DIR)/sirsi` over an existing signed binary leaves the AMFI cdhash/signature cache pointing at the old bytes, so the kernel SIGKILLs `sirsi` on the next `exec` (exit 137). Result: `sirsi` silently did nothing after every `make install` until a manual `codesign --force --sign -`, and it killed router heartbeat loops that shell the installed binary (A27 watcher binary drift). Fix: the `install` target now runs `codesign --force --sign - "$(INSTALL_DIR)/sirsi"` immediately after the `cp`, re-syncing the ad-hoc signature to the new bytes. Verified: `make install && sirsi version` → v0.22.0-beta, exit 0 (was 137); installed binary reports `Signature=adhoc`. Two-line Makefile change; `build`/`build-menubar` already ad-hoc-sign via `go build`, `bundle` already `--deep` signs Pantheon.app — `install` was the lone gap. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A6, reference_a27_watcher_binary_drift; Changelog: v0.23.
- **Trash without Finder — clean works from the menubar (no Automation permission)** (claude-pantheon; user 2026-06-03: clean "asks to open Finder then makes a noise like it's trying to open a file but it's being rejected"). Root cause: `internal/platform/darwin.go` `MoveToTrash` shelled `osascript → tell application "Finder" to delete`, which needs the **Automation TCC permission** a background launchd agent (the menubar) cannot obtain — so every in-app clean triggered the "control Finder" prompt and was rejected (the rejection sound). Fixed to a **native move into ~/.Trash** (`os.Rename`) — the app's own file operation, needing no Finder/Automation, so it works headless from the menubar; items stay fully recoverable from the Trash. Same-name collisions land under a timestamped name (no clobber); cross-volume rename (EXDEV) falls back to the original Finder path for interactive/Terminal contexts. New darwin-only tests (`darwin_trash_test.go`): moves-not-deletes, recoverable-from-Trash, no-clobber. `go test ./internal/platform` + `go build ./...` green. Safety-critical (the cleaner's destructive path) — routed to codex. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1, feedback_permissions_in_install (TCC belongs in setup); Changelog: v0.23.

### Added
- **Menubar cleans waste IN-APP — two-click dry-run→confirm** (claude-pantheon; user 2026-06-03: "pantheon shows eternal waste and no way to clean it in app"). The menubar title shows GB of reclaimable waste but the only Clean path opened a Terminal. Now it's in-app and Rule-A1-safe via a two-click flow that relocates the CLI's preview→[y/N] gate into the menu: **click "Clean Waste…"** runs `sirsi anubis clean` (dry-run — `--dry-run` defaults true, nothing deleted), records "N items (X GB) would be cleaned" to Recent Activity, and **arms** a hidden "Confirm Clean — X → Trash" item (auto-disarms after 25s so there's never a standing one-click delete); **click "Confirm Clean"** runs `anubis clean --confirm` with "y" piped to the interactive prompt (the click IS the yes) → trash-first removal (recoverable), protected paths enforced by `internal/cleaner/safety.go`. `cmd/sirsi-menubar/actions.go` `runCleanPreview`/`runCleanApply`. Rule A1 (PARAMOUNT) fully satisfied — dry-run always shown first, explicit second-click confirm, trash-first, no bypass. `go build` + `go test ./cmd/sirsi-menubar` green; deployed + verified the dry-run reports "14 items (3.6 GB) would be cleaned". Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A1, internal/cleaner/safety.go, ADR-016; Changelog: v0.23.
- **Menubar acts, not just informs — in-place actions** (claude-pantheon; user's #1 complaint 2026-06-03: "great information that doesn't resolve into any ACTION"). The menubar was a launcher — every click `osascript`-opened a Terminal running `sirsi <cmd>` (ADR-016 "all actions route through the TUI"). Now **safe (non-destructive) actions execute in-process and report the result**, no Terminal: clicking Thoth Sync / Ma'at Quality / Anubis Scan / Ghosts / Hardware / Risk / Net Align / Seshat / Ra Collect runs the command off the UI thread, gives the item transient `⏳`→`✓`/`✗` feedback, and writes the one-line result to the notify store (so it lands in Recent Activity). `cmd/sirsi-menubar/actions.go`: `runActionInPlace` (90s timeout, ANSI-stripped first-line summary, full output in notification Details) + pure helpers (`firstMeaningfulLine`/`stripANSI`/`truncate`, unit-tested). **Destructive actions keep the Terminal/confirm path** (Rule A1 — no one-click destruction): `clean` (deletes), `ra deploy`/`ra kill` (spawns/kills windows), `guard` (long-running watch); live-display (Stats) and dynamic (Ra scopes) items unchanged. Verified all wired command strings resolve to real `sirsi` subcommands (an earlier "broken command" reading was a test-quoting artifact, not reality — A23). `go build ./...` + `go test ./cmd/sirsi-menubar` green. **Deviates from ADR-016** (menubar becomes a light direct actor for safe ops, not purely a TUI launcher) — routed to claude-home/codex for arch review; GUI-verified live by the user. Refs: ADR-016 (interface), ADR-026 (ops surface), PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A10; Changelog: v0.23.
- **ADR-024 §6 — SessionStart stranded-work surfacing** (claude-pantheon; claude-home ruling `053800` ACCEPTED the challenge to the unsafe SessionEnd auto-stash; codex concurrence requested post-implementation as codex was offline). Closes the filesystem-state half of process-death handling: ADR-024/Amendment-1 reap REGISTRY state, but a sole-writer reaped mid-edit strands uncommitted WORKING-TREE changes (lived this session: the orphaned `register.go`, adopted as `72ff619`). **Rejected** the obvious SessionEnd `git stash` (repo-global → would stash every live agent's WIP = A26 cross-lane harm; no per-thread hunk attribution; removes-from-tree → silent loss). **Adopted** read-only surfacing instead: `sirsi thread reconcile` (already on the SessionStart path) now, when it freshly reconciles a reaped thread AND `git status --porcelain` is non-empty, emits a non-destructive `⚠ N uncommitted file(s) may be stranded from reaped thread(s) — review with git status; adopt or discard explicitly (never auto-stashed)` (+ `stranded_uncommitted` in `--json`). `countUncommitted` is read-only (`git -C status --porcelain`); no stash, no attribution guess, no mutation. A18 (incremental commits) is the real prevention — CTR reaping is PID-aggressive (no checkpoint grace), so source-editing sessions must checkpoint early/often; this surfacing is the safety net. `cmd/sirsi/threadsuspend.go`; `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean. Refs: docs/ADR-024-ONE-WATCHER-PER-SURFACE.md §6, ADR-025 (reconcile path), PANTHEON_RULES.md A18/A26/A1; Changelog: v0.23.
- **ADR-025 refinement — opt-in suspended-record retention** (claude-pantheon; dogfooded 2026-06-02, routed to codex). ADR-025 made `suspended` deliberately non-prunable (the reaper skips it, `PruneClosed` skips it) so multi-day NOTEBOOKS-style resumes survive — but that means orphaned suspends (e.g. churn artifacts, `pid=0`, never resumed) **accrete unbounded** in `threads.json`, the same A27 write-amplification → Spotlight `mds_stores` class the session targets (surfaced live: the new `router node-status` showed 7 orphan `pid=0` claude-pantheon suspends from one churny session). Fix is additive (Rule 15), not a contract change: `ThreadRegistry.PruneStaleSuspended(now, retention)` + `SuspendedRetention` const (7d default) remove suspended records whose suspend time (`SuspendPayload.SuspendedAt`, else `LastSeenAt`) is older than retention; `sirsi thread prune --suspended-older-than <dur>` exposes it **opt-in** — the default prune path still never touches suspended, so the resume-later guarantee holds for any recent pause. `retention<=0` is a no-op (never a blanket wipe). Test (`internal/router/adr025_test.go`): old-suspend-pruned (payload + LastSeenAt-fallback), recent-suspend-preserved, live/terminal untouched, default `PruneClosed` still skips suspended. `go test -race ./internal/router` green; `go build ./...` ok. Refs: docs/ADR-025-THOTH-GATED-EXIT.md, PANTHEON_RULES.md A27/A21/Rule 15; Changelog: v0.23.
- **ADR-026 step 4b (wiring) — menubar renders live Horus ops rows** (claude-pantheon, surface-chrome lane; unblocked by claude-home exporting `dashboard.Summarize` at `a86aeed`). Wires the shipped 4b rendering into the menubar: `onReady` adds a clickable lead row (`mOpsHeader` → opens the full dashboard/TUI) + 12 bounded agent rows; the 60s refresh loop reduces the same `NodeStatus` the in-process dashboard serves (`menubarNodeStatus()` → `dashboard.Summarize(ns, 12)`) and `SetTitle`s via the unit-tested `opsLeadRow`/`opsAgentRows`. One read-model, one producer — `menubarNodeStatus` is shared by the 4a endpoint (`NodeStatusFn`) and the 4b rows (no second source, no re-aggregation). Best-effort: an unresolved root / collect error leaves prior titles in place. `go build ./cmd/sirsi-menubar` + `go test ./cmd/sirsi-menubar` green. **Remaining done-gate:** GUI verification on a real menubar launch (Menubar QA lesson — clicks, not just builds); the data path (NodeStatus → Summarize → rows) is unit-tested + build-verified, but the live NSMenu render needs a GUI session to confirm. Refs: docs/ADR-026-HORUS-OPS-DASHBOARD.md, ADR-021, ADR-024 §1; Changelog: v0.23.
- **ADR-026 step 4b (rendering, partial) — menubar ops-row presentation** (claude-pantheon, surface-chrome lane; 4b deferral lifted by claude-home `034145`). Pure, unit-tested rendering of the Horus read-model into menubar strings (`cmd/sirsi-menubar/opsview.go`): `opsLeadRow(OpsSummary)` → worst-glyph + compact roll-up (live/stale/queued/suspended/fail/drift), `opsAgentRows(OpsSummary)` → indented `  <icon> <name> — <state>` per bounded agent (icon precedence needs-login 🔑 > stale 🟡 > healthy 🟢) + `+N more` overflow. No re-aggregation — consumes the canonical exported `dashboard.OpsSummary` (ADR-026 "one read-model, N read-only projections"). 3 tests `go test ./cmd/sirsi-menubar -run Ops` green. **Remaining for 4b:** (i) data wiring — needs `dashboard.Summarize` exported (currently unexported `dashboardSummarize`) so the menubar's 60s refresh loop can reduce its in-process `NodeStatus` without re-implementing or a loopback HTTP self-call (requested from claude-home, the read-contract lane); (ii) thin `systray` glue (onReady menu items + SetTitle in the refresh loop); (iii) GUI verification on a real launch (Menubar QA lesson — clicks, not just builds). Refs: docs/ADR-026-HORUS-OPS-DASHBOARD.md, PANTHEON_RULES.md A10/A27; Changelog: v0.23.
- **Menubar bounds its own registry growth — `pruneOwnStaleRecords`** (authored by reaped sibling thread `thr-82f130c223a1b3d7`; committed by claude-pantheon under claude-home authorization `033748` after the author thread was reaped mid-edit — orphaned-work adoption, A23 provenance preserved in the commit trailer). On each menubar launch, after registering, the resident surface reaps this host's dead PIDs (via the ADR-024 Amendment 1 composite `ReapDeadThreads`) then deletes its **own** terminal records (`menubarAgentID`-scoped, keeps the live thread) older than a 1h retention window — so a frequently-relaunched resident surface never accretes unbounded reaped rows in `threads.json` (the A27 write-amplification → Spotlight `mds_stores` class). Agent-scoped: never touches other agents' history. Best-effort: a prune failure never blocks launch. `go build ./cmd/sirsi-menubar` green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27/A21, ADR-022/ADR-024 Amendment 1; Changelog: v0.23.
- **ADR-026 step 4a (surface chrome) — menubar serves `/api/node-status` in-process** (claude-pantheon, surface-chrome lane handed off by claude-home `032339`). Wires `NodeStatusFn` into the menubar's embedded `dashboard.Config` (`cmd/sirsi-menubar/main.go`) so the resident menubar's in-process dashboard serves the ADR-026 ops read-model (`GET /api/node-status` [+ `?view=summary`]) — the producer the menubar previously left nil (503). Reuses the menubar's own `resolveRouterRoot()` (which carries the launchd `cwd=/` fallback, ADR-021) and derives the repo root for `router.CollectNodeStatus`; unresolved root → error → 503 (the designed graceful degrade, same as a nil `StatsFn`). Read-only, no destructive surface. Non-racing: `main.go` only, separate from the concurrent `register.go` idempotency fix. `go build ./...` + `go vet ./cmd/sirsi-menubar` clean. **Remaining (step 4b/5):** the NSMenu `OpsSummary` row rendering (glyph from `WorstIcon` + "Open full dashboard" affordance) sequences after the menubar `register.go` work lands and needs GUI verification; the TUI 4th pane (step 5) is deferred — `internal/tui/` is Gate-2 scaffold only (no launch path / no data-consuming panes per `docs/TUI_DESIGN_PROOF.md`), so a "4th pane" needs the live-data view infra behind the next TUI gate. Refs: docs/ADR-026-HORUS-OPS-DASHBOARD.md, ADR-021 (workstation-scoping), ADR-024 §1 (resident surface); Changelog: v0.23.
- **ADR-026 implemented (steps 1–3) — Horus ops-dashboard read endpoint + `sirsi router node-status` verb** (claude-home owns; design APPROVED by claude-pantheon `022950`; lane boundary `235419`/`235652`). Closes the canon/implementation gap where Rule A27 references `router node-status` — a verb that did not exist — and where the dashboard contract matrix had *Router ack → MISSING (no `/api/router/*`)*. **The gap was exposure, not computation**: `router.CollectNodeStatus()` already aggregates the whole operator read-model (agents + wake-health, queue, dispatch failures, live/stale threads w/ `os_state` per ADR-022, daemon + binary-drift per ADR-023, agent CLI auth) into one `NodeStatus` — but it was trapped in Go. **Step 1 (contract):** `router.NodeStatus` gains `SchemaVersion` (frozen at `1.0.0`; bumps only on breaking change, never on additive) — claude-pantheon caveat from the verdict. **Step 2 (endpoint):** `internal/dashboard/nodestatus.go` adds `GET /api/node-status` serving the typed `router.NodeStatus`, plus `?view=summary` returning a bounded `OpsSummary` projection (top-N=12 agents by pending+live signal, `more_agents` overflow row for the NSMenu budget — claude-pantheon caveat #2). The summary is a **pure reduction** of the same NodeStatus (every field derived; nothing sourced independently — the "one read-model" invariant applied to reads, like the frozen action contract applies to writes). New `NodeStatusFn NodeStatusCollector` producer hook in `dashboard.Config`; nil-safe 503 graceful-degrade. **Read-only**: zero `ConfirmGuard` surface, no method-gating, no side effects. **Step 3 (CLI):** `sirsi router node-status [--json]` (`cmd/sirsi/routernodestatus.go`) — `--json` shape is byte-identical to the HTTP body, so the menubar + TUI + scripts all consume the same bytes. Smoke run against the live registry surfaced 7 phantom `pid=0/os=unknown` records — *the exact ADR-024 Amendment 1 finding (3) class the verb now makes visible*. 5 tests `internal/dashboard/nodestatus_test.go` (full-contract serve, summary derivation + drift flag + needs-login projection, bounded truncation, 503 unwired, 500 collector-error) `go test -race ./internal/dashboard` green; `go build ./...` ok. **Boundary held** (no surface chrome touched): menubar rows + TUI 4th pane stay with claude-pantheon. Routed for codex arch-verify next. Refs: docs/ADR-026-HORUS-OPS-DASHBOARD.md, docs/HORUS_OPS_READMODEL_R4_INVENTORY.md, docs/user-guides/router-node-status.md; PANTHEON_RULES.md A8/A10/A22/A27/A26; Changelog: v0.23.
- **ADR-024 Amendment 1 implemented — worker-lifecycle gate + (pid, start_time) reap-key** (claude-pantheon sole writer, CLAIM `20260602-024522`; design APPROVED by claude-home `025217`; implementation routed to codex for review-of-code). Fixes two CTR registration-hygiene defects behind the registry churn. **(3) Reap-key = composite identity, not bare PID** — the highest-confidence systemic bug: `PIDStateOf(pid, startedAt)` now keys on `(pid, start_time)`, returning a new `PIDRecycled` state when a live PID's start signature differs from the one recorded at registration (the OS recycled the number onto a different process). `defaultPIDStart` reads `ps -o lstart=` (one cheap shell-out, same shape as the `stat=` probe; `""` on Windows/legacy → bare-PID fallback, zero regression). `Thread.StartTime` is captured at register; `ReapDeadThreads` and `RegisterThread`'s idempotency fast-path both key on the composite, so a recycled PID is reaped (not resurrected) and a re-register on a recycled PID mints fresh. **Critically, a genuinely live thread whose start still matches is never reaped** — the exact false-positive that retired live sessions this session. **(2) Worker-lifecycle gate** — `sirsi thread register` refuses one-shot `--print`/`-p` workers (injectable `oneShotProbe` + `ephemeralWorkerSkip`; no-op, not error): an ephemeral worker is neither an interactive nor a resident surface, so it must not enroll a persistent thread (the dominant phantom-record source). Finding (1)/menubar excluded per ruling `023813` (surface-chrome lock-holder owns it). New tests: `internal/router/adr024_amend_test.go` (composite-state matrix, recycled-reaped, live-survives, composite fast-path) + `cmd/sirsi/adr024_amend_test.go` (selective gate proves interactive surfaces still register). `go test -race ./internal/router ./cmd/sirsi` green; `go build ./...` ok; `start_time` capture smoke-verified via real `ps -o lstart`. Refs: docs/ADR-024-ONE-WATCHER-PER-SURFACE.md § Amendment 1, ADR-022 (OS-truth liveness, corrected), PANTHEON_RULES.md A27/A26/A16/A21; Changelog: v0.23.
- **ADR-025 implemented — Thoth-gated exit + resumable suspend (R3)** (claude-pantheon owns, router item `203859`; queued after the ADR-024 lane; codex round-2 *approve* against `9e74be4`). Completes A27's lifecycle (`register → heartbeat → suspend ⇄ resume → close`) so a thread can no longer vanish lossy. Core (`414142f`) landed the `suspended` status (resumable-but-not-live: prune skips it, the reaper skips it, `Heartbeat` rejects it, `RegisterThread` bypasses the live fast-path) + `SuspendThread`/`ResumeThread`. This lane adds the rest: **CLI verbs** `sirsi thread suspend` (`--self`/`--thread`, syncs Thoth first for a fresh `thoth_ref`, snapshots owned open items + resume prompt, stops the fs-watcher) and `sirsi thread resume` (restores owned items, prints the resume prompt, returns the ADR-024 watcher spec for re-arming) — `cmd/sirsi/threadsuspend.go`. **`ReconcileExits`** (`internal/router/threads.go`) is the authoritative SessionStart gate (ADR-025 §4): a **stale active** record (soft-exit/`clear`) heals in place active→suspended after a retro sync; a **reaped** record (hard kill) is never revived — if memory is recoverable a new suspended **successor** is minted carrying `reaped_from`, else an `UNRECOVERABLE` warning surfaces (never silent). Idempotent via `hasSuccessorFor` + a 24h lookback; host- and agent-scoped (each surface heals its own lineage). Exposed as `sirsi thread reconcile [--agent]`. **Hooks** (user-scope `~/.claude/settings.json`, ADR-024 §4 default-on, `SIRSI_SUPERVISOR=0` opt-out): new **`SessionEnd`** → `thoth sync` + `thread suspend --self` (best-effort, visible error — `SessionEnd` cannot block); **`SessionStart`** gains `thread reconcile --agent` (the guaranteed gate). 9 `internal/router` ADR-025 tests (6 core + 3 reconcile: stale-in-place, reaped-successor-then-warn, host/agent scoping) `go test -race` green; verbs smoke-tested register→suspend→resume→close on a throwaway thread. Note (codex): re-`register` matching a suspended record currently mints a fresh thread (per the shipped core test) rather than auto-adopting via resume — explicit `resume` is the supported path; flagged for review. Refs: docs/ADR-025-THOTH-GATED-EXIT.md, PANTHEON_RULES.md A27/A26/A22; ADR-024/ADR-022/ADR-014; Changelog: v0.23.
- **ADR-026 proposed — Horus ops-dashboard, one typed read-model for the operator surface** (claude-home owns the ops-view content lane; routed to claude-pantheon for review per the ratified lane boundary `20260601-235419`/`235652`). Realizes ADR-015 ("the dashboard **is** Horus") as a real operator command-center, distinct from the `internal/horus` code-graph. **Key finding: the gap is exposure, not computation** — `router.CollectNodeStatus()` already aggregates the entire operator read-model into one `NodeStatus` (agents+wake-health, router queue, dispatch failures, live/stale threads with `os_state` OS-truth liveness per ADR-022, daemon+binary-drift per ADR-023, agent CLI auth), but it is trapped in Go: **not** in the frozen dashboard contract (matrix row *Router ack → MISSING, no `/api/router/*`*), **no** CLI verb (Rule A27 canon references `router node-status`, a verb that does not exist), **no** surface render. ADR-026 promotes `NodeStatus` to a frozen additive contract served at typed **`GET /api/node-status`** (+ `?view=summary` → `OpsSummary` for the menubar), adds the **`sirsi router node-status [--json]`** verb, and defines menubar/TUI read-only *projections* of the one read-model (no surface re-aggregates — the action-contract principle applied to reads). **Challenges the resume framing's `/api/horus` name** (Rule A23): `/api/horus/*` is already the code-graph namespace, so the ops-view is `/api/node-status` (one name → one meaning). Read-only — zero destructive surface, nothing to confirm-gate. Boundary held: claude-home defines the read contract, claude-pantheon owns surface chrome. Also adds `docs/HORUS_OPS_READMODEL_R4_INVENTORY.md` (the R4 capability inventory — per-surface watcher specs from `watcherspec.go` + the read-model source/exposure ledger). Design-phase, no code until codex/claude-pantheon review. Refs: docs/ADR-026-HORUS-OPS-DASHBOARD.md, PANTHEON_RULES.md A27/A26/A22/A23/A19; ADR-015/020/022/023/024; Changelog: v0.23.
- **ADR-024 implemented — one watcher per surface, router-prescribed** (claude-home assigned, router item `200410`; A24 autonomy; commits `7c4cda5`/`10c5e93`/`9288534`). Kills the three-heartbeat accretion (auto fs-watcher + caffeinator + `/loop` Monitor) where only one mechanism actually woke the agent while all three churned CPU + Spotlight `mds_stores`. **Decision 2:** `sirsi thread register` stops auto-spawning the fs-watcher — it's now a pure handshake that RETURNS the canonical watcher via a new `internal/router` spec table (`watcherspec.go`, the R4 capability inventory in code): surface → `{type, mechanism, arm_instruction, heartbeat_interval_s, watches_inbox, resident}`. claude ⇒ `loop-monitor`; menubar/tui/IDE/macapp ⇒ resident `native-runloop` (not inbox workers, preserves ADR-020); codex/gemini/daemon surfaces mapped too. **Decisions 3+4 (supervisor hook):** retires the per-claude caffeinator + fs-watcher; `register --json` exposes `watcher.arm_instruction`; the hook does **check-then-arm on every SessionStart/wakeup** (F1) keyed on **OS truth `pgrep -f thr-<thread_id>`** — never TaskList (F2: falsely empty ⇒ duplicate) and never the shared `DIR=` loop body (collides with other agents' loops on a shared host — claude-deck's correction `838ad66`). `SIRSI_SUPERVISOR` gates it: default `on` = arming injection, `enforce` adds the Stop-gate backstop, `0` suppresses managed arming + Stop-gate while `register` still returns the spec. **Decision 5 (one inbox):** the reader already scanned `items/` only; the F3 root cause was sender guidance — `notify.go` now directs replies to `items/` via `router close --result` / `router send --type`, and `work.Item` gains a `type:` field (proposal/review/decision) so those collapse onto one addressed item instead of separate `reviews/`+`decisions/` channels. All **7 codex acceptance tests** pass (register-no-spawn/menubar-resident/idempotent-spec, SIRSI_SUPERVISOR=0, F1, F2, F3) across Go + python; `go test -race` green. Remaining deploy step: migrate the hook to user-scope `~/.claude/` (Decision 4 default-on). Refs: docs/ADR-024-ONE-WATCHER-PER-SURFACE.md, PANTHEON_RULES.md A27/A26/A24/A11/A19; Changelog: v0.23.
- **Dashboard action contract frozen — E1/E2/E3/E5** (codex freeze-gate ruling, router item `162436`; single-writer lane = claude-pantheon; commits `8675796`/`f5b3084`/`edb8a74`). Converges all four surfaces (CLI/menubar/TUI/SwiftUI) onto one typed `internal/dashboard` contract instead of each inventing action semantics. **E3:** typed `ActionRequest`/`ActionResult`/`PreparedAction` (`contract.go`) + typed `StatsResponse` mirroring the menubar `StatsSnapshot` JSON tags, so `/api/stats` is no longer an opaque `[]byte` at the boundary (decode-through with honest passthrough fallback). **E2:** `ConfirmGuard` (`confirm.go`) — server-enforced, single-use, tokenized two-phase confirmation (SHA-256 action hash; rejects missing/unknown/expired/mismatched/reused tokens; default = dry-run/prepare; the token is the safety boundary, no client `confirm()`). A shared `requireConfirm()` gates all 5 destructive endpoints; **fixes the `/api/clean` defect where an omitted `dry_run` deleted for real** (Rule A1, PARAMOUNT). **E1:** canonical `ActionSpec` registry (`actions.go`) folding the legacy 8 actions with the 12 gap-list actions (audit, maat, risk, network/fix, thoth/sync, seshat/ingest, net/align, ra/deploy/kill/collect), reachable via `GET /api/actions` + typed `POST /api/run`. **E5:** `POST /api/run` accepts a typed `ActionRequest` (server-defined base args + opt-in caller positional args — no arbitrary injection), runner+SSE retained for streaming; legacy `?cmd=` kept but cannot fire destructive. E4/E6 + renice-exemption deferred as documented fast-follow. Surface work (menubar Step 2, TUI wiring) is unblocked pending codex's implementation review. `go test -race ./internal/dashboard`: green. Refs: docs/DASHBOARD_CONTRACT_MATRIX.md, ADR-020, PANTHEON_RULES.md A1/A4/A16/A27; Changelog: v0.23.
- **One build-version contract + local binary-drift detection** (ADR-023; claude-home owns, codex-pantheon verifies — router item `20260601-143914...plan-version-contract`). Fixes the **CTR deploy-drift class** behind ADR-022: the fix `ca6e343` reached `~/.local/bin/sirsi` but `/opt/homebrew/bin/{sirsi,sirsi-menubar}` stayed stale, so the menubar ran the OLD `internal/router` reaper silently. Root cause was distribution, not logic — five disagreeing `var version` literals (`v0.21.0`/`v0.20.0`/`v0.4.0-standalone`…), no single source, no drift visibility. New `internal/version/build.go` holds one stamped contract (`Version`/`Commit`/`Date` + `Info`/`Current`), with a `debug.ReadBuildInfo()` fallback so plain `go build` self-reports honestly (A23). All 7 `cmd/*/main.go` literals replaced; `.goreleaser.yaml` + `Makefile` ldflags unified onto `internal/version.*`. `sirsi version --json` and a new `sirsi-menubar version [--json]` emit the same `Info` shape. New `internal/selfupdate` (`DetectMethod`/`ScanHost`/`BuildReport`) discovers sibling binaries and probes each via `version --json` (200 ms, **no network**), classifying **D2 sibling drift** + **D3 PATH drift**; no `internal/router` import (no cycle). `sirsi doctor` appends a `binary-drift` finding that the SessionStart `health-line.sh` surfaces automatically. Proven on the real binary: stamped `sirsi`+`sirsi-menubar` both report `v0.22.0-beta`+commit; a staged stale sibling triggered D2 (severity 2) and rendered `health:🔴 … binary-drift`. Verified atomic `sirsi self-update` + cosign signing deferred (follow-up router items). `go test -race ./internal/version ./internal/selfupdate`: green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A13/A23/A7; Changelog: v0.23.

### Fixed
- **CTR false-active resurrection + zombie-blind reaper** (claude-home owns; codex-pantheon verifies — router item `20260601-024355...execute-fix-ctr-false-active`). Three coupled defects let dead threads show `active` and the registry balloon to 1050 records: **(B1)** a late heartbeat could revive a terminal record; **(B2)** the reaper used `kill -0`, which a defunct (zombie `Z`) process answers, so zombies were never reaped; **(volume)** non-idempotent registration minted a new record per tick. Fixes (`internal/router/`): terminal `reaped`/`stale-heartbeat` statuses + `IsTerminal()`; `Heartbeat` refuses to revive terminal records; OS-truth liveness primitive (`liveness*.go`, build-tagged, injectable per A16/A21) distinguishing alive/gone/**defunct** via `ps -o stat=`; `ReapDeadThreads` (host-scoped); idempotent `RegisterThread` (one live session → one thread); `thread list` integrity warning + `💀`; `CollectNodeStatus.os_state` so Horus can't show a dead PID live; new `sirsi thread prune` to clear tombstones (the write-churn feeding Spotlight `mds_stores`). Proven on the real binary (register→kill→reap); live registry swept 1050→0, other lanes' 19 pending preserved. `go test -race`: green. Refs: PANTHEON_RULES.md A27/A26/A21/A16; Changelog: v0.23.
- **A27 watcher re-arm idempotency keyed on thread_id, not the shared loop body** (ADR-024). The prescribed re-arm check `pgrep -f "<thread-specific signature>"` was being implemented against the shared `DIR=.agents/idea-router/items` string — which every Claude surface runs verbatim — so on a shared host the check matches *other agents'* live loops and falsely reports "already armed," leaving a thread registered-but-unwatched. Observed 2026-06-01: a fresh `claude-deck` session's check matched `claude-home`'s running loop (`thr-7a3f16…`). ADR-024 `arm_instruction` and §3 now require the signature to include the **thread_id** (`pgrep -f thr-<thread_id>`), the same `(agent_id, pid)` identity ADR-022 reaps on. Refs: docs/ADR-024-ONE-WATCHER-PER-SURFACE.md; PANTHEON_RULES.md A27; Changelog: v0.23.

### Removed
- **v0.22 BubbleTea TUI implementation removed** (ADR-018, 2026-05-21; status now *Partially In Force — Amended By ADR-020*). All `internal/output/tui*.go` files (~4,800 LOC, 20 files), the TUI gateway entry point, `sirsi status --live`, and the no-args TUI launcher are gone. Binary dropped 24.2 MB → 22.2 MB. The `charm.land/bubbletea/v2` dependency is removed from `go.mod`. **Scope clarification (2026-05-29):** this removed the *unreleasable v0.22 implementation*, not the TUI surface category. ADR-020 reopened the surface decision and closed Hybrid C; a new Mole-grade TUI is the next interactive deliverable, designed from scratch (no restoration of the deleted code as foundation). The "intentional and immediate" framing applies to the v0.22 deletion only.
- **`sirsi` with no args** no longer launches an interactive surface; it now prints help. This holds for v0.23 until the new TUI lands. Per-verb behavior and flags are unchanged — see `docs/CLI_COMPATIBILITY.md` for the full matrix.

### Reopened
- **Interactive surface decision reopened and re-closed as Hybrid C** (ADR-020, 2026-05-29). After user direction *"TUIs are the wave… if we can't build one, it calls into question our ability to build Sirsi overall,"* the surface category was put under multi-track evaluation. Closure: new Mole-grade TUI ships first on macOS/Windows/Linux; Mac native SwiftUI follows in a later phase. No `internal/tui/` Go code lands before a `docs/TUI_DESIGN_PROOF.md` clears codex review (per ADR-020 §"Why This TUI Will Be Different" Gate).

### Proposed
- **ADR-021 — Deities Must Not Assume Single-Repo** (proposed 2026-05-31, routed to codex-pantheon). The menubar's Osiris reported `osiris assess failed` because `cmd/sirsi-menubar/stats.go:84` defaults `RepoDir: "."`, and a LaunchAgent-spawned menubar runs with cwd=`/` (not a git repo). The fix is not "pin a repo" — it names a design principle: deities whose domain is workstation-scoped (Osiris risk, Anubis hygiene, Ma'at quality, Isis pressure) must source scope from **CTR workstation discovery** (`sirsi thread` registry + `sirsi thread discover`), never the process cwd. Osiris becomes a workstation-wide risk aggregator; non-git/zero-repo states degrade to benign, never `failed`. No code lands before the ADR is accepted. See `docs/ADR-021-DEITIES-NOT-SINGLE-REPO.md`.

### Added
- **Menubar registers as a resident CTR router surface — surface-ladder Step 1** (claude-pantheon, commit `543e959`; approved by codex-pantheon router item `20260601-055029` with constraints). The user's interactive-surface ladder is `CLI > menubar > TUI > SwiftUI` + IDE plugins, and "registration" means **every surface is a router-registered thread** (A26/A27), not just a renderer. The existing Go `fyne.io/systray` menubar (`cmd/sirsi-menubar/`) — already running the in-process `internal/dashboard` server, guard bridge, and a 4h jackal scan — now registers one thread (`agent=sirsi-menubar`, `surface=menubar`) bound to its **own PID** (`os.Getpid()`, not a spawned Terminal child), heartbeating on a **bounded 60s ticker deliberately decoupled from the stats tick** to avoid the registry write-amplification that fed `mds_stores`. New `cmd/sirsi-menubar/register.go`: `resolveRouterRoot` (env → walk-up → conventional checkout; best-effort, skips registration rather than blocking launch), idempotent register (reuses live `(agent_id,pid)`; cross-restart dead records retired by ADR-022 OS-truth reaping, which `thread list` applies on read), bounded `heartbeatLoop`, `closeMenubarThread`. An explicit SIGTERM/SIGINT handler in `onReady` closes the thread on graceful shutdown (logout/launchd stop); `kickstart -k` SIGKILLs so that path relies on reaping. Verified live: `sirsi thread list` shows exactly **one** active menubar record across repeated restarts; real-SIGTERM close confirmed (record → `closed`). Surfaced a real deploy-drift bug in passing (two `sirsi-menubar` binaries — `/opt/homebrew/bin` 18.4MB vs `~/.local/bin` 12.1MB; redeployed both), feeding claude-home's `sirsi doctor`/`self-update` plan (`143914`). Menu-action → dashboard-contract refactor is **Step 2** (not in this change). Canon amendments (A27 resident-surface wording, ADR-020 ladder) pending codex's call on ADR form.
- **`internal/tui/` scaffold — Phase-2 batch-2 Gate 2** (claude-pantheon, routed to codex-pantheon for review). Gate 1 cleared (codex approved 2026-05-31 `reviews/20260531-codex-pantheon-tui-design-proof-gate1-review.md` + user sign-off), so the design proof becomes code — **scaffold only**, per Codex's review scope: primitives, state model, command registry, renderer contract, fixture screens, tests. **No functional resurrection of v0.22 and no operator-facing launch path** — `cmd/sirsi` does not import the package and `sirsi` no-args still prints help (`docs/CLI_COMPATIBILITY.md` unchanged). Contents: the 5 layout primitives (`primitives.go`), the binary split-tree + 3 named layouts (`layout.go`), the `Command` registry with **data-driven status hints** (`command.go` — a hint is a projection of registered commands, so a dead key cannot render: §7 delta 2), the state/`Reduce` contract with the Rule A1 destructive-confirm guarantee (`state.go` — `clean`/`ra.kill` arm a confirm modal, never fire on one keystroke), the renderer contract with a **first-class linear/no-altscreen renderer** (`renderer.go`), the closed **glyph-width policy** covering *all* non-ASCII layout glyphs — box-drawing, `◉`, `▸`, `→`, `✓`, `⏱`, `…` — each with measured single-width + ASCII fallback (`glyph.go`, addressing Codex's Gate-1 precondition that safe-glyph discipline extend beyond hieroglyphs), the semantic color ladder with `NO_COLOR`→attribute-only (`color.go`), and the 3 proof screens as fixtures (`fixtures.go`). Tests satisfy every Codex scaffold precondition: no hint references an unregistered/unwired command; fixture renders at **80×24, 100-col, 120×40** stay within width and use only grid-safe glyphs; width/fallback asserted for every non-ASCII glyph; `NO_COLOR`/reduced-motion/no-altscreen fixtures. `go test -race`: green, **93.6%** coverage; `golangci-lint`: 0 issues. Supersedes the prior "(draft) awaiting review" status of `docs/TUI_DESIGN_PROOF.md`.
- **`sirsi thread discover` — live-session reconcile for CTR** (commit `10a97b7`, codex-pantheon **approved** 2026-05-31). After a reboot the thread registry goes cold: every prior PID is reaped and nothing re-registers, because registration was manual-only (`sirsi thread register`). `discover` queries running agent processes on this host (bounded `pgrep -x`/`lsof` — no broad scans, no Python), resolves each one's cwd to an agent in `agents.json`, and registers live, *mappable* sessions anchored to their PID so the existing watcher/reaper lifecycle owns them. **Honesty by construction (Rule A23):** home-launched sessions (no repo binding) are reported `unmappable`, never registered; a cwd matching two agents of one surface (the real `codex-homebrew` vs `codex-homebrew-tools` collision) is reported `ambiguous`, never guessed. Pure decision logic in `internal/router/discover.go` (`ReconcileDiscovery`, surface-scoped longest-ancestor match) with 9 unit tests per Rule A16 (no real processes); enumeration + apply + CLI in `cmd/sirsi/threaddiscover.go`; `--self` for SessionStart-hook use (Phase 2); stable snake_case `--json` for sweeps. **Phase 1.5:** wired into the hourly verification `sweep.sh` (alongside `thread scout`) so a cold registry self-heals for repo-launched sessions. This is the CTR-discovery primitive **ADR-021** names for workstation-scoped deities. Live at install: `discovered=6 registered=0 unmappable=5 skip=1` — confirming the premise (every session was home-launched) and the already-registered skip path.
- **`docs/TUI_DESIGN_PROOF.md` (draft)** — Phase-2 batch-2 **Gate 1** deliverable per ADR-020 §"Why This TUI Will Be Different." The first artifact of the Hybrid C TUI track is *not code* — it is a design proof that must clear codex-pantheon + user review before any `internal/tui/` package is created. Specifies the 5-primitive layout system, density/typography rules, the load-bearing **glyph budget** (Egyptian hieroglyphs forbidden in layout-bearing cells; deity identity via BMP-safe sigils + color + a startup font-capability probe), modeless keyboard model with command palette, error-state altitudes, accessibility (linear screen-reader mode, `NO_COLOR`, high-contrast, reduced-motion), three canonical ASCII mocks (scan / Ra fleet / router inbox), and the six structural "different from v0.22" deltas. Awaiting review.
- **Knowledge Substrate** — semantic verification layer via the Understand-Anything Claude Code plugin. First run on 2026-05-26 produced `.understand-anything/knowledge-graph.json` (3,340 nodes, 6,947 edges, 9 architectural layers, 14-step pedagogical tour). Codified as **ADR-019**.
  - User-facing: `docs/user-guides/knowledge-substrate.md`
  - Web page: `docs/pantheon/knowledge-substrate.html` (→ `sirsi.ai/pantheon/knowledge-substrate`)
  - Case study: `docs/case-studies/2026-05-26-knowledge-substrate-day-1.md`
  - Three-tool split codified: Thoth (memory) / Seba (architectural map) / Knowledge Substrate (semantic verification). No deity sovereignty changes — Seba's mapping authority unchanged.
  - Bidirectional sync: `.thoth/memory.yaml` gains a `## Knowledge Graph (Understand-Anything)` block + `sync_protocol`; rule in `~/CLAUDE.md` so every Thoth-enabled repo auto-updates after `/understand` runs.
  - Long-term direction: cross-repo, cross-agent hypergraph on **Hedera Consensus Service**. Workspace-canon builder vision at `~/Development/HYPERGRAPH_VISION.md`; pointer added to `~/Development/AGENTS.md` § Knowledge Substrate.
  - CLI surface spec'd in ADR-019 § 6 (`sirsi hypergraph status|refresh|chat|explain|diff|layers|tour|export`), gated by `configs/hypergraph.yaml` `enabled:` and a `hypergraph` build tag. Implementation pending codex-pantheon review.
- `docs/CLI_COMPATIBILITY.md` — concise per-verb compatibility matrix for the v0.22 → v0.23 transition.
- **FSEvents-driven wake** — `.agents/idea-router/wake.example.plist` is a deployable launchd template. Copy to `~/Library/LaunchAgents/`, fill in paths, `launchctl load`. launchd watches `state.json`, `items/`, `proposals/` for any change and fires ONE dispatch pass per change (`sirsi router run --once`). `ThrottleInterval=10` prevents refire loops. **Zero idle process** — no polling daemon, no cron, no heartbeat. Replaces the prior 1-second-interval polling daemon (86,400 reads/day → ~0 idle, dispatch latency dropped from ≤1s to milliseconds).
- `internal/work/work_test.go` — round-trip coverage for Send/Get/ListInbox/Close, YAML quoting edge cases, status transitions.
- `internal/work` YAML quoting: `Send` now writes frontmatter values as double-quoted strings (`from: "claude-pantheon"`), escaped properly so titles/agent-ids containing colons, `|`, `*`, `&`, newlines, etc. round-trip cleanly. `Get`/`ListInbox` parsing handles both quoted and bare forms.
- `routercmd.go` split: `workRoot()` (read-only, no mkdir side-effect) for `status/pull/show`, `workRootEnsure()` (mkdir items/) for `send/close`. Audits no longer materialize an items/ directory.

- **Pull-model work queue** — bare-minimum any-to-any routing between agent threads, no daemon required:
  - `sirsi router send --from <id> --to <id> --title <s> --instructions <text-or-@file>` — write one work item
  - `sirsi router pull <agent>` — list open items addressed to an agent
  - `sirsi router show <id>` — print full body + frontmatter
  - `sirsi router close <id> --result <text-or-@file>` — flip status to closed
  - `sirsi router status` — count open vs closed items, open by recipient
  - New `internal/work` package; items live as plain markdown under `.agents/idea-router/items/`
- `.claude/hooks/router_inbox_check.py` now also surfaces pull-model items (was legacy-only).
- `TestRouterPullModelRoundtrip` integration test.

### Removed
- **Legacy push-model router CLI verbs**: `watch`, `run`, `daemon`, `work` (--poll), `install-agent`, `uninstall-agent`, `service-status`, `node-status`, `smoke`, `submit-existing`, plus the legacy `inbox` verb. The pull model replaces all of them with one mental model (file in items/ + recipient pulls). The `internal/router` Go package is left intact (still imported by `agentcmd.go`, `threadcmd.go`, `setup.go`, `internal/mcp/tools.go` for thread/agent registry reads) — a follow-up can prune the now-dead dispatcher/runner/launchctl code from that package.
- Any running `sirsi router daemon` process (e.g., from the launch agent) keeps running on its loaded binary, but restarts will fail with "unknown command" — uninstall the launch agent with `launchctl unload ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.sirsi.idea-router.*.plist` if you previously installed one.

### Fixed
- Path containment check in the (removed) `submit-existing` verb used `filepath.EvalSymlinks` so tempdir tests worked on macOS; same pattern carries forward to `workRoot()`.

## [0.22.0-beta] — 2026-05-18

### Hardening Sprint Complete (Codex-reviewed, 30+ commits)

#### Safety
